### 2019ApJ...881..108J: New Constraints on the Dust and Gas Distribution in the LkCa 15 Disk from ALMA (2019; protoplanetary_disks; archival_reuse)
- question: What gas and dust surface density profiles and disk mass of the LkCa 15 disk best reproduce the ALMA 12CO 3-2 radial intensity profile, channel maps and 0.87 mm continuum?
- step 2: Fit the dust continuum profile
  - method: Fit a fixed dust surface density profile to the observed continuum radial profile [3.1 surface density profile and paramet…: "These values are determined by fitting the azimuthal averaged radial intensity profile of the observed dust continuum map"]
  - software: RADMC-3D
  - parameter: RC = 66 AU, gamma = -0.15, RC_arctan = 66 AU, gamma_arctan = 5.35, Mdust = 9.8e-5 Msun [3.1 surface density profile and paramet…: "we adopt a fixed dust density profile of $RC = 66$ AU, $\gamma = -0.15$, $RC_{\rm arctan} = 66$ AU, $\gamma_{\rm arctan} = 5.35$"]
  - assumption: mm-dust scale height 0.1 x 1 AU x (r/20 AU)^1.25; opacity at 1 mm 13.2 cm2/g [3.2 physical model: "The resulting dust opacity at the wavelength of 1 mm is 13.2 cm$^2$ g$^{-1}$."]
  - validation: reduced chi2 of dust profile [3.2 physical model: "Then we calculate the reduced $\chi^{2}$ for dust continuum emission between the radial intensity profiles of the model and the ALMA observation."]
- step 3: Compute self-consistent disk temperature for each gas model
  - method: Iterate Monte Carlo radiative transfer and hydrostatic equilibrium until temperatures agree within 3% [3.2 physical model: "the difference between two temperature structures is within 3% everywhere"]
  - software: RADMC-3D
  - parameter: Teff = 4350 K blackbody star; dust-to-gas ratio 0.001 for micron dust [3.2 physical model: "We assume the host star of LkCa 15 is a black-body radiator with an effective temperature of 4350 K."]
  - assumption: MRN size distribution n(a)~a^-3.5, 5e-6 to 1e-2 mm; opacity at 1 um 5.2e3 cm2/g [3.2 physical model: "between a minimum grain size of $5 \times 10^{-6}$ mm and a maximum grain size of $1 \times 10^{-2}$ mm"]
- step 4: Grid search of gas surface density for 12CO
  - method: Compute 12CO channel and moment-0 maps for 4096 models (8^4 grid of Mdisk, gamma, RC_arctan, gamma_arctan), subtract continuum, convolve with Gaussian beam, compare azimuthal radial profile with reduced chi2 [3.1 surface density profile and paramet…: "Then, we search the parameter space to obtain the best-fit model that has the least reduced $\chi^2$ of the radial intensity profile of $^{12}$CO emission."]
  - software: RADMC-3D
  - parameter: RC fixed at 12500 AU; no exponential cutoff in gas [3.1 surface density profile and paramet…: "we fix the $RC$ at 12500 AU to slow down the decrease of the gas density at the outer part of the disk"]
  - parameter: Photodissociation threshold 2.0e20 H nuclei; CO freeze-out below 20 K [3.2 physical model: "We include the freeze-out effect of CO by setting the number density of $^{12}$CO to zero at the region where temperature is below 20 K."]
  - assumption: 12CO/H2 = 1.4e-4 by mass [3.2 physical model: "we assume the abundance ratio of $^{12}$CO/H$_2$ to be 1.4 $\times 10^{-4}$ in mass"]
  - fitting: Brute-force grid search, minimum reduced chi2; no sampler [3.1 surface density profile and paramet…: "We set up a four-dimensional parameter space of these four free parameters."]
  - validation: Heat maps of chi2; comparison of channel maps of best-fit, high and low mass models with observation [5.2 Constraint on the disk mass: "In Figure 9 we compare the three models’ channel maps with the ALMA observation"]
- step 5: Estimate 13CO abundance
  - method: Manually adjust a power-law 12CO/13CO ratio, inner and outer 45 AU, using best-fit 12CO temperature structure [4.2 13CO image: "We separately fit the $\alpha$ for the inner and outer disk regions by manually adjusting $n_0$ and $\alpha$."]
  - software: RADMC-3D
  - validation: reduced chi2 of 13CO = 0.33 [4.2 13CO image: "The reduced $\chi^2$ of $^{13}$CO is derived to be 0.33."]
- result: Best-fit gas disk mass = 0.1 ± not stated Msun [4.1 12CO intensity profile: "$M_{\rm disk} = 0.1 M_{\odot}$, $\gamma = 4.0$, $RC_{\rm arctan} = 45$ AU, and $\gamma_{\rm arctan} = 10$"]
- result: Best-fit gamma = 4.0 ± not stated dimensionless [4.1 12CO intensity profile: "$\gamma = 4.0$"]
- result: Best-fit gas cavity radius = 45 ± not stated AU [4.1 12CO intensity profile: "$RC_{\rm arctan} = 45$ AU"]
- result: Reduced chi2 of best-fit 12CO = 2.51 ± not stated dimensionless [4.1 12CO intensity profile: "The best-fit model results in a reduced $\chi^2$ of 2.51."]
- result: Reduced chi2 high-mass (0.33 Msun) and low-mass (0.033 Msun) models = 14.6 and 6.7 ± not stated dimensionless [5.1 dependence of parameters: "The high-mass model result in a reduced $\chi^2$ of 14.6, and the low-mass model result in a reduced $\chi^2$ of 6.7."]
- result: Dust cavity radius / FWHM = ~65 AU / ~40 AU ± not stated AU [3.1 Dust continuum and line maps: "The dust continuum map shows an inner hole of $\sim$ 65 AU in radius, and the full width at half maximum of this dust cavity is at $\sim$ 40 AU."]
- result: Dust disk outer edge = ~200 ± not stated AU [5.2 Constraint on the disk mass: "The mm-size dust disk has an outer edge at $\sim$ 200 AU"]
- result: Fitted dust mass = 9.8e-5 ± not stated Msun [3.1 surface density profile and paramet…: "a total dust mass of $M_{dust} = 9.8 \times 10^{-5} M_{\odot}$"]
- result: Midplane temperature at 100 AU and 500 AU = ~15 K and ~11 K ± not stated K [4.1 12CO intensity profile: "In the mid-plane, the temperature decreases from $\sim$ 15 K at 100 AU to $\sim$ 11 K at 500 AU."]
- result: Reduced chi2 of continuum for best-fit model = 255 ± not stated dimensionless [4.1 12CO intensity profile: "has a large reduced $\chi^2$ of 255"]
- limitation (authors): Simple power law underpredicts 12CO beyond ~400 AU; mass depends on the CO/H2 abundance [4.1 12CO intensity profile: "Note that the fitted disk mass depends on the abundance ratio of $^{12}$CO/H$_2$ in our model."]
- limitation (authors): 13CO fit is not self-consistent [4.2 13CO image: "it is not a self-consistent way as compared to the fitting of $^{12}$CO intensity"]
- not stated (card): Imaging details (tclean parameters, weighting, robust, cell/image size) for the continuum and line cubes; Calibration details and flux scale uncertainty; Continuum subtraction and line-free channels; Moment-map and radial-profile extraction details (inclination, PA, bins, noise/error estimation); Disk inclination, position angle and systemic velocity used in the models; Gas temperature/microturbu…

### 2024A&A...687A.299L: Chemistry across dust and gas gaps in protoplanetary disks. Modelling the co-spatial molecular rings in the HD 100546 disk (2024; protoplanetary_disks, astrochemistry; theory_or_simulation_compared_to_alma)
- question: Where and how bright are molecular line rings expected in gapped disks across a range of physical conditions, and can thermochemical DALI models explain the molecular rings co-spatial with the dust rings in HD 100546
- step 2: Set up the DALI gas and dust density structure
  - method: Parametrised flared disk with tapered power-law gas surface density, multiplicative drops for cavity and gap, and small and large dust populations [2.3.1 Gas density structure: "follow the standard DALI setup for a flared disk with an exponentially tapered power law in the radial direction and a Gaussian distribution of the gas in the vertical direction"]
  - software: DALI
  - parameter: flaring index psi = 0, scale height h_c = 0.1 at the characteristic radius [3.1 The fiducial model: "Therefore, the fiducial model has a low flaring index of $\psi = 0$ and a low scale height of $h_c = 0.1$ at the characteristic radius."]
  - parameter: gas density lowered by 1e-5 inside the 15 au gas cavity and by 1e-2 between 15 and 20 au [3.1 The fiducial model: "To match these data, the gas density inside the gas cavity is lowered by a factor of $10^{-5}$ and between the gas (15 au) and dust cavity (20 au) by a factor of $10^{-2}$."]
  - parameter: grain sizes 5 nm to 1 um (small) and to 1 mm (large), MRN index -3.5 [Appendix DALI model setup: "Both populations have a minimum grain size of 5 nm but the small grains have a maximum size of 1 $\mu$m, whereas the large grains go up to 1 mm."] [Appendix DALI model setup: "An MRN distribution with an index of -3.5 is used to set the grain sizes $a$ within these populations (cite: Mathis1977)."]
  - assumption: global gas-to-dust mass ratio 100 [Table of model parameters: "$\Delta_{\mathrm{gas/ dust}}$ & 100 & Gas-to-dust mass ratio"]
  - assumption: disk inclination 41 deg [Table of model parameters: "$i$ & 41$\degree$ & Disk inclination ($0\degree$ is face-on)"]
  - assumption: distance 108.1 pc [Table of model parameters: "$d$ & 108.1 pc & Distance to the star"]
  - validation: Compared model continuum and CO isotopologue emission with ALMA profiles; gas gap depth explored with CO isotopologues [3.1 The fiducial model: "we find that CO isotopologue emission is consistent with a shallow gas gap where the gas is depleted by at most one order of magnitude"]
- step 3: Run the chemical networks
  - method: Three networks (nitrogen/hydrocarbon, CO isotopologue, HCO+) evolved on the DALI density and temperature structure with molecular initial conditions [2.3.4 Chemistry: "Both chemical networks are started with molecular initial conditions and a fiducial total (gas $+$ ice) C/O ratio of 0.4"] [2.3.4 Chemistry: "All chemical networks are evaluated at 5 Myr, the approximate age of the HD 100546 star (cite: Arun2019; Pineda2019)."]
  - software: DALI
  - parameter: non-thermal desorption rates updated to 5e-4 and 1.4e-3 molecules per grain per UV photon [2.3.4 Chemistry: "The non-thermal desorption rates of and CO are updated from $1.3\times 10^{-3}$ and $2.7\times 10^{-3}$ molecules desorbed per grain per incident UV photon to $5\times 10^{-4}$ and $1.4\times 10^{-3}$ molecules desorbed per grain per incident UV photon respectively"]
  - parameter: cosmic ray ionisation rate 1e-18 s^-1 [Appendix DALI model setup: "a cosmic ray ionisation rate consistent with that found in other protoplanetary disks of $10^{-18}$ s$^{-1}$ is adopted (cite: Aikawa2021)."]
  - assumption: chemistry evolved to 5 Myr; fiducial total C/O of 0.4 [2.3.4 Chemistry: "All chemical networks are evaluated at 5 Myr, the approximate age of the HD 100546 star (cite: Arun2019; Pineda2019)."]
  - validation: The network reproduces CN, HCN and related emission in other disks within a factor of a few, per the paper [3.3 Integrated intensity maps: "The chemical network used to calculate these abundances reproduces the CN, HCN, HNC, and emission in other protoplanetary disks within a factor of a few in general"]
- step 4: Raytrace synthetic observations and compare with ALMA
  - method: DALI raytracer, non-LTE level populations, continuum subtraction and convolution to the observed beam of each species [2.3.5 Raytracing: "The synthetic ALMA observations are produced using the raytracer in DALI."] [2.3.5 Raytracing: "Finally, all synthetic image cubes are continuum subtracted and convolved to a beam matching that of the observations of that particular species"]
  - software: DALI
  - parameter: blended components raytraced separately and summed [2.3.5 Raytracing: "Therefore, these two and three transitions were raytraced and added assuming the emission is optically thin."]
  - assumption: emission of blended components optically thin; level populations without LTE [2.3.5 Raytracing: "The level populations are calculated without assuming local thermodynamical equilibrium (LTE)"]
  - validation: A model is considered successful if it reproduces the data within a factor of about 2-3 [3 Results: "Therefore, a model is expected to reproduce the data within a factor of $\sim 2-3$ in general (cite: Kama2016; Sturm2023)."]
- step 5: Explore gas gap depth, C/O, background UV, flaring and X-ray luminosity
  - method: Grid of DALI models varying one parameter at a time around the fiducial model; compare column densities, radial profiles and line ratios [4.1 Radial structures: "The thermochemical models spanning a range of gas gap depth ($\delta_{\rm gas\ gap} = 1-10^{-5}$)"] [3.5 Different C/O ratios: "In this section, the effect of an increased C/O ratio is explored in the fiducial model with a gas gap of $\delta_{\rm gas\ gap} = 10^{-1}$."]
  - software: DALI, scipy
  - parameter: background UV 1 G0 in the fiducial model, lowered by a factor of ten in variations [3.6 Background UV: "The models assume that the external UV field is 1 $G_0$, the mean UV field in the interstellar medium."] [3.6 Background UV: "Lowering the background UV field by a factor of ten decreases the CN and column densities by that same factor at $300-400$ au."]
  - parameter: X-ray luminosity increased by factors of 4 and 6 [Appendix X-ray luminosity: "the mean and maximum X-ray luminosities reported in (cite: Skinner2020) that are a factor of 4 and 6 higher than that in the fiducial model."]
  - parameter: column densities smoothed with a savgol filter over 10 points, 3rd order polynomial [3.4.1 Column densities: "the column densities inside 250 au are smoothed in log space with a savgol filter in scipy over 10 points and a 3rd order polynomial"]
  - assumption: small dust follows the drop in gas density in the gap [3.4 The effect of a gas gap: "The small dust is assumed to follow the drop in the gas density."]
- step 6: Test a combined model with radially varying C/O and lowered background UV
  - method: Two models with background UV reduced by 10 and C/O raised to 1.01 across the dust rings or only in two 10 au wide rings [4.2.4 C/O ratio / summary: "two models with a background UV field attenuated by a factor of 10 and where the C/O ratio is increased to 1.01 in two rings."] [4.2.4 C/O ratio / summary: "In this model, the C/O ratio is only increased in two 10 au wide rings from 35 to 45 au and from 215 to 225 au to match the location of the observed emission ring."]
  - software: DALI
  - parameter: C/O = 1.01 in rings at 35-45 au and 215-225 au; fiducial C/O 0.4 elsewhere [5 Conclusions: "and the fiducial C/O ratio of 0.4 in the other disk regions reproduces the double ringed morphology seen in the HCN, CN, and emission and it roughly reproduces the morphology of the NO."]
  - validation: Model intensities compared with observations within about a factor of 5 [4.2.4 C/O ratio / summary: "this model does reproduce the intensity of these molecules within a factor of $\sim 5$"]
- result: Fiducial gas gap depth between 40 and 175 au = factor of ~10 drop in gas density (delta_gas_gap = 1e-1) ± no more than a factor of ~10 dimensionless [abstract: "In particular, the CO isotopologue emission is consistent with a shallow gas gap with no more than a factor of $\sim 10$ drop in gas density at $40-175$ au."]
- result: Gas cavity, dust cavity and gap radii of the fiducial model = gas cavity 0-15 au, dust cavity 0-20 au, gap 40-175 au ± not stated au [abstract: "The fiducial model of a gapped disk with a gas cavity at $0-15$ au, a dust cavity at $0-20$ au, and a gas and dust gap at $40-175$ au"]
- result: Best-fit C/O ratio for [C I], CN and C2H = ~0.8-1.2 ± not stated dimensionless [5 Conclusions: "and is an elevated C/O of $\sim 0.8-1.2$."]
- result: C/O in narrow rings of the radially varying C/O model = 1.01 in rings 35-45 au and 215-225 au ± not stated dimensionless [4.2.4 C/O ratio / summary: "In this model, the C/O ratio is only increased in two 10 au wide rings from 35 to 45 au and from 215 to 225 au to match the location of the observed emission ring."]
- result: Fiducial model underprediction of atomic carbon inside ~108 au = an order of magnitude ± not stated dimensionless [3.5.1 Column densities and emission pro…: "Inside $\sim108$ au, the fiducial model underpredicts the emission of atomic carbon by an order of magnitude."]
- result: Decrease of CN and C2H column densities for 10x lower background UV at 300-400 au = factor of 10 ± not stated dimensionless [3.6 Background UV: "Lowering the background UV field by a factor of ten decreases the CN and column densities by that same factor at $300-400$ au."]
- result: Decrease of NO column density for 10x lower background UV at 200-350 au = factor of 5 ± not stated dimensionless [3.6 Background UV: "The NO decreases by a factor of 5 from $200-350$ au."]
- result: Height of peak CN number density at inner rim of outer dust ring, flattest models (psi = -0.1 and 0) = z = 55-53 au at r = 175 au ± not stated au [3.7 Flaring: "For the two flattest models ($\psi = -0.1$ and 0) the CN at $r=175$ au peaks at $z=55-53$ au"]
- result: Height of peak CN number density, more flared models (psi = 0.1 and 0.2) = 66-68 au at r = 176 au ± not stated au [3.7 Flaring: "for the two more flared models ($\psi = 0.1$ and 0.2), the CN peaks at a height of $66-68$ au at $r=176$ au."]
- result: Location of the water snowline in the HD 100546 disk (inferred) = inside 15 au (unresolved at 31 au resolution) ± not stated au [4.2.1 tracing a low gas-phase water abu…: "Therefore, the water snowline in the HD 100546 disk is likely unresolved at the 31 au resolution of the observations and thus located inside 15 au."]
- limitation (authors): Thermochemical fits are not unique and degeneracies exist [3 Results: "In addition, a thermochemical model reproducing the observed line intensities within this factor is not unique and degeneracies in the model exist due to e.g., differences in the physical and thus thermal and chemical structure of the model."]
- limitation (authors): Small maximum recoverable scale of the [C I] data filters emission at large radii [3.5.1 Column densities and emission pro…: "This could partially be due to the small maximum recoverable scale of 2" ($\sim 108$ au in radius), filtering the emission at larger distances."]
- limitation (authors): Water photodesorption is not included in the small HCO+ network, so HCO+ is overpredicted in the gap [4.2.1 tracing a low gas-phase water abu…: "Photodesorption of water ice is not included in the network used to predict the abundance in this work."]
- not stated (card): CASA version used for the [C I] reduction; Which channels were flagged as line-containing for uvcontsub, and the order of the continuum fit; Channel width / spectral resolution and cell and image size of the [C I] cube; Final rms of the [C I] image; Primary-beam correction and flux calibration scale uncertainty for the Band 10 data; Whether DALI source code and the three chemical networks are ava…

### 2021A&A...647A.118L: Exploring HNC and HCN line emission as probes of the protoplanetary disk temperature (2021; protoplanetary_disks, astrochemistry; theory_or_simulation_compared_to_alma)
- question: Can HNC and HCN emission, especially their line ratio, probe disk temperature and other physical and chemical conditions?
- step 4: Construct physical disk models
  - method: Specify viscous gas surface density, Gaussian vertical density, two dust populations and stellar radiation; vary mass, vertical height and flaring [Physical framework: "We adopt the gas surface density profile as for a viscous evolving accretion disk"] [Physical framework: "The gas density in the vertical direction follows a Gaussian distribution, with a scale-height angle of $h=h_{c}(R/R_{c})^{\psi}$."]
  - software: DALI
  - parameter: γ=1; ψ=0.1, 0.2, 0.3; h_c=0.1, 0.2 rad; R_c=60 au [Table 1: "| $\gamma$ | 1 | | $\psi$ | 0.1, 0.2, 0.3 | | $h_{\rm c}$ | 0.1, 0.2 rad | | $R_{\rm c}$ | 60 au |"]
  - parameter: M_gas=10^-5, 10^-4, 10^-3, 10^-2, 10^-1 M_sun [Table 1: "| $M_{\rm gas}$ | $10^{-5} , 10^{-4} , 10^{-3}, 10^{-2}, 10^{-1} M_\odot$ |"]
  - parameter: T Tauri: T_eff=4000 K, L_*=1 L_sun; Herbig Ae: T_eff=10000 K, L_*=10 L_sun [Physical framework: "a T Tauri star with $T_{\rm eff}$=4000 K and $L_\ast$=1 $L_\odot$, and a Herbig Ae star with $T_{\rm eff}$=10000 K and $L_\ast$=10 $L_\odot$"]
  - parameter: Accretion UV: 10000 K blackbody onto a 1 M_sun, 1.5 R_sun star; default accretion rate 10^-8 M_sun yr^-1 [Physical framework: "modeled as a blackbody emitting at 10000 K on to a 1$M_{\odot}$ and 1.5$R_{\odot}$ star"] [Physical framework: "We took the typical accretion rate for young T Tauri stars of $10^{-8}\,\rm M_{\odot}\,yr^{-1}$ (cite: Hartmann2016; Manara2016) as the default in our model"]
  - parameter: Fiducial: 10^-2 M_sun, ψ=0.2, h_c=0.1, T Tauri star [Results: "the fiducial model, which is a $10^{-2}\,\rm M_{\ensuremath{\odot}}$ disk with $\psi$=0.2, $h_c$=0.1 orbiting a T Tauri star."]
  - assumption: Gas-to-dust ratio 100 [Physical framework: "The dust surface density is scaled from the gas surface density assuming a typical gas-to-dust ratio of 100."]
  - assumption: Small grains 0.005–1 μm at gas scale height; large grains 1–1000 μm at χh, χ=0.2; f_large=0.99 [Physical framework: "a small-grain population (0.005-1 $\mu$m) with the same scale height $h$ as the gas"] [Physical framework: "a large-grain population (1-1000 $\mu$m) with a reduced scale height of ${\chi}h$, where ${\chi}=0.2$."]
  - assumption: External UV G_0≈2.7×10^-3 erg s^-1 cm^-2 between 6 and 13.6 eV; cosmic-ray ionization 5×10^-17 s^-1 per H2 [Physical framework: "of $\rm \sim2.7\times10^{-3}\,erg\,s^{-1}\,cm^{-2}$ between 6 and 13.6 eV range, and a cosmic-ray ionization rate of $\rm 5\times10^{-17}\,s^{-1}$ per $\rm H_2$."]
  - assumption: X-rays: thermal radiation at 7×10^7 K, 1–100 keV, luminosity 10^30 erg s^-1; same prescription for T Tauri and Herbig models [Physical framework: "The X-ray spectrum was taken as thermal radiation at $7\times10^7$ K between 1 and 100 keV, with the X-ray luminosity of 10$^{30}$ erg s$^{-1}$."] [Physical framework: "The same prescription was used for the T Tauri and Herbig models."]
  - validation: Varying f_large between 0.9 and 0.99 produced no significant cyanide line-emission differences [Physical framework: "We have tested models in which we varied the $f_{\rm large}$ parameter between 0.9 and 0.99, but found no significant differences in line emission of cyanides (cite: Cazzoletti2018)."]
- step 5: Solve chemistry and thermal structure
  - method: Monte Carlo dust radiative transfer; chemical abundances and non-LTE excitation; iterate heating–cooling balance to self-consistent gas temperature [Physical and chemical models: "DALI first solves the dust continuum radiative transfer based on a Monte Carlo method to obtain the local UV flux and dust temperature"] [Physical and chemical models: "the abundances for chemical species are calculated with a chemical network simulation and are then fed into the non-LTE excitation calculation"]
  - software: DALI, KIDA
  - parameter: Initial [C]/[H]=1×10^-4; [O]/[H]=3.5×10^-4; [N]/[H]=1.6×10^-5 [Table 1: "| $\rm [C]/[H]$ | 1$\times 10^{-4}$ | | $\rm [O]/[H]$ | 3.5$\times 10^{-4}$ | | $\rm [N]/[H]$ | 1.6$\times 10^{-5}$ |"]
  - parameter: HNC+H barrier 200 K; HNC+O barrier 20 K [Chemical network: "we used an updated reaction rate coefficient of HNC+H based on (cite: Graninger2014) with an energy barrier of 200 K"] [Chemical network: "we included the reaction of HNC+O with an energy barrier of 20 K"]
  - parameter: Grain binding energies: CN 1600 K; HNC and HCN 2050 K [Chemical network: "We used binding energies to grains of 1600 K for CN and 2050 K for HNC and HCN"]
  - assumption: Steady-state chemistry with initial ISM CNO abundances and ISM C/O [Chemical network: "The chemistry was run in steady-state mode with initial ISM abundances for CNO"] [Chemical network: "thus with the ISM C/O."]
  - assumption: Chemical model inherited from Cazzoletti2018 except the two HNC-destruction changes [Chemical network: "The entire chemical model is the same as was used for the investigation of CN emission in disks (cite: Cazzoletti2018), except for two changes made relating to the destruction of HNC"]
  - validation: Iterate to self-consistency; updated network has negligible effects on CN emission [Physical and chemical models: "the final $T_{\rm gas}$ is obtained by iterating the above steps until a self-consistent solution is reached."] [Chemical network: "The updated network has negligible effects on the CN emission."]
- step 6: Generate synthetic line observables
  - method: Ray-trace spectral line emission; convolve integrated maps; extract intensity profiles, HNC-to-HCN ratios and integrated fluxes [Emission morphology: "For a given temperature structure and abundance profile, spectral line emission is obtained through ray-tracing."] [Emission morphology: "the velocity-integrated intensity maps for HNC and HCN at the two transitions from our fiducial model, convolved with a $0.2\ensuremath{''}$ Gaussian beam."]
  - software: DALI
  - parameter: HNC and HCN J=1-0 and J=3-2; 0.2'' Gaussian beam [Emission morphology: "In this work, we focus on rotational transitions of $J=1-0$ and $J=3-2$ for HNC and HCN"] [Emission morphology: "convolved with a $0.2\ensuremath{''}$ Gaussian beam."]
  - assumption: Face-on disk, inclination 0°, distance 150 pc [Emission morphology: "The disk is set to be face-on (inclination of 0$\ensuremath{^\circ}$) and placed at a distance of 150 pc."]
  - validation: Compare intensity rings with column-density peaks and emitting-region density and excitation [Emission morphology: "The predicted ring-like emission patterns, seen clearly in radial intensity profiles, are consistent with the column density profiles in which peaks of column densities are located off-center"] [Excitation and column density: "We find that the excitation temperature for the $1-0$ line in our model is $\sim50\%$ of the gas temperature."]
- step 7: Test sensitivity to disk conditions
  - method: Compare model line fluxes, profiles, column densities and ratios across physical, radiation and elemental-abundance variations [Dependence of line fluxes on disk param…: "explore the dependences of line fluxes on key disk properties."] [Dependence on varying the C and O eleme…: "we performed three additional carbon- and oxygen-depleted models"]
  - software: DALI
  - parameter: Additional accretion rates: 10^-7, 10^-9, 10^-10 M_sun yr^-1 [Dependence of line fluxes on disk param…: "we ran a set of additional models with different accretion rates ($10^{-7}, 10^{-9},$ and $10^{-10}\,\rm M_{\odot}\,yr^{-1}$"]
  - parameter: C and O depleted by factors 10 and 100; third model adds oxygen depletion to C/O=1.5 [Dependence on varying the C and O eleme…: "The initial overall abundances of carbon and oxygen were depleted by a factor of 10"] [Dependence on varying the C and O eleme…: "abundances of carbon and oxygen were depleted by a factor of 100"]
  - parameter: Additional characteristic radii R_c=15 and 30 au [Emission dependence on varying disk par…: "we ran additional models with $R_{c}=$15 and 30 au, keeping the inner disk surface density profile identical"]
  - assumption: Nitrogen abundance unchanged in depletion models [Dependence on varying the C and O eleme…: "The nitrogen abundance was kept the same as in the original model."]
  - assumption: Inner disk surface density profile unchanged in smaller-R_c models [Emission dependence on varying disk par…: "keeping the inner disk surface density profile identical"]
  - validation: Check whether outward-increasing line ratios persist with high C/O and elemental depletion [Summary: "The simulated HNC-to-HCN line ratio generally increases outward, which is consistent with the preferential destruction of HNC in warmer regions."] [Summary: "This conclusion is robust even for high C and O depletions and C/O$>$1."]
- result: Fiducial-model HNC 3-2 emission peak radius = 100 ± not stated au [Emission morphology: "For HNC lines, emission peaks at 100 au for the $3-2$ transition and at $\sim$300 au for $1-0$ transition"]
- result: Fiducial-model HNC 1-0 emission peak radius = ~300 ± not stated au [Emission morphology: "For HNC lines, emission peaks at 100 au for the $3-2$ transition and at $\sim$300 au for $1-0$ transition"]
- result: Fiducial-model prominent HCN ring radius, both transitions = 100 ± not stated au [Emission morphology: "For HCN, our model predicts a prominent ring around 100 au for both transitions"]
- result: Fiducial-model fainter HCN 1-0 second ring radius = 300 ± not stated au [Emission morphology: "a fainter second ring around 300 au also emerges in the $1-0$ transition."]
- result: Fiducial-model radial HNC-to-HCN line-ratio range over 50–350 au = 0.1 to 0.6 ± not stated dimensionless [Emission morphology: "The HNC-to-HCN line ratio generally increases outward, varying from 0.1 to 0.6 in regions where most line emission originates (50 to 350 au in the fiducial model)."]
- result: Modeled disk-integrated HNC-to-HCN 1-0 line ratio = 0.2~0.3 ± not stated dimensionless [HNC-to-HCN line ratio compared to obser…: "Disk-integrated HNC-to-HCN line ratios are rather constant in our models with varying stellar and disk conditions, with 0.2$\sim$0.3 for the $1-0$ transition and 0.1$\sim$0.2 for the $3-2$ transition"]
- result: Modeled disk-integrated HNC-to-HCN 3-2 line ratio = 0.1~0.2 ± not stated dimensionless [HNC-to-HCN line ratio compared to obser…: "Disk-integrated HNC-to-HCN line ratios are rather constant in our models with varying stellar and disk conditions, with 0.2$\sim$0.3 for the $1-0$ transition and 0.1$\sim$0.2 for the $3-2$ transition"]
- result: Model 1-0 excitation temperature relative to gas temperature = ~50% ± not stated dimensionless [Excitation and column density: "We find that the excitation temperature for the $1-0$ line in our model is $\sim50\%$ of the gas temperature."]
- limitation (authors): Model abundances and column densities uncertain by at least a factor of a few [Chemical network: "Given the uncertainties in individual rate coefficients and physical structure, our model abundances and column densities have uncertainties of at least a factor of a few."]
- limitation (authors): Equal HNC and HCN photodissociation rates assumed; actual HNC rate may be twice as high [Chemical network: "The same photodissociation rate was used for HCN and HNC; the actural HNC photodissociation rate may be a factor of 2 higher than that of HCN (cite: Aguado2017)."]
- limitation (authors): TW Hya short-spacing coverage may affect its radial profile and apparent central component [Comparisons to new HNC observations of …: "The data presented here lack the short-spacing baselines that might affect the radial profile, thus future observations are needed to confirm the presence of this central component."]
- not stated (card): Resolve the TW Hya project-code discrepancy: 2017.1.0156.S in the observation section versus 2017.1.01056.S in the acknowledgements.; TW Hya uvcontsub line-free channels, fit order and spectral-window selections.; tclean cell size, image size, deconvolver, cleaning mask, threshold and iteration limits for both datasets.; Whether primary-beam correction or self-calibration was applied.; TW Hya dep…

### 2018A&A...612A..72J: The ALMA-PILS survey: 3D modeling of the envelope, disks and dust filament of IRAS 16293-2422 (2018; star_formation, astrochemistry; archival_reuse)
- question: What do 3D dust and gas radiative transfer models of IRAS 16293 (envelope, disks, filament, two radiation sources) imply about the disk structures and the luminosities of sources A and B, constrained by ALMA-PILS 868 um continuum and CO is…
- step 2: Compare envelope temperature for one vs two sources
  - method: RADMC-3D Monte Carlo dust temperature for 1D envelope with density plateau; volumes above 30, 50, 90 K for one or two sources [Envelope temperature structure: "we used the dust envelope model from Eq. [\[eq:rhoplat\]](#eq:rhoplat) with one or two radiation sources in `RADMC-3D`, without including the filament and disk component models."]
  - software: RADMC-3D
  - parameter: rplat = 600 AU, p = 1.7, L = 21 Lsun total, L1 = 10.5, 14.0 or 18.0 [Envelope temperature structure: "$r_{\mathrm{plat}}$ = 600 AU, $L_*$ = 21 L$_{\ensuremath{\odot}}$ (if one source) and $L_1$ = 10.5, 14.0 or 18.0 L$_{\ensuremath{\odot}}$"]
  - assumption: Sublimation temperatures 30 K (CO), 50 K (H2CO), 90 K (H2O) [Envelope temperature structure: "as these temperatures roughly correspond to the sublimation temperatures of CO, H$_2$CO and H$_2$O, respectively"]
- step 3: Model 3D dust density (envelope, filament, PP-disk or rotating toroid disks) and compare to 868 um continuum
  - method: RADMC-3D dust temperature and ray tracing at 868 um, convolved to 0.5'' beam, compared with peak fluxes (within 10%), aspect ratio of disk A (1.64, within 10%) and N_H2 limit; coarse parameter-space exclusion [4.1 Disk component model constraints: "which we required the model to match within 10 % along with the peak central flux density of source A within 10 %."]
  - software: RADMC-3D, MIRIAD
  - parameter: PP-disk p = 0.5, flaring Psi = 0.25 fixed; Sigma0, H0, i, LA free [4.6 Model analysis: "while $p$ and $\varPsi$ were fixed to 0.5 and 0.25, respectively"]
  - parameter: Disk radii 150 AU (A) and 50 AU (B); MA = 1 Msun, MB = 0.1 Msun [4 Dust density structures of IRAS 16293: "The disk component models were fixed to 150 and 50 AU around source A and B, respectively"]
  - assumption: Dust-to-gas mass ratio 100; bare-grain opacities above 90 K, iterated twice [Dust opacity & temperature: "Therefore, two recursions of the bare-grain opacity allocation, that is, three Monte Carlo dust temperature estimations in total were performed"]
  - validation: SED sanity check; filament column density check against 0.056 Jy/beam gives 9.9 vs model 9.7 g cm-2 [4.3 Dust filament: "The dust column density of the filament was derived to be 9.9 g cm$^{-2}$, while the model column density was 9.7 g cm$^{-2}$"]
- step 4: Model CO isotopologue emission and constrain luminosity ratio
  - method: LIME non-LTE line radiative transfer on RADMC-3D dust temperatures; abundance jump at 30 K; synthetic moment-0 maps compared qualitatively with observations at different LA [Gas line emission modeling: "the $J$=3–2 line emission from $^{13}$CO, C$^{17}$O and C$^{18}$O of select dust models were modeled in `LIME`"]
  - software: LIME, RADMC-3D
  - parameter: Freeze-out factor 0.01 below 30 K; abundances lowered by factor 5 for matching C18O [Abundance modeling: "a drop factor of 10$^{-2}$ was used in regions with $T$ $<$ 30 K, to imitate freeze-out"]
  - assumption: Gas and dust temperatures coupled; Keplerian rotation (PP-disk), Terebey et al. velocities (toroid), free-fall elsewhere; LIME model radius 2000 AU [Gas line emission modeling: "we assumed that the dust and gas temperatures are coupled"]
  - validation: Qualitative comparison of synthetic and observed moment maps [Gas line emission modeling: "Optimally, the absorption features around each source and the major features of the interbinary CO isotopologue emission seen in Fig. [2](#fig:co_obs) were recreated in the synthetic moment zero maps"]
- result: Luminosity of source A = ~18 ± coarse parameter steps; not stated Lsun [abstract: "$L_\mathrm{A}$ $\sim$ 18 L$_\ensuremath{\odot}$ and $L_\mathrm{B}$ $\sim$ 3 L$_\ensuremath{\odot}$"]
- result: Luminosity of source B = ~3 ± not stated Lsun [abstract: "$L_\mathrm{A}$ $\sim$ 18 L$_\ensuremath{\odot}$ and $L_\mathrm{B}$ $\sim$ 3 L$_\ensuremath{\odot}$"]
- result: Observed aspect ratio of 868 um continuum around source A = 1.64 ± not stated dimensionless [4.1 Disk component model constraints: "The observed 868 $\upmu$m continuum emission in a 2$^{\prime\prime} \times 2^{\prime\prime}$ box around source A was found to have an aspect ratio of 1.64"]
- result: Disk B PP-disk scale height at 100 AU needed for LB = 3 Lsun = >53 ± not stated AU [Discussion: "the PP-disk model around source B needs a scale-height of more than 53 AU at 100 AU, when $L_\mathrm{B}$ = 3 L$_{\ensuremath{\odot}}$"]
- result: Relative difference in 90 K volume, single star vs binary = up to ~40% ± not stated percent [Envelope temperature structure: "This is more pronounced for the 90 K volumes, where the relative difference is almost up to $40~\%$."]
- result: Filament dust column density, observed vs model = 9.9 vs 9.7 ± not stated g cm-2 [4.3 Dust filament: "The dust column density of the filament was derived to be 9.9 g cm$^{-2}$, while the model column density was 9.7 g cm$^{-2}$"]
- limitation (authors): Match is qualitative; no confidence intervals; parameters coarse; CO abundance result may be model dependent [Conclusions: "This result needs to be investigated further, as the low abundances could be model dependent."]
- not stated (card): Calibration, imaging parameters (robust, deconvolver, clean masks) and combination of 12m and ACA data are only in Jørgensen et al. 2016; Which PILS data products/cubes were used and their final rms; Exact stellar/disk parameters for models in full (model grids not fully listed); no code released; Exact moment-0 velocity ranges/line frequencies and noise thresholds; Image-plane comparison of mode…

### 2025ApJ...995L...7L: The Rotation Dip in the Envelope─Disk Transition of HH 111: Evidence for Magnetic Braking (2025; star_formation; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Whether the gas kinematics in the envelope-disk transition region of HH 111 VLA 1 show a rotation (and infall) velocity dip that supports magnetic braking
- step 3: Build flattened infalling-rotating envelope model and radiative transfer
  - method: Parametric flattened envelope with power-law density and temperature, ballistic envelope kinematics, power-law angular momentum in transition region, Keplerian disk inside 160 au; LTE radiative transfer with RADMC-3D to make model channel … [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "we constructed a flattened, infalling, and rotating envelope model based on (cite: Lee_2010ApJ) and extended it to include the transition region and Keplerian disk."] [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "Radiative transfer was performed using RADMC-3D (cite: Dullemond_2012ascl.soft02015D) under LTE to produce model channel maps and then the PV diagram of"]
  - software: RADMC-3D
  - parameter: C18O abundance fixed at 1.7e-7 relative to H2 [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "The C$^{18}$O abundance relative to molecular hydrogen is fixed at 1.7 $\times$ 10$^{-7}$."]
  - parameter: inclination ~18 deg to the plane of sky; model spans 10 au to 8000 au [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "The inclination angle of the flattened envelope to the plane of sky is $\sim$18$^\circ$, a mean value derived from the disk (cite: Lee_2020NatAs...4..142L) and jet (cite: Reipurth_1992ApJ)."] [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "The model spans from 10 au"]
  - parameter: best-fit structure: H0 ~0.4 arcsec (160 au), theta0 ~18 deg, n0 ~1.3e6 cm^-3, p ~ -0.13+/-0.01, T0 ~20+/-1 K, q ~ -0.5+/-0.1 [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "The best-fit physical parameters are: $H_0 \sim$ 0$\overset{\prime\prime}{.}$4 (160 au), $\theta_0 \sim 18^\circ$, $n_0 \sim 1.3 \times 10^6$ cm$^{-3}$, $p \sim -0.13\pm0.01$, $T_0 \sim 20\pm1$ K and $q \sim -0.5\pm0.1$."]
  - assumption: single power-law density and temperature across envelope, transition region and disk [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "We adopt single power-law forms across the envelope, transition region, and disk."]
  - assumption: total mass of protostar plus disk 1.8 Msun; envelope mass neglected in infall velocity [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "The mass of the envelope is $\sim$0.6$M_\odot$, which contributes less than 15% to the infall velocity and is therefore neglected."]
  - assumption: envelope ballistic with conserved angular momentum and energy; rotation and radial velocities same as mid-plane values [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "In the envelope, we assume a ballistic motion under the conservation of angular momentum and energy (cite: Sakai_2014Natur.507...78S)."]
  - validation: Model PV diagram overlaid on the observed PV diagram and residual map made by subtracting the model from the observed PV diagram [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "The residual map in Figure [3](#fig:pv_obs_model)(c), obtained by subtracting the model from the observed PV diagram"]
- step 4: Baseline transition-region model with power-law angular momentum
  - method: Power-law angular momentum l(R) = l0 (R/Re)^b between envelope and disk with b set from l0 and the Keplerian value at Rd; infall velocity from energy conservation [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "Since no analytic solution exists for the transition region, we adopted a power-law form as the angular momentum distribution for the first try"] [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "These values yield *b* $\sim$ 0.38."]
  - software: RADMC-3D
  - parameter: b ~0.38 (l0 ~1333 au km/s, ~530 au km/s at Rd ~160 au) [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "The power-law index *b* is determined using the envelope angular momentum $\ell_0 \sim 1333$ au km s$^{-1}$ (cite: Lee_2010ApJ)"]
  - validation: Residuals show the model does not reproduce the sharp velocity drop in the intermediate region, motivating a rotation dip [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "This discrepancy motivates the additional modeling of a rotation velocity dip, as discussed in the next subsection."]
- step 5: Add log-Gaussian rotation dip and declining infall velocity
  - method: Multiply the angular-momentum-conserving rotation profile by 1 - A exp(-(ln(R/Rdip))^2/(2 sigma^2)) with different widths inside and outside the dip; infall velocity given a log-Gaussian decline from Rc [3.4 A Dip in Rotation Velocity: "we adopted the angular-momentum–conserving profile as the baseline and introduced a log-Gaussian term to model the dip"]
  - software: RADMC-3D
  - parameter: first attempt: A_phi ~0.8, R_phi,dip ~1.5 arcsec (600 au), sigma_in ~1.6, sigma_out ~0.4; Rc ~5 arcsec, sigma_c ~1 [3.4 A Dip in Rotation Velocity: "The best-fit parameters are: $A_\phi$ $\sim$ 0.8, $R_{\phi,dip}$ $\sim$ 1$\overset{\prime\prime}{.}$5 (600 au), $\sigma_{\rm in}$ $\sim$ 1.6, and $\sigma_{\rm out}\sim$0.4."]
  - validation: Improved agreement between model and observed velocity profiles, especially the dip between ~4 and 1 arcsec; ~30% drop in the outer region (13 to 5 arcsec) still not captured [3.4 A Dip in Rotation Velocity: "This refinement significantly improved the agreement between the model and the observed velocity profiles"]
- step 6: Add log-Gaussian dip in infall velocity and refine the best-fit model
  - method: Log-Gaussian dip in infall velocity applied at radii >3 arcsec, peak infall shifted to 3.5 arcsec, and rotation profile slightly refined [3.5 A Dip in Infall Velocity: "we introduced a similar log-Gaussian dip in the infall profile, applied only at radii greater than 3"] [3.5 A Dip in Infall Velocity: "we shift the position of the peak infall velocity from"]
  - software: RADMC-3D
  - parameter: infall: A_r ~0.35, R_r,dip ~7.5 arcsec (3000 au), sigma_r ~0.3, R_c ~3.5 arcsec (1400 au), sigma_c ~0.85 [3.5 A Dip in Infall Velocity: "The best-fit parameters for infall velocity are: $A_r \sim$ 0.35, $R_{r,dip}\sim7\overset{\prime\prime}{.}$5 (3000 au), $\sigma_r\sim0.3$, $R_c\sim3\overset{\prime\prime}{.}$5 (1400 au), and $\sigma_c\sim0.85$."]
  - parameter: rotation (refined): R_phi,dip ~1.5 arcsec (600 au), A_phi ~0.9, sigma_in ~1.3, sigma_out ~0.5 [3.5 A Dip in Infall Velocity: "To further improve the fit, we also slightly refined the rotation velocity profile with $R_{\phi,dip}$ $\sim$ 1$\overset{\prime\prime}{.}$5 (600 au), $A_\phi$ $\sim$ 0.9, $\sigma_{\rm in}$ $\sim$ 1.3, and $\sigma_{\rm out}\sim$ 0.5."]
  - assumption: density and temperature power-law simplifications do not affect the inferred velocity structure [3.6 The Best-fit Model: "However, the fitting results indicate that these assumptions do not affect the inferred velocity structure."]
  - validation: Residuals below ~10% of original values in most regions; up to 50% within 1 arcsec; velocity uncertainties estimated to be less than 20% [3.6 The Best-fit Model: "the residuals are reduced to below approximately 10% of the original values in most regions."] [3.6 The Best-fit Model: "matches observation well, with velocity uncertainties estimated to be less than 20%."]
- result: Infall velocity relative to free-fall and to ballistic (conserved energy) value near 7 arcsec = ~60% of free-fall; ~70% of conserved value ± not stated dimensionless [abstract: "dropping to approximately 60% of the free-fall velocity and 70% of conservation of angular momentum and energy"]
- result: Minimum rotation velocity at the dip (1.5 arcsec, 600 au) = 0.1 ± not stated km/s [4.1 Comparison to theoretical Model: "reaching a minimum of 0.1 km s"]
- result: Extent of transition region = ~5200 to 160 au ± not stated au [abstract: "in the transition region between the envelope and disk (from"]
- result: Power-law index b of angular momentum in baseline transition model = ~0.38 ± not stated dimensionless [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "These values yield *b* $\sim$ 0.38."]
- result: Envelope density power-law index p = -0.13 ± 0.01 dimensionless [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "The best-fit physical parameters are: $H_0 \sim$ 0$\overset{\prime\prime}{.}$4 (160 au), $\theta_0 \sim 18^\circ$, $n_0 \sim 1.3 \times 10^6$ cm$^{-3}$, $p \sim -0.13\pm0.01$, $T_0 \sim 20\pm1$ K and $q \sim -0.5\pm0.1$."]
- result: Mid-plane H2 number density at R0 = 2000 au = 1.3e6 ± not stated cm^-3 [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "The best-fit physical parameters are: $H_0 \sim$ 0$\overset{\prime\prime}{.}$4 (160 au), $\theta_0 \sim 18^\circ$, $n_0 \sim 1.3 \times 10^6$ cm$^{-3}$, $p \sim -0.13\pm0.01$, $T_0 \sim 20\pm1$ K and $q \sim -0.5\pm0.1$."]
- result: Temperature at R0 = 2000 au = 20 ± 1 K [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "The best-fit physical parameters are: $H_0 \sim$ 0$\overset{\prime\prime}{.}$4 (160 au), $\theta_0 \sim 18^\circ$, $n_0 \sim 1.3 \times 10^6$ cm$^{-3}$, $p \sim -0.13\pm0.01$, $T_0 \sim 20\pm1$ K and $q \sim -0.5\pm0.1$."]
- result: Temperature power-law index q = -0.5 ± 0.1 dimensionless [3.3 A Flattened Infalling Rotating Enve…: "The best-fit physical parameters are: $H_0 \sim$ 0$\overset{\prime\prime}{.}$4 (160 au), $\theta_0 \sim 18^\circ$, $n_0 \sim 1.3 \times 10^6$ cm$^{-3}$, $p \sim -0.13\pm0.01$, $T_0 \sim 20\pm1$ K and $q \sim -0.5\pm0.1$."]
- result: Rotation dip log-Gaussian parameters (final): A_phi, R_phi,dip, sigma_in, sigma_out = A_phi ~0.9; R_phi,dip ~1.5 arcsec (600 au); sigma_in ~1.3; sigma_out ~0.5 ± not stated dimensionless (R_phi,dip in arcsec/au) [3.5 A Dip in Infall Velocity: "To further improve the fit, we also slightly refined the rotation velocity profile with $R_{\phi,dip}$ $\sim$ 1$\overset{\prime\prime}{.}$5 (600 au), $A_\phi$ $\sim$ 0.9, $\sigma_{\rm in}$ $\sim$ 1.3, and $\sigma_{\rm out}\sim$ 0.5."]
- result: Infall log-Gaussian parameters (final): A_r, R_r,dip, sigma_r, R_c, sigma_c = A_r ~0.35; R_r,dip ~7.5 arcsec (3000 au); sigma_r ~0.3; R_c ~3.5 arcsec (1400 au); sigma_c ~0.85 ± not stated dimensionless (radii in arcsec/au) [3.5 A Dip in Infall Velocity: "The best-fit parameters for infall velocity are: $A_r \sim$ 0.35, $R_{r,dip}\sim7\overset{\prime\prime}{.}$5 (3000 au), $\sigma_r\sim0.3$, $R_c\sim3\overset{\prime\prime}{.}$5 (1400 au), and $\sigma_c\sim0.85$."]
- result: Velocity uncertainty of the best-fit model = less than 20% ± not stated dimensionless [3.6 The Best-fit Model: "matches observation well, with velocity uncertainties estimated to be less than 20%."]
- limitation (authors): Residuals within 1 arcsec remain large, up to 50%, attributed to innermost-envelope asymmetry and the linear PV structures [3.6 The Best-fit Model: "they remain relatively large, with some areas retaining up to 50%, likely due to the reported asymmetry in the innermost envelope"]
- limitation (authors): Simplified single power-law density and temperature contribute to residuals [3.6 The Best-fit Model: "Notice that some residuals should also result from the simplified power-law assumptions for density and temperature."]
- limitation (authors): Efficiency of magnetic braking depends on uncertain physical parameters [4.1.2 Region II: Magnetic Braking: "the efficiency of angular momentum removal via magnetic braking depends on uncertain physical parameters"]
- not stated (card): Whether calibration used the ALMA pipeline or manual scripts, and the CASA version(s); tclean parameters: deconvolver, cell size, image size, masks, niter/threshold, and how continuum was subtracted (line-free channels); Spectral setup of the C18O window (channel width of the raw data, rest frequency used) and any regridding to 0.15 km/s; feather parameters (e.g. total power scaling, resolution m…

### 2012ApJ...757..129R: Kinematics of the CO Gas in the Inner Regions of the TW Hya Disk (2012; protoplanetary_disks; archival_reuse)
- question: What do the spatially and spectrally resolved 12CO J=2-1 and J=3-2 line wings from the TW Hya disk reveal about the temperature and kinematic structure of the inner disk?
- step 2: Fit fiducial model
  - method: Parametric viscous-disk model with LIME non-LTE radiative transfer, imaged through CASA; MPFIT chi2 minimization on visibilities then manual adjustment [3.2 The Failure of Standard Models: "utilized the modified Levenberg-Marquardt minimization algorithm `MPFIT` (cite: markwardt09) to help find parameters that produced a low $\chi^2$ in the spectral visibilities"]
  - software: LIME, MPFIT, CASA
  - parameter: PA fixed 151 deg; M*=0.8 Msun; inclination free; T10,q free per line [3.2 The Failure of Standard Models: "the major axis position angle was set to 151$^\circ$ (east of north)"]
  - assumption: vertically isothermal, mu=2.37, Keplerian, vr=vz=0 [3.1 Modeling Overview: "we implicitly assumed that the disk is vertically isothermal (at each radius)"]
  - fitting: MPFIT chi2 on visibilities, then manual tweaks; priors not stated [3.2 The Failure of Standard Models: "the model parameters were slightly adjusted manually to best reproduce the observed channel maps"]
  - validation: Compare model and data channel maps [3.2 The Failure of Standard Models: "The fiducial model is a good match to the ALMA data near the systemic velocity, but notably fails to reproduce the emission line wings"]
- step 3: Test high-q, high-M*, high-i models
  - method: Uniformly modify q, M*, or i and compare channel maps [3.2 The Failure of Standard Models: "We constructed such a “high-$q$" model with a larger radial temperature gradient ($q = 0.65$)"]
  - software: LIME
  - parameter: q=0.65; M*=1.5 Msun; i=8 deg [3.2 The Failure of Standard Models: "a more inclined viewing geometry ($i = 8\ensuremath{^\circ}$)"]
  - validation: All reproduce wings but fail near systemic velocity [3.2 The Failure of Standard Models: "No reasonable combinations of $q$, $M_{\ast}$, and $i$ can simultaneously reproduce both the CO line wings and the emission morphology near the systemic velocity."]
- step 4: Alternative models: hot inner disk, non-Keplerian, warp
  - method: Add radius-dependent temperature scaling, velocity scaling f(r), or inclination i(r); warp via quaternion rotation [3.3.3 A Disk Warp: "The velocity field is tilted using a quaternion transformation, as described in more detail in the Appendix."]
  - software: LIME, CASA
  - parameter: dT=3 inside r_hot=4 AU [3.3.1 A Hot Inner Disk: "scaling factor $\delta T = 3$ inside a radius $r_{\rm hot} = 4$ AU"]
  - parameter: non-Keplerian: r_b≈60 AU, x=0.15 [3.3.2 A Non-Keplerian Velocity Field: "Adopting $r_b \approx 60$ AU and $x = 0.15$ to describe $f$"]
  - parameter: warp: i0≈8 deg at r0=5 AU, y=0.15 [3.3.3 A Disk Warp: "with $i_0 \approx 8\ensuremath{^\circ}$ at $r_0 = 5$ AU and a gradient $y = 0.15$"]
  - assumption: vertically thin warped disk aligned with major axis [3.3.3 A Disk Warp: "aligned with the disk major axis (assuming a vertically thin disk; see the Appendix)"]
  - fitting: Qualitative, not optimized [3.3.2 A Non-Keplerian Velocity Field: "The {$r_b$, $x$} values adopted here are not unique (nor optimized)"]
  - validation: Compare channel maps and PV diagrams [3.3.1 A Hot Inner Disk: "as well as in position-velocity diagrams (constructed from a slice along the disk major axis)"]
- step 5: Test warp against scattered light
  - method: RADMC-3D 0.8 micron image of warp model with dust only outside 4 AU, isotropic scattering; azimuthal profile in 20-deg bins at r=70-88 AU [4 Discussion: "we calculated a normalized intensity profile in 20$^\circ$ azimuthal bins in an annular ring from $r = 70$-88 AU."]
  - software: RADMC-3D
  - assumption: dust and gas co-located outside 4 AU; no dust inside [4 Discussion: "assuming that the dust and gas are co-located outside a radius of 4 AU"]
  - fitting: Not a fit [4 Discussion: "this scattered light model is *not* a fit to the *HST*/STIS observations"]
  - validation: Compared to Roberge et al. azimuthal modulation [4 Discussion: "demonstrate a striking resemblance to the azimuthal modulation pattern inferred from the *HST*/STIS data"]
- result: Hot model temperature scaling = 3 inside 4 AU ± not stated dimensionless [3.3.1 A Hot Inner Disk: "scaling factor $\delta T = 3$ inside a radius $r_{\rm hot} = 4$ AU"]
- result: Warp inclination gradient y = 0.15 ± not stated dimensionless [3.3.3 A Disk Warp: "a gradient $y = 0.15$"]
- result: Fiducial inclination = 5.8 ± not stated deg [Table 2: "$i_{10}$ | \[$^\circ$\] | 5.8 | 5.8 | 6.0 | 8.0 | 5.8"]
- limitation (authors): Model parameters are not unique nor optimized; limited resolution [3.3.3 A Disk Warp: "the warp model parameters we determine or even the formulation we adopt are not unique, nor optimized"]
- not stated (card): CLEAN threshold, polygon mask geometry, cell and image size; Exact line-free channels and polynomial order for continuum subtraction; LIME settings (number of grid points, photons) and CO abundance X_co value; Gas-to-dust ratio and dust properties details for RADMC-3D model; Exact MPFIT setup, initial values and how manual adjustments were made; Distance to TW Hya adopted

### 2016A&A...586A...5M: A detailed view of the gas shell around R Sculptoris with ALMA (2016; evolved_stars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the mass and temperature of the gas shell around R Scl, and how did the mass-loss rate evolve during and after the thermal pulse?
- step 3: Fit shell mass and temperature
  - method: Grid of 1D Monte Carlo radiative transfer models, chi2 minimisation on integrated intensities of all transitions [3.2 The shell emission: "For each model we determine the best-fit model (Fig. [6](#f:shellspec)) by minimising the $\chi^2$ value"]
  - software: Monte Carlo radiative transfer code (Schöier & Olofsson 2001)
  - parameter: masses 2.5e-3 to 4.75e-3 Msun step 0.25e-3; T 10-290 K step 20 K; Gaussian shell FWHM 2'' [3.2 The shell emission: "The grid is calculated for shell-masses between $2.5\times10^{-3}$ $M_{\odot}$ to $4.75\times10^{-3}$ $M_{\odot}$ in steps of $0.25\times10^{-3}$ $M_{\odot}$ and temperatures between 10 K to 290 K in steps of 20 K."]
  - assumption: f(12CO)=4.3e-4, constant temperature, 20% observational uncertainty, distance 370 pc [3.2 The shell emission: "The temperature is assumed to be constant throughout the shell."]
  - fitting: chi2 minimisation over grid, sigma=20% [3.2 The shell emission: "$\sigma_{\rm{obs,i}}$ is the uncertainty in the observation is assumed to be 20%"]
  - validation: Compared with Olofsson et al. 2015 results from SD and CI [3.2 The shell emission: "using essentially independent methods (interferometry vs. SD observations and an independent consistency check with CI observations)"]
- step 4: Model CSE mass-loss from shell-subtracted SD spectra
  - method: Convolve best-fit shell model with SD beams, subtract from SD spectra, model residual with constant mass-loss rate radiative transfer [3.3 The recent evolution of the stellar…: "we convolved the best-fit model of the shell with the SD beams of the respective transitions (Fig. [7](#f:sdsub), top), and subtract it from the SD observations."]
  - software: Monte Carlo radiative transfer code (Schöier & Olofsson 2001)
  - parameter: a=2.5; T_kin power law beta=-0.85; R0 = 3 stellar radii; velocity 3 to 14.3 km/s [3.3 The recent evolution of the stellar…: "The velocity increases gradually from 3 km s$^{-1}$ to 14.3 km s$^{-1}$"]
  - assumption: Constant mass-loss rate, 1/r^2 density, black-body Teff 2300 K, L 5200 Lsun [3.3 The recent evolution of the stellar…: "Assuming a constant mass-loss rate results in a 1/$r^2$ density distribution."]
  - fitting: Manual fit to SD spectra; model degenerate [3.3 The recent evolution of the stellar…: "Finally, the models are very degenerate between the chosen mass-loss rate, half-abundance radius, temperature profile, and velocity profile."]
  - validation: Emitting region sizes consistent with ALMA/SD limits [3.3 The recent evolution of the stellar…: "These results are consistent with the limits set by the ALMA and SD data"]
- result: Shell mass = 4.5e-3 ± ±1.5e-3 Msun [3.2 The shell emission: "The best-fit model gives a shell-mass of $(4.5\pm1.5)\times10^{-3}$ $M_{\odot}$ at a temperature of 50$^{+200}_{-20}$ K."]
- result: Shell temperature = 50 ± +200/-20 K [3.2 The shell emission: "at a temperature of 50$^{+200}_{-20}$ K"]
- result: Mass-loss rate during shell creation = 2.3e-5 ± +1.7/-1.3 e-5 Msun/yr [3.2 The shell emission: "leads to a mass-loss rate of $(2.3^{+1.7}_{-1.3})\times10^{-5}$ $M_{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$ during the creation of the shell."]
- result: Average post-pulse mass-loss rate = 1.6e-5 ± not stated Msun/yr [3.3 The recent evolution of the stellar…: "using a mass-loss rate of 1.6$\times10^{-5}$ $M_{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$ and a half-abundance radius of 7.2$''$."]
- result: Recent mass-loss rate (IRAM CO 1-0, APEX CO 3-2) = 3.5e-6 ± not stated Msun/yr [3.3 The recent evolution of the stellar…: "a mass-loss rate of 3.5$\times10^{-6}$ $M_{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$"]
- limitation (authors): Cycle 0 lacks ACA, so extended flux is lost; mass-loss modelling degenerate [3.3 The recent evolution of the stellar…: "The loss of extended flux in the current ALMA data prevents using these observations to effectively constrain the mass-loss rate evolution"]
- limitation (authors): Distance very uncertain [2.1 R Sculptoris: "Current distance estimates are very uncertain, ranging from 266 pc"]
- not stated (card): Phase and other calibrator details, flagging and bandpass solutions; Exact CLEAN thresholds, cell and image size; Channel binning and line-free channels; Molecular data (collisional rates) used in radiative transfer; Details of shell subtraction normalisation and how SD data beam-matched; Treatment of ALMA flux scale across SBs; Model grid for the CSE fits (how the fit was chosen)

### 2020ApJ...904...78S: Constraining the Chemical Signatures and the Outburst Mechanism of the Class 0 Protostar HOPS 383 (2020; star_formation, protoplanetary_disks, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Can the chemical signatures (N2H+ vs HCO+) and the disk mass/stability of the outbursting Class 0 protostar HOPS 383 constrain the outburst mechanism, e.g. gravitational instability?
- step 6: Fit radiative transfer disk+envelope models to ALMA and ALMA+VLA visibilities and the SED
  - method: RADMC-3D models (disk, Ulrich envelope with outflow cavity, protostar) with MCMC fitting using emcee, for truncated and exponentially tapered disks; Fourier transform of synthetic images to visibilities [Radiative Transfer Code: "We use RADMC-3D ((cite: Dullemond12)) for the continuum modeling and to calculate the temperature throughout the density structure of the disk and envelope."] [Radiative Transfer Code: "Synthetic visibilities are then created by Fourier-transforming the synthetic millimeter images."]
  - software: RADMC-3D, emcee, pdspy
  - parameter: chi^2 weights w_vis = 0.5, w_SED = 1 [Radiative Transfer Code: "Our fits use $w_{\text{vis}} = 0.5$ and $w_{\text{SED}} = 1$ for both the ALMA only and the ALMA+VLA models, which we empirically determined to fit both data sets simultaneously."]
  - parameter: Protostar temperature 4000 K; luminosity L* free [Protostar: "Our models include a central protostar with a temperature of 4000 K, representative of a general low mass star in the pre-main sequence phase."]
  - parameter: Dust: 60% silicate, 15% amorphous carbon, 25% porosity; minimum grain size 0.005 micron; envelope max grain 1 micron; disk max grain size and p (2.5-4.5) free [Dust: "The dust grain models consist of 60% silicate, 15% amorphous carbon, and 25% porosity."] [Dust: "The minimum grain size is assumed to be 0.005 $\mu$m for all opacities."]
  - parameter: SMA visibilities not fit [Model Fitting: "We do not include the visibility data from the SMA due to their lower signal-to-noise."]
  - assumption: Models constrain dust mass only; total mass quoted with gas-to-dust ratio of 100 [Model Results: "we list the total mass using the canonical gas-to-dust ratio of 100"]
  - assumption: Protostar mass used only to set the envelope density structure, not fit [Envelope: "is the mass of the protostar only used for setting density structure and is not constrained by fitting"]
  - fitting: emcee MCMC minimizing weighted chi^2 of visibilities and SED; best fit = median of walkers once steady; 1 sigma (68%) as uncertainty; lost walkers trimmed; priors not stated [Radiative Transfer Code: "The 1$\sigma$ value around the median containing $\sim$68% of the walkers is considered as the uncertainty on the parameters."] [Radiative Transfer Code: "The walkers that are lost, meaning that they are outside the primary distribution of walkers, are “trimmed" and not considered when determining parameter uncertainties."]
  - validation: Visual comparison of models with visibilities and SED; convergence judged by stable best-fit values over ~150-200 steps; ALMA+VLA models fit short baselines and far-IR/submm SED worse and do not include free-free [Radiative Transfer Code: "the best fit values of the parameters remain more or less constant for $\sim$ 150 - 200 steps."] [HOPS 383 ALMA+VLA Model: "The models do not fit short baselines as well as the ALMA only models and the fit to the far-IR and submillimiter SED is less optimal than the ALMA only models."]
- step 7: Compute Toomre Q versus radius and pre-outburst Q from the model profiles
  - method: Use model surface density and temperature (averaged over a few slices above midplane) in Q = c_s Omega / (pi G Sigma); pre-outburst temperature scaled by (L_pre/L_burst)^(1/4) [Toomre Q vs. Radius: "Extracting these, we can use Equation [\[eq:Q\]](#eq:Q) to plot Toomre’s $Q$ as a function of radius."] [Toomre Q vs. Radius: "we scale the disk temperature by $\sqrt[4]{L_{*, pre}/L_{*, burst}}$"]
  - software: RADMC-3D
  - parameter: L*,pre = 0.5 Lsun; model-derived L*,burst; protostar masses 0.1 to 0.5 Msun [Toomre Q vs. Radius: "We use the model derived luminosities for L$_{*, burst}$ and take L$_{*, pre}$ = 0.5 L$_{\odot}$"] [Toomre Q vs. Radius: "Therefore we plot the $Q$ with different protostar masses of 0.1 M$_{\odot}$ to 0.5 M$_{\odot}$."]
  - assumption: Keplerian disk; dust-to-gas 1:100 [Toomre Q vs. Radius: "We assume the disk around HOPS 383 to be Keplerian since there is no evidence pointing otherwise."] [Toomre Q vs. Radius: "We assume a dust-to-gas mass ratio of 1:100 for our calculation."]
- result: ALMA-only model disk mass, inclination and position angle = ~0.02 Msun; i ~ 51 deg; PA ~ 49 deg ± see Table 2 (e.g. 0.026 +0.004/-0.003 truncated) Msun; deg [HOPS 383 ALMA Model: "Both models have a protostellar disk mass of $\approx$0.02 $M_{\odot}$ and independently find the same inclination ($i \approx 51^{\circ}$) and position angle ($P.A. \approx 49^{\circ}$)."]
- result: ALMA-only model envelope masses and radii (truncated, tapered) = 0.38 and 1.1 Msun; 5700 and 12000 au ± not well constrained Msun; au [HOPS 383 ALMA Model: "Although the envelope masses (0.38 $M_{\odot}$ and 1.1 $M_{\odot}$) and radii (5700 au and 12000 au) differ between the truncated and tapered model"]
- result: ALMA-only model luminosity = ~5 ± not stated Lsun [HOPS 383 ALMA Model: "The luminosities of both models are very similar with $L \sim 5$ $L_{\odot}$."]
- result: ALMA+VLA model disk masses (truncated, tapered) = ~0.33 and ~0.21 ± see Table 2 (0.330 +0.025/-0.028; 0.214 +0.011/-0.019) Msun [HOPS 383 ALMA+VLA Model: "The ALMA+VLA model results find massive disks of $M \sim 0.33 M_{\odot}$ and $M \sim 0.21 M_{\odot}$ for the truncated and the tapered cases, respectively."]
- result: ALMA+VLA model envelope masses (truncated, tapered) = 0.32 and 0.09 ± not stated Msun [HOPS 383 ALMA+VLA Model: "The truncated disk model has a very similar envelope mass compared to the ALMA only models with $M_{env}$ = 0.32 $M_{\odot}$."]
- result: Radius of the 25 K temperature limit now (ALMA only, ALMA+VLA models) = ~550 au; ~250-400 au ± not stated au [HCO+ and N2H+: "the ALMA only and the ALMA+VLA models have 25 K temperature limit at $\sim$550 au radius and $\sim$250 - 400 au radius, respectively."]
- result: Radius of the 25 K temperature limit before the outburst = 50-100 ± not stated au [HCO+ and N2H+: "Before the outburst, the models have the 25 K temperature limit at a radius around 50 - 100 au"]
- result: Free-free to total 9 mm flux ratio and rescaled ALMA+VLA disk masses (truncated, tapered) = 0.57; ~0.14 and ~0.092 ± not stated dimensionless; Msun [Estimating Free-Free Subtracted Disk Pr…: "the ratio of free-free emission to total flux density is 234 $\mu$Jy/411 $\mu$Jy $\approx 0.57$."]
- limitation (authors): Analytic ALMA disk masses are lower limits if the 0.87 mm emission is optically thick [Disk Mass: "The dust emission may not be optically thin at 0.87 mm for all radii, thus, the disk masses are likely lower limits for their respective temperatures."]
- limitation (authors): VLA visibility fits omit the free-free contribution; models cannot fit VLA visibilities without worsening the SED fit [HOPS 383 ALMA+VLA Model: "Our models are not able to fully reproduce the visibilities from the VLA, without sacrificing the fit for the SED."]
- limitation (authors): Envelope properties are poorly constrained for lack of short baselines [Model Results: "While we model the envelopes, their properties are expected to be poorly constrained due to the lack of short baseline data."]
- not stated (card): Which of the ALMA execution blocks (2016 vs 2017) enter the continuum image and how they were combined; Self-calibration details: reference antenna, solution intervals, number of rounds, final cell/image size, clean threshold, mask and niter; How the 0.87 mm Gaussian source size was fit (task, region, uv or image plane); Line-free channel selection for uvcontsub and the 12CO channel/clean paramet…

### 2016MNRAS.460.2933M: Exocometary gas in the HD 181327 debris ring (2016; debris_disks; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Where are the mm dust and CO gas located in the HD 181327 debris ring, and what is the origin and mass of the CO?
- step 2: Fit dust ring model in visibilities
  - method: Axisymmetric Gaussian ring, RADMC-3D images, uvsim visibilities, emcee MCMC [3.1 Dust continuum: "we compare the observations with an axisymmetric debris disc model in the Visibility space."]
  - software: RADMC-3D, uvsim, emcee
  - parameter: Free parameters: r0, h, Δr, Mdust, PA, i, RA and Dec offsets; h lower limit 0.03 [3.1 Dust continuum: "The free parameters in our model image and visibilities are $r_0$, $h$, $\Delta r$, $M_{\mathrm{dust}}$, PA, inclination ($i$) and RA- and decl-offset."]
  - assumption: Astrosilicate, 3.5 g/cm3, Dohnanyi q=-3.5, amin 1.3 um, amax 1 cm, kappa_abs=1.1 cm2/g at 1.3 mm [3.1 Dust continuum: "We assume a Dohnanyi-like size distribution with a power law index of -3.5, and minimum and maximum grain size $a_{\min}=1.3 \mu$m and $a_{\max}=1.0$ cm."]
  - fitting: emcee affine-invariant sampler, uniform priors, likelihood ∝ exp(-chi2/2) [3.1 Dust continuum: "The likelihood function is defined proportional to $\exp[-\chi^2/2]$"]
  - validation: chi2 differs by 0.2% from MEM model; residuals consistent with noise [3.1 Dust continuum: "When we compare the $\chi^2$ of our best axisymmetric model with the MEM model we find a difference of 0.2%."]
- step 4: Fit non-LTE CO model for gas mass
  - method: RADMC-3D non-LTE models in image space against natural-weighted dirty map; MCMC on MCO, r0, Δr over grid of Tk and electron density [3.2 12CO (2-1): "The free parameters in the MCMC are the CO gas mass (M$_\mathrm{CO}$), ring radius ($r_0$) and FWHM of the ring ($\Delta r$)."]
  - software: RADMC-3D
  - parameter: ne0 between 1e-2 and 1e7 cm-3; Tk half to twice dust temperature (50 K) [3.2 12CO (2-1): "We vary n$_{\mathrm{e}^{-}}^{0}$between 10$^{-2}$-$10^{7}$ cm$^{-3}$ to cover from the radiation-dominated regime to LTE."]
  - assumption: h=0.07 and orientation fixed to dust fit; Keplerian rotation; systemic velocity 0.1 km/s; electrons as collision partner [3.2 12CO (2-1): "with a fixed vertical aspect ratio $h$ of 0.07 and fixed disc orientation"]
  - fitting: MCMC, likelihood ∝ exp(-chi2/2) accounting for independent beams and channel correlation [3.2 12CO (2-1): "taking into account the number of independent beams in the image and the correlation between adjacent channels in ALMA data"]
  - validation: Model spectrum compared with half-disc spectra [3.2 12CO (2-1): "The model spectrum matches roughly the observed CO line profile."]
- result: Dust ring radius r0 = 86.0 ± 0.4 AU [Table 1: "$r_{0}$ \[AU\]"]
- result: Dust ring FWHM = 23.2 ± 1.0 AU [abstract: "a radial width of $23.2\pm1.0$ AU"]
- result: Dust mass in main ring = 0.442 ± 0.005 Mearth [3.1 Dust continuum: "The derived total dust mass in the main ring is $0.442\pm0.005$ M$_{\oplus}$."]
- result: Inclination = 30.0 ± 0.7 deg [Table 1: "$30.0\pm0.7$"]
- result: Aspect ratio h upper limit = 0.14 ± 99.7% confidence dimensionless [3.1 Dust continuum: "we can put a 99.7% confidence upper limit of 0.14 for $h$"]
- result: CO mass (ne0=100 cm-3, Tgas=Tdust) = 1.8e-6 ± 0.6e-6 Mearth [3.2 12CO (2-1): "We find M$_\mathrm{CO}=1.8\pm0.6 \times10^{-6}$ M$_{\oplus}$"]
- result: CO ring radius = 81 ± +10/-9 AU [3.2 12CO (2-1): "$r_0=81^{+10}_{-9}$ AU"]
- limitation (authors): Dust mass depends strongly on assumed composition and amax; h not fully constrained [3.1 Dust continuum: "On the other hand the scale height is not fully constrained."]
- not stated (card): Briggs robust value; Line-free channels for continuum fit; uvmem regularization/hyperparameters and pixel size; uvmem and uvsim code availability; Priors' bounds for MCMC and number of walkers/steps; Pipeline CASA version; Mask/exact kernel for CO spectrum smoothing

### 2026A&A...711A.159M: The formation radius of HCN in O-rich asymptotic giant branch stars (2026; evolved_stars, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What is the formation radius of HCN in the inner circumstellar envelopes of the O-rich AGB stars R Crt and IK Tau, to shed light on nonequilibrium chemical processes?
- step 2: Model dusty circumstellar envelope of R Crt via SED fitting
  - method: Run a grid of 1D spherical continuum radiative transfer models with DUSTY V2 varying tau_10 and T_c, and find the best fit to VizieR photometric SED via reduced chi-squared [Model: "To describe the dusty envelope, we used the code DUSTY V2 (cite: dusty), which solves the continuum radiative transfer for a spherical dusty envelope, including dust scattering, absorption, and emission."] [Model: "A grid of models was run, where the optical depth at 10 $\mu$m, $\tau_{10}$, and the dust temperature at the condensation radius, $T_c$, were varied."]
  - software: DUSTY V2
  - parameter: Spherical grains of radius 0.1 um; warm silicate optical properties from Suh (1999) [Model: "We considered spherical grains with a radius of 0.1 $\mu$m, with the optical properties of warm silicate dust from (cite: Suh1999)."]
  - assumption: Spherical dusty envelope; dust present only beyond condensation radius r_c with uniform gas-to-dust ratio; 20% uniform flux uncertainties [Model: "For the envelope, we assumed the presence of dust with a uniform gas-to-dust mass ratio, $\Psi$, only beyond a given radial distance, which can be rationalized as the dust condensation radius, $r_c$."] [Model: "We adopted $20 \%$ uniform uncertainties for all measurements."]
  - fitting: Grid search minimizing reduced chi-squared over T_c and tau_10 [Model: "A grid of models was run, where the optical depth at 10 $\mu$m, $\tau_{10}$, and the dust temperature at the condensation radius, $T_c$, were varied. Moreover, a $\chi^2_\textrm{red}$ analysis was performed to find the model that best reproduces the observed spectral energy distribution (SED)."]
  - validation: Visual inspection of best-fit SED against photometric data and chi-squared contour map (Fig. 3) [Model: "The best-fit model is compared with the observed fluxes in the left panel of Fig. 3, while the right panel shows the $\chi^2_\textrm{red}$ parameter as a function of $T_c$ and $\tau_{10}$."]
- step 3: Model gaseous envelope (mass loss rate and temperature profile) of R Crt via CO lines
  - method: Run a grid of non-LTE radiative transfer models for CO (v=0 and v=1) using modified RATRAN, varying mass loss rate and gas temperature exponent delta, and fit to archival single-dish/space CO line integrated intensities [Model: "To describe the gaseous envelope of R Crt, we proceeded as in (cite: Massalkhi2024) and carried out a grid of non local thermodynamic equilibrium (LTE) radiative transfer models for CO, varying the mass loss rate and exponent $\delta$ of the gas temperature radial profile."] [Model: "We included CO in the vibrational states $v=0$ and $v=1$ to account for infrared pumping and used the Monte Carlo algorithm of RATRAN (cite: ratran), modified to enable a layer-dependent number of levels, to solve the statistical equilibrium (see (cite: Massalkhi2024))."]
  - software: RATRAN
  - parameter: CO fractional abundance = 2.0e-4 relative to H2; v_0 = v_inf / 4; beta = 1; photospheric pressure = 5e-5 bar; density exponent b = 2 [Model: "where we assume $\varv_0 = \varv_\infty/4$ and $\beta=1$ (cite: Decin2010)."] [Model: "The fractional abundance of CO with respect to H$_2$ at the stellar photosphere was set to $2.0 \times 10^{-4}$ relative to H$_2$, a value typical of O-rich envelopes (cite: Kahane1994)"]
  - assumption: Spherical symmetry; power-law gas temperature T_g(r) = T_* (r/R_*)^-delta; 20% uniform uncertainties on CO intensities [Model: "The gas temperature as a function of radial distance is described by $$\begin{equation} T_g(r) = T_\star \left( \frac{r}{R_\star}\right)^{-\delta}, \label{eq:temp} \end{equation}$$"] [Model: "We adopted $20 \%$ uniform uncertainties for all CO lines."]
  - fitting: Grid search minimizing reduced chi-squared over mass loss rate and delta [Model: "A $\chi^2_\textrm{red}$ analysis was carried out to find the best-fit model by comparing the calculated velocity-integrated intensities with the observed intensities of multiple CO lines (see Table 3)."]
  - validation: Consistency check varying CO abundance by a factor of 2 [Model: "Additional models were run as a consistency check, varying the assumed CO abundance by a factor of 2. The resulting best-fit parameter ranges remain sufficiently narrow to support the adopted value for the CO abundance."]
- step 4: Constrain HCN formation radius (r_h) via non-LTE LVG radiative transfer modeling
  - method: Compute non-LTE LVG radiative transfer models of HCN (J=4-3 in v=0 and v2=1) with an inner abundance hole of radius r_h = 1 to 10 R_*, integrate over +/- 5 km s^-1 around V_sys, and compare with observed azimuthally averaged radial brightn… [Model: "We considered that HCN forms at some initial radial distance $r_h$ from the star, so that there is a hole or void of HCN in the central region around the star."] [Model: "The excitation and radiative transfer of HCN was solved in non-LTE adopting the large velocity gradient (LVG) formalism."]
  - parameter: r_h = 1 to 10 R_*; velocity integration range = V_sys +/- 5 km s^-1; f_0 = 3.5e-7 (R Crt) and 4.3e-7 (IK Tau); r_e = 1.0e16 cm (R Crt) and 2.0e16 cm (IK Tau); 41 rotational levels in v=0, v2=1, v2=2, v3=1, v1=1 [Model: "Following (cite: Schoier2013), the abundance distribution at the initial radius for R Crt and IK Tau are 3.5 $\times$ 10$^{-7}$ and 4.3 $\times$ 10$^{-7}$. The $r_e$ value is evaluated through the empirical expression in Eq. (3) of (cite: Schoier2013) and are 1.0 $\times$ 10$^{16}$ cm and 2.0 $\times$ 10$^{16}$ cm for…"] [Model: "We considered the first 41 rotational levels within the ground vibrational state and within the vibrationally excited states $v_2$ = 1, $v_2$ = 2, $v_3$ = 1, and $v_1$ = 1"]
  - assumption: Gaussian HCN abundance fall-off beyond r_h; optically thick free-free radio photosphere between R_* and R_mm with ionization degree 1e-5; H2 ortho-to-para ratio of 3; He/H2 = 0.17; ro-vibrational collision rates scaled down by 10,000 [Model: "we assumed an ionization degree of 10$^{-5}$ based on chemical equilibrium calculations; (cite: Agundez2020)"] [Model: "adopting an ortho-to-para ratio of 3) and from (cite: Dumouchel2010) for collisions with He, adopting a solar abundance of 0.17 relative to H$_2$ for He (cite: Asplund2009)."]
  - fitting: Comparison of radial profile morphology, radius of intensity maximum, and reduced chi-squared evaluated over 20 points between 0 and 300 mas [Results: "In this case we computed $\chi^2$ using 20 points with radii between 0 and 300 mas and obtain reduced $\chi^2$ values of 0.0774, 0.0652, 0.0326, 0.0131, 0.0043, 0.0007, 0.0015, 0.0042, 0.0083, and 0.0106 for the models with $r_h$ = 1 to 10 $R_\star$, respectively."]
  - validation: Tested sensitivity to asymmetric bright clumps by repeating azimuthal averages over angular sectors with and without the bright bump (Appendix B); verified flux filtering impact (<20%) by combining extended and compact ALMA configurations [Observations: "To evaluate how flux filtering could affect our results, we combined the data of the extended configuration with those from the more compact ones. From this exercise we concluded that the fraction of flux filtered out when using only extended configuration data below 20 %."] [Azimuthal profiles depending on the ang…: "We consider three cases in which the observed brightness is azimuthally averaged over three different regions: (1) the full image, (2) the region without the bump, and (3) the region with the bump."]
- result: Stellar luminosity L_* (R Crt) = 7000 ± not stated L_sun [Table 1: "| $L_\star$ [$L_\odot$] | 7000 $^{(e)}$ | 7900 $^{(g)}$ |"]
- result: Dust condensation radius r_c (R Crt) = 1.8e14 ± not stated cm [Table 1: "| $r_c$ [cm] | 1.8 $\times$ 10$^{14}$ $^{(e)}$ | 2.0 $\times$ 10$^{14}$ $^{(g)}$ |"]
- result: 10 um dust optical depth tau_10 (R Crt) = 0.05 ± not stated dimensionless [Table 1: "| $\tau_{10}$ | 0.05 $^{(e)}$ | 1.0 $^{(g)}$ |"]
- result: Dust condensation temperature T_d(r_c) (R Crt) = 1000 ± not stated K [Table 1: "| $T_d(r_c)$ [K] | 1000 $^{(e)}$ | 1000 $^{(g)}$ |"]
- result: Gas-to-dust mass ratio Psi (R Crt) = 890 ± not stated dimensionless [Table 1: "| $\Psi$ | 890 $^{(e)}$ | 130 $^{(g)}$ |"]
- result: Mass loss rate M_dot (R Crt) = 8.7e-7 ± not stated M_sun yr^-1 [Table 1: "| $\dot{M}$ [$M_\odot$ yr$^{-1}$] | 8.7 $\times$ 10$^{-7}$ $^{(e)}$ | 4.8 $\times$ 10$^{-6}$ $^{(g)}$ |"]
