- result: HCN J=4-3 v=0 radial intensity maximum position (R Crt) = 66 ± not stated mas [Results: "If we examine the radius at its intensity maximum, the best match is achieved at 66 mas in agreement with observations, while the models with the closest intensity maxima are those with $r_h$ = 5 $R_\star$."]
- result: HCN J=4-3 v=0 radial intensity maximum position (IK Tau) = 55 ± not stated mas [Results: "The model with $r_h$ = 5 $R_\star$ produces an intensity maximum closest to the observed value (55 mas)"]
- result: HCN formation radius r_h (R Crt) = 4-6 ± not stated R_* [abstract: "The best models locate the formation radius of HCN at 4-6 $R_\star$ in R Crt and 3-5 $R_\star$ in IK Tau."]
- result: HCN formation radius r_h (IK Tau) = 3-5 ± not stated R_* [abstract: "The best models locate the formation radius of HCN at 4-6 $R_\star$ in R Crt and 3-5 $R_\star$ in IK Tau."]
- limitation (authors): Extended configuration lacks short baselines and cannot properly describe structures larger than ~0.6'', filtering out up to 20% of the flux. [Observations: "The use of the extended configuration lacking short baselines prevents us from properly describing structures larger than $\sim$ $0.6\ensuremath{''}$."]
- limitation (authors): Gas densities in the very inner layers (r < r_c) are subject to important uncertainties. [Model: "We note that densities in these very inner layers are subject to important uncertainties."]
- limitation (authors): The radiative transfer model assumes a 1D spherical envelope, whereas the observed HCN emission is asymmetric and exhibits a bright spot/clump. [Model: "We considered an AGB star surrounded by a spherical envelope of gas and dust."]
- not stated (card): Exact pixel/cell size and image dimensions used in GILDAS mapping; Line-free channel selection and continuum subtraction procedure for the HCN line cubes; Solution intervals and SNR thresholds used in the two phase self-calibration loops; RMS noise levels achieved in the HCN J=4-3 v=0 and v2=1 channel maps or integrated intensity maps; Exact grid step sizes and ranges for DUSTY (T_c, tau_10) and …

### 2021MNRAS.504.2687N: Morpho-kinematics of the circumstellar envelope of the AGB star R Dor: a global view (2021; evolved_stars, astrochemistry; archival_reuse)
- question: Characterise the global morpho-kinematics (rotation, expansion, turbulence, absorption, south-eastern blue-shifted stream, and broad outflows) of the circumstellar envelope of the oxygen-rich AGB star R Dor from the stellar surface up to ~…
- step 3: Evaluate LTE radiative transfer and optical depth over the stellar disc
  - method: Calculate LTE unabsorbed emission epsilon and optical depth tau over 0.2 < r < 1.0 arcsec for 29SiO and SO2 and compare predicted self-absorbed emission against the observed 3-sigma upper limit over the stellar disc (R < 0.035 arcsec) at V… [Absorption over the stellar disc: "Quantitatively, we can safely place a 3-$\sigma$ upper limit of 4.2 mJy beam$^{-1}$ (10 mJy over the stellar disc) on the brightness measured at maximal absorption, $V_{z}$$\sim$$-$4 km s$^{-1}$, over the stellar disc ($R$\<0.035 arcsec)."] [Absorption over the stellar disc: "We first calculate $\varepsilon$ and $\tau$ at a temperature $T$$\sim$270 K using Relations 2 and 3 for $^{29}$SiO."]
  - parameter: Stellar disc R < 0.035 arcsec; T = 270 K; Delta V = 2.8 km s^-1 [Absorption over the stellar disc: "Namely, at $T$=270 K, $\varepsilon$=8.2 Jy arcsec$^{-2}$ and $\tau$=0.36 for $\Delta{V}$=2.8 km s$^{-1}$: the self-absorbed emission is $\sim$27 mJy over the stellar disc, nearly 3 times the upper limit placed on the observed emission."]
  - assumption: LTE, 28SiO/H2 = 5.5 10^-5, 29Si/28Si = 8%, H2 density = 1.6 10^7 (r/0.2)^-2 cm^-3 (mass-loss rate ~1.6 10^-7 M_sun yr^-1), SO2/H2 = 5 10^-6 exp(-(r/1.78)^2) [Absorption over the stellar disc: "We use a column density $N$ \[molecules cm$^{-3}$ arcsec\]=0.7 obtained by integration from $r$=0.8 to 1.0 arcsec for a $^{28}$SiO/H$_2$ abundance of 5.5 10$^{-5}$ (cite: VandeSande2018), an isotopic ratio $^{29}$Si/$^{28}$Si=8% (cite: DeBeck2018) and an H$_2$ density of the form 1.6 10$^{7}$ $(r/0.2)^{-2}$ \[molecule…"] [Absorption over the stellar disc: "The abundance relative to H$_2$ is now of the form 5 10$^{-6}\exp[-(r/1.78)^2]$ (cite: Danilovich2016; Danilovich2020), $E_{\rm{u}}$=581.92 K and $A_{\rm{ji}}$=3.45 10$^{-4}$ s$^{-1}$, but the relation $\tau$$\sim$11.8$\varepsilon/T$ still applies."]
- step 6: Test whether extended 29SiO absorption is an interferometric short-spacing artefact
  - method: Simulate visibilities with CASA SIMOBSERVE for three isotropic sky brightness models (case 1: stellar continuum disc R < 0.04 arcsec; case 2: CSE disc R < 0.3 arcsec with absorbed stellar disc; case 3: sum of cases 1 and 2) using the actua… [6.1 Absorption of the 29SiO line emissi…: "visibilities $U$ corresponding to the actual antenna configuration illustrated in Figure"] [Absorption of the 29SiO line emission b…: "Turning now to the actual $^{29}$SiO observations, we select the two frequency intervals covering $V_z$=$\pm$4 km s$^{-1}$ and define a third interval, meant to measure continuum emission, made of two sets of 9 frequency channels each (7.7 km s$^{-1}$) at the beginning and end of the frequency band."]
  - software: CASA, SIMOBSERVE
  - parameter: Case 1: 1 Jy/pixel for R < 0.04 arcsec; Case 2: 0.1 Jy/pixel for 0.04 < R < 0.3 arcsec; Case 3: Case 1 + Case 2 [Absorption of the 29SiO line emission b…: "The first model, case 1, shown in blue, represents continuum emission and is described as a uniform brightness of 1 Jy/pixel over the stellar disc ($R$\<0.04 arcsec)."] [Absorption of the 29SiO line emission b…: "The second model, case 2, in red, shows a disc of uniform brightness, arbitrarily set to 0.1 Jy/pixel, and having a radius of 0.3 arcsec, meant to represent the emission of the CSE."]
  - assumption: Isotropic sky brightness distributions [Absorption of the 29SiO line emission b…: "It assumes the isotropy of the brightness in the plane of the sky and considers three different models, each defined by a simple radial dependence of the brightness as illustrated in Figure 3a."]
  - fitting: Rayleigh noise distribution fit |U| exp(-0.5 |U|^2 / sigma^2) to visibility amplitudes [Absorption of the 29SiO line emission b…: "The noise is well described by a form \|$U$\|$\exp(-\sfrac{1}{2}|$$U$$|^2/\sigma^2)$, meaning a Gaussian distribution in each of Re($U$) and Im($U$) with a common rms value, $\sigma$, measured to be 0.62 Jy."]
  - validation: Direct comparison of raw visibility amplitudes between V_z = +4 km s^-1 and V_z = -4 km s^-1 channels where uv coverage, noise, and calibration are identical. [Absorption of the 29SiO line emission b…: "The short spacing problem is precisely the same for the $V_z$=$+$4 km s$^{-1}$ and $V_z$=$-$4 km s$^{-1}$ cases; the noise is the same; the calibration uses only observations made for this purpose and is also the same for the two channels."]
- step 7: Reconstruct 3D outflow geometry and model outer SiO self-absorption using SO, 28SiO, CO, and HCN
  - method: Deproject SO, 28SiO, CO, and HCN data cubes onto perpendicular outflow planes P1 (PA = 60 deg) and P2 (PA = 150 deg) using a radial velocity law V = V_term r / (r + r_1/2); compare observed radial flux profiles to optically thin LTE predic… [Overall picture of the morpho-kinematic…: "Using the law introduced in the preceding paragraph with $V_{\rm{term}}$=7 km s$^{-1}$ and $r_{\sfrac{1}{2}}$=30 au (0.5 arcsec) the map of the intensity projected on P1, displayed in panel b of Figure 6, gives $\varphi$$\sim$45$^\circ$."] [Absorption of SiO emission: "Finally, we model the $^{28}$SiO emission over two rings, 0.35\<$R$\<0.55 arcsec and 0.65\<$R$\<0.85 arcsec, far enough from the stellar disc for continuum emission to be negligible."]
  - parameter: V_term = 7 km s^-1, r_1/2 = 30 au (0.5 arcsec) for deprojection; rings 0.35-0.55 and 0.65-0.85 arcsec for 28SiO fit [Overall picture of the morpho-kinematic…: "Using the law introduced in the preceding paragraph with $V_{\rm{term}}$=7 km s$^{-1}$ and $r_{\sfrac{1}{2}}$=30 au (0.5 arcsec) the map of the intensity projected on P1, displayed in panel b of Figure 6, gives $\varphi$$\sim$45$^\circ$."] [Absorption of SiO emission: "Finally, we model the $^{28}$SiO emission over two rings, 0.35\<$R$\<0.55 arcsec and 0.65\<$R$\<0.85 arcsec, far enough from the stellar disc for continuum emission to be negligible."]
  - assumption: V = V_term r / (r + r_1/2); density propto r^-2; temperature T = T_0.5 r^-p; LTE radiative transfer [Overall picture of the morpho-kinematic…: "in particular for an acceleration law of the form $V$=$V_{\rm{term}}r/(r+r_{\sfrac{1}{2}})$, tending asymptotically to the terminal velocity $V_{\rm{term}}$ when $r$ tends to infinity and reaching the value $\sfrac{1}{2}V_{\rm{term}}$ for $r$=$r_{\sfrac{1}{2}}$"] [Absorption of SiO emission: "It assumes a $r^{-2}$ radial dependence of the density. The temperature is taken of the form $T$=$T_{0.5}r^{-p}$."]
  - fitting: Adjustment of r_1/2, N_0.5, line width parameters, T_0.5, and p to match 28SiO ring spectra [Absorption of SiO emission: "The parameters being adjusted are the expansion parameter $r_{\sfrac{1}{2}}$, the density $N_{0.5}$ at $r$=0.5 arcsec, the line width parameters and the temperature parameters $T_{0.5}$ and $p$."]
  - validation: Sensitivity test varying r_1/2 between 0.25 and 0.75 arcsec; comparison of smeared 29SiO to scaled 28SiO radial distributions. [Overall picture of the morpho-kinematic…: "The value of $r_{\sfrac{1}{2}}$ can be chosen anywhere between 0.25 and 0.75 arcsec (15 and 45 au) without $\varphi$ changing by more than $\pm$5$^\circ$."] [Absorption of SiO emission: "The observed increase of absorption between $^{29}$SiO and $^{28}$SiO is $\sim$5 in the red hemisphere and $\sim$10 in the blue hemisphere, well bracketing the predicted value of 7.7."]
- result: 3-sigma upper limit on 29SiO brightness at maximal absorption (V_z ~ -4 km s^-1) over stellar disc = 4.2 ± not stated mJy beam^-1 [Absorption over the stellar disc: "Quantitatively, we can safely place a 3-$\sigma$ upper limit of 4.2 mJy beam$^{-1}$ (10 mJy over the stellar disc) on the brightness measured at maximal absorption, $V_{z}$$\sim$$-$4 km s$^{-1}$, over the stellar disc ($R$\<0.035 arcsec)."]
- result: 3-sigma upper limit on 29SiO flux density at maximal absorption over stellar disc = 10 ± not stated mJy [Absorption over the stellar disc: "Quantitatively, we can safely place a 3-$\sigma$ upper limit of 4.2 mJy beam$^{-1}$ (10 mJy over the stellar disc) on the brightness measured at maximal absorption, $V_{z}$$\sim$$-$4 km s$^{-1}$, over the stellar disc ($R$\<0.035 arcsec)."]
- result: Visibility noise rms sigma for 29SiO = 0.62 ± not stated Jy [Absorption of the 29SiO line emission b…: "The noise is well described by a form \|$U$\|$\exp(-\sfrac{1}{2}|$$U$$|^2/\sigma^2)$, meaning a Gaussian distribution in each of Re($U$) and Im($U$) with a common rms value, $\sigma$, measured to be 0.62 Jy."]
- result: Position angle of common back-to-back outflow axis projection = 60 ± 5 deg [Overall picture of the morpho-kinematic…: "A very crude model of two back-to-back outflows is used to evaluate the orientation of the common outflow axis: it projects on the plane of the sky 60$^\circ$$\pm$5$^\circ$ east of north."]
- result: Angle phi of the outflow axis with the plane of the sky = 45 ± 5 deg [Overall picture of the morpho-kinematic…: "Using the law introduced in the preceding paragraph with $V_{\rm{term}}$=7 km s$^{-1}$ and $r_{\sfrac{1}{2}}$=30 au (0.5 arcsec) the map of the intensity projected on P1, displayed in panel b of Figure 6, gives $\varphi$$\sim$45$^\circ$."]
- result: Modelled 28SiO temperature T_0.5 at r = 0.5 arcsec = 315 ± not stated K [Absorption of SiO emission: "The fit requires again a much steeper radial dependence of the temperature than used in earlier works: the fits displayed in Figure 1 are for $T_{0.5}$=315 K and a 1/$r$ dependence ($p$=1) meaning $T$=158 K at $r$=1 arcsec."]
- result: Modelled 28SiO density at r = 0.5 arcsec = 1625 ± not stated molecules cm^-3 [Absorption of SiO emission: "The density at $r$=0.5 arcsec is 1625 molecules per cm$^3$, in agreement with the value found for the $^{29}$SiO fit, $\sim$40% of that given by (cite: VandeSande2018)."]
- limitation (authors): LTE assumption and morpho-kinematic models are crude and oversimplified (e.g., modelling shock/pulsation broadening as Gaussian fluctuations and assuming simple power-law radial profiles). [Absorption over the stellar disc: "While local thermal equilibrium (LTE) is not expected to provide a reliable description of the CSE, in particular in the close neighbourhood of the star, its prediction provides a useful reference with which to compare observations."]
- limitation (authors): Poor uv coverage at short baselines for the 2013.1.00166.S (28SiO, CO, HCN) data limits reliable analysis to r <= 60 au (1 arcsec) and prevents quantitative statements at larger radii. [Observations and data reduction: "Accordingly, we restrict the analysis to distances from the star not exceeding 60 au (1 arcsec)."]
- limitation (authors): The 3D inclination angle of the blue stream with the line of sight and the inclination of the rotation axis with the plane of the sky are unknown. [The blue stream: "The angle made by the stream with the line of sight is unknown."]
- not stated (card): Exact versions of CASA and GILDAS used for calibration, imaging, and simulation; Specific cleaning thresholds, loop gain, number of iterations, and masking strategy in CASA and GILDAS; Image sizes (number of pixels) and pixel sizes for the 28SiO, CO, HCN, and SO cubes; Whether primary beam correction was applied to the cubes; Optimization algorithm / fitting routine used for the custom morpho-kin…

### 2016ApJ...832...37G: ALMA Observations of Lyα Blob 1: Halo Substructure Illuminated from Within (2016; high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Do the submillimetre sources at the centre of SSA22-LAB01 and their surrounding halo substructure explain the extended Lyα emission of the blob?
- step 3: Run zoom simulation and mock observations
  - method: GIZMO zoom simulation, Powderday/Hyperion RT, SIMALMA mock ALMA, mock STIS with noise, SExtractor source detection [3.2: "To simulate the ALMA observations we use the CASA task SIMALMA, setting the simulation parameters to match the real observations"]
  - software: GIZMO, MUSIC, Powderday, yt, FSPS, Hyperion, CASA simalma, SExtractor
  - parameter: dust = 40% of metal mass; Kroupa IMF; WD01 dust, RV=3.15 [3.2: "the dust mass is assumed to be a constant fraction (40%) of the total metal mass in a given cell"]
  - validation: Compared mock and real source counts, with chance projection contamination estimated [3.3: "In a random 5 _[′′]_ radius aperture we would expect 4 _±_ 1 sources by chance"]
- step 4: Lyα radiative transfer
  - method: TLAC Monte Carlo RT with central galaxies as sources, SFR-to-Lyα relation, peeling, SMC-like dust [3.2: "We use the code TLAC (Gronke et al. 2014, 2015) with the default core skipping parameter _x_ = 3."]
  - software: TLAC
  - parameter: f_ion = 1% [3.2: "We assume that _f_ ion is 1%"]
  - validation: Compared Lyα maps with MUSE observation [3.3: "Figure 4 shows the projected Ly _α_ surface brightness maps of the simulated halo compared to the latest integral field observations from MUSE."]
- result: Fraction of centrally generated Lyα photons escaping the box = 58 ± not stated percent [3.3: "58% of centrally generated photons escape the simulation box"]
- result: Fraction of HI mass in subhalos = 77 ± not stated percent [3.3: "The majority (77%) of the H I mass"]
- result: Synthetic STIS sources vs observed = 5 vs 8 ± not stated sources [3.3: "Formally, we detect five sources at the _>_ 3 _σ_ level"]
- limitation (authors): Missing flux relative to SCUBA-2 and a lack of more compact array data. [2.1 ALMA: "Deeper observations with a slightly more compact array configuration would be beneficial to address this."]
- not stated (card): Number of SBs/execution blocks and observing dates for the ALMA data; Flux calibrator and absolute flux uncertainty; Whether archive-delivered or manually produced calibration was used, and CASA version; Feathering parameters and SCUBA-2 map scaling; SimALMA parameter values; Simulation initial conditions and code configuration; Molecular gas mass conversion details; SED template normalization de…

### 2024AJ....168..142L: Gemini Planet Imager Observations of a Resolved Low-inclination Debris Disk around HD 156623 (2024; debris_disks; multi_facility_alma_minor)
- question: Constrain the presence and location of the hidden inner edge of HD 156623’s debris disk.
- step 1: Fit disk geometry
  - method: Fit MCFOST models simultaneously to Stokes Qphi, Q and U. [Disk Morphology: "Models were fit to Stokes $\mathcal{Q}_\phi$, $\mathcal{Q}$ and $\mathcal{U}$ data simultaneously, as $\mathcal{Q}_\phi$ is the only available data with the quadrupole noise signal removed."]
  - software: MCFOST
  - parameter: Free: minimum grain size, porosity, dust mass, PA, inclination, inner radius, outer radius and critical radius. [Disk Morphology: "the minimum grain size ($a_\text{min}$), grain porosity, dust mass (within the grain size distribution used) $M_\text{d}$, disk position angle (PA), inclination (*i*), dust inner radius $R_\text{in}$, dust outer radius $R_\text{out}$, and the critical radius $R_\text{c}$"]
  - assumption: Spherical grains; Mie scattering; azimuthally symmetric, optically thin disk; 1.65 microns. [Disk Morphology: "Following the procedure in (cite: esposito2018), our models assume Mie scattering from spherical grains in an azimuthally-symmetric, optically-thin disk centered on the star, observed at 1.65 microns (the *H* band peak wavelength)."]
  - assumption: Scale height/radius = 0.055. [Disk Morphology: "The disk has a vertical structure where the scale height is a constant fraction of the radius, set to 0.055,"]
  - fitting: Parallel-tempered MCMC: three temperatures, 120 walkers each, 3500 iterations; discard 2900, retain final 600 zeroth-temperature iterations. [Disk Morphology: "We used a parallel-tempered MCMC with three temperatures, each with 120 walkers."] [Disk Morphology: "Walkers were initialized randomly from a uniform distribution and then simulated for 3500 iterations."]
  - validation: Inspect posterior corner plot; use maximum likelihood or 99.7% bounds for strongly non-Gaussian posteriors. [Disk Morphology: "A corner plot of posteriors from the MCMC is shown in Figure [2](#mcfost-corner)."] [Disk Morphology: "parameters with strongly non-Gaussian posteriors are reported as either maximum likelihood values or 99.7% confidence upper/lower limits."]
- step 3: Measure scattering phase functions
  - method: Measure brightness at multiple radii, correct for surface density and r², average radii, and split East/West halves. [Observed Polarized Scattering Phase Fun…: "SPFs were instead determined by taking measurements of $B_\text{obs}(r,\theta)$ at multiple radii around the disk, then averaging over the varying radii."] [Observed Polarized Scattering Phase Fun…: "f(\theta) \sim \frac{B_\text{obs}(r,\theta)}{\rho(r)} \cdot r^2"]
  - parameter: 15–78 AU; surface-density proxy from average best-fit radial brightness profile, peak normalized to 1. [Observed Polarized Scattering Phase Fun…: "this range is 15 AU (at the FPM limit) to 78 AU."] [Observed Polarized Scattering Phase Fun…: "The disk surface density, $\rho(r)$, was approximated using the average best fit radial brightness profile where the peak surface brightness was normalized to 1."]
  - assumption: Azimuthal symmetry, optical thinness and MCFOST morphology; scattering independent of azimuthal angle. [Observed Polarized Scattering Phase Fun…: "we take the disk to be azimuthally symmetric (but radially varying, related to the observed surface brightness) and optically thin, with the previously-described morphological parameters inferred via `MCFOST`."] [Observed Polarized Scattering Phase Fun…: "We also assume that scattering is independent of azimuthal angle"]
  - fitting: Fit Henyey-Greenstein functions after dividing polarized SPFs by a Rayleigh polarization curve. [Observed Polarized Scattering Phase Fun…: "We additionally fit a Henyey-Greenstein SPF (cite: henyey1941diffuse) to each of our observed scattering SPFs divided by a Rayleigh polarization curve (approximating the total intensity SPF),"]
  - validation: Propagate annular pixel variance; assess East/West HG fits. [Observed Polarized Scattering Phase Fun…: "Errors were derived by the variance of pixels in each annulus, similar to the procedure for the radial brightness profiles, and propagated appropriately through the calculation."] [Observed Polarized Scattering Phase Fun…: "For the Eastern half of the disk, the HG function is a poor fit, even with the best fit value $g$ = 0.33;"]
- step 7: Constrain inner radius with radiative transfer
  - method: Fit spatially resolved, azimuthally symmetric MCFOST SED models. [Thermal Emission: "To constrain the inner radius, we then compute the SED for a spatially resolved, azimuthally symmetric disk using `MCFOST`."]
  - software: MCFOST, emcee
  - parameter: Free rin, amin and Md; uniform priors, 0 < rin < 15 and -2 < log amin < 1. [Thermal Emission: "leaving only $r_\text{in}$, $a_\text{min}$, and $M_\text{d}$ as free parameters."] [Thermal Emission: "with bounds on the priors for $r_{\rm in}$ (0$< r_{\rm in} <$15) and log$a_{\rm min}$ (-2$< {\rm log}a_{\rm min} <$1)."]
  - assumption: Kurucz 9000 K atmosphere; same grain and disk assumptions as scattered-light model. [Thermal Emission: "We set up this resolved model using a Kurucz 9000 K model atmosphere (cite: castelli2004new) and the same parameters and assumptions (e.g. Mie scattering, dust grain size and composition) as the scattered-light model"]
  - fitting: emcee affine invariant ensemble: 16 walkers, uniform initialization and priors, 1500 iterations, discard 100 and retain 1400. [Thermal Emission: "We explore the posterior for those parameters again with a (cite: goodman2010ensemble) affine invariant MCMC ensemble sampler using the `emcee` package with 16 walkers and uniform priors,"] [Thermal Emission: "Walkers were initialized randomly from a uniform distribution and then simulated for 1500 iterations."]
  - validation: Compare model with photometry and posterior corner plot; reduced χ² = 1.11, with AKARI and ALMA residuals. [Thermal Emission: "The maximum likelihood model SED is shown compared to available photometry in Figure [9](#fig:mcfost-sed), and a corner plot of the approximate posteriors is shown in Figure [10](#fig:mcfost-sed-corner)."] [Thermal Emission: "This best-fit MCFOST SED model has $\chi_\nu^2$ = 1.11, similar to the simpler models above."]
- result: Disk position angle = 100.9 ± +1.6/-2.2 deg [Disk Morphology: "| PA (deg) | 102.081 | $100.9^{+1.6}_{-2.2}$ | 100.9$^{+1.9}_{-2.2}$ |"]
- result: Disk inclination = 36.6 ± +2.1/-9.9 deg [Disk Morphology: "| Inclination (deg) | 38.978 | $36.6^{+2.1}_{-9.9}$ | 34.9$^{+3.6}_{-9.5}$ |"]
- result: Scattered-light inner-radius upper limit = <26.6 ± 99.7% confidence AU [Abstract: "We find that HD 156623’s debris disk inner radius is constrained to less than 26.6 AU from scattered light imagery and less than 13.4 AU from SED modeling at a 99.7% confidence interval,"]
- result: Scattered-light outer-radius lower limit = >139.2 ± 99.7% confidence AU [Disk Morphology: "| $R_\text{out}$(AU) | 184.482 | $>$ 139.2 | $>$ 139.3 (99.7% confidence) |"]
- result: Critical radius = 65.2 ± +1.3/-2.3 AU [Disk Morphology: "| $R_\text{c}$(AU) | 67.113 | $65.2^{+1.3}_{-2.3}$ | 64.4$\pm$1.8 |"]
- result: Scattered-light maximum-likelihood minimum grain size = 1.13 ± not stated μm [Discussion: "`MCFOST` scattered-light image modeling reports the maximum likelihood value for $a_{min}$ as 1.13 $\mu$m,"]
- result: Eastern HG asymmetry parameter = 0.33 ± not stated dimensionless [Observed Polarized Scattering Phase Fun…: "For the Eastern half of the disk, the HG function is a poor fit, even with the best fit value $g$ = 0.33;"]
- result: Western HG asymmetry parameter = 0.45 ± not stated dimensionless [Observed Polarized Scattering Phase Fun…: "however, the HG function with $g$ = 0.45 is a reasonable approximation for the Western half."]
- result: SED maximum-likelihood inner radius = 2.37 ± not stated AU [Thermal Emission: "SED modeling, on the other hand, returns a maximum likelihood value of 2.37 AU"]
- result: SED inner-radius upper limit = <13.4 ± 99.7% confidence AU [Thermal Emission: "resulting in an upper limit of $<$13.4 AU at 99.7% confidence ($<$8.85 AU at 95% confidence)."]
- result: SED inner-radius upper limit = <8.85 ± 95% confidence AU [Thermal Emission: "resulting in an upper limit of $<$13.4 AU at 99.7% confidence ($<$8.85 AU at 95% confidence)."]
- result: SED minimum grain-size upper limit = <0.21 ± not stated μm [Thermal Emission: "The minimum grain size $a_\text{min}$ is much smaller as suggested by SED modeling, $<$0.21 $\mu$m at compared to $\sim$1 $\mu$m from scattered-light image modeling."]
- result: MCFOST SED reduced chi squared = 1.11 ± not stated dimensionless [Thermal Emission: "This best-fit MCFOST SED model has $\chi_\nu^2$ = 1.11, similar to the simpler models above."]
- result: Best-fit midplane temperature at inner radius = ∼500 ± not stated K [Thermal Emission: "The maximum temperatures calculated by `MCFOST` at $r_\text{in}$ and $R_\text{c}$ at the disk midplane in the best fit model are $\sim$500 K and 355 K respectively."]
- result: Best-fit midplane temperature at critical radius = 355 ± not stated K [Thermal Emission: "The maximum temperatures calculated by `MCFOST` at $r_\text{in}$ and $R_\text{c}$ at the disk midplane in the best fit model are $\sim$500 K and 355 K respectively."]
- limitation (authors): Image-derived inner radius limited by focal-plane mask. [Thermal Emission: "Scattered-light image modeling is limited by the focal-plane mask in its determination of $r_\text{in}$,"]
- limitation (authors): SED inner radius and minimum grain size reach prior boundaries. [Thermal Emission: "SED modeling, on the other hand, returns a maximum likelihood value of 2.37 AU and is limited by the prior bound at zero,"]
- limitation (authors): Dust mass functions mainly as a brightness scaling factor. [Disk Morphology: "Additionally, we do not expect the dust mass $M_\text{d}$ to be physically meaningful; instead, it essentially functions as a scaling factor for the global surface brightness of the disk."]
- not stated (card): ALMA calibration, imaging, aperture and flux-extraction settings to independently reproduce the imported continuum measurement.; Software versions actually used.; Image-fitting likelihood, pixel noise covariance, fit masks and numerical prior bounds.; Full MCFOST input files, grain size-distribution limits and exponent, optical constants, grid and photon settings.; SED dust-mass prior bounds and …

### 2015ApJ...812...27A: ALMA Observations of the Transition from Infall Motion to Keplerian Rotation around the Late-phase Protostar TMC-1A (2015; star_formation, protoplanetary_disks; observational_alma_primary)
- question: How does infall motion in the envelope of the Class I protostar TMC-1A transition to Keplerian rotation in the disk, and what does this imply for Keplerian disk formation?
- step 3: Fit Keplerian disk model to high-velocity C18O channel maps
  - method: 3D LTE radiative transfer disk model, MCMC chi2 minimisation on pixels >3 sigma with |dV|>2.4 km/s [4.3 Rotating Disk Models: "We use Markov Chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) method to find the minimum $\chi^{2}$ efficiently."]
  - software: MCMC (unnamed code of Ohashi et al. 2014)
  - parameter: fixed i=65 deg, Rin=0.1 AU, Rout=200 AU; 7 free parameters [Table 2: "fixed | $i$ | $R_{\rm in}$ | $R_{\rm out}$"]
  - assumption: LTE, X(C18O)=3.0e-7, zero turbulence, mean molecular weight 2.37 mH, position/PA from continuum, Vsys 6.4 [4.3 Rotating Disk Models: "we assume Local Thermodynamic Equilibrium (LTE) and a typical abundance of C$^{18}$O relative to H$_{2}$, $X($C$^{18}$O$)=3.0\times 10^{-7}$"]
  - fitting: reduced chi2 minimisation; uncertainties from range where chi2 < min+1 [4.3 Rotating Disk Models: "The uncertainty of each parameter is defined by the range of the parameter where the reduced $\chi^{2}$ is below the minimum plus one"]
  - validation: reduced chi2=0.72; inclination scan 5-85 deg [4.3 Rotating Disk Models: "Reduced $\chi^{2}=0.72$"]
- step 4: Model infalling envelope plus disk and compare with PV diagrams
  - method: FE models (A-F) with Vr = alpha x free-fall, varied v_out, R_kep, alpha, i, compared with major and minor axis PV diagrams; simobserve for spatial filtering [5.2 Nature of the infall motion: "Four parameters of the model mentioned above, $v_{\rm out}$, $R_{\rm kep}$, $\alpha$, and the inclination angle of the disk $i$ are changed to reproduce the observed PV diagrams."]
  - software: CASA simobserve
  - parameter: best model A: (v_out, R_kep, alpha, i)=(0.85, 100 AU, 0.3, 65 deg) [5.2 Nature of the infall motion: "$(v_{\rm out},\ R_{\rm kep},\ \alpha,\ i)=(0.85,\ 100\ {\rm AU},\ 0.3,\ 65^{\circ})$"]
  - assumption: flattened envelope to 900 AU; envelope surface density beyond 200 AU reduced by factor 2 [5.2 Nature of the infall motion: "the surface density of the envelope at radii more than 200 AU is reduced by a factor of two"]
  - fitting: by-eye comparison of models, no formal fit [Discussion, Nature of the infall motion: "such fittings are not performed here. This is because models including the infalling envelope must be compared with the channel maps not only at higher velocities, but also at lower velocities"]
  - validation: spherical envelope (SE) models compared in Appendix; q=-0.5 tested [5.2 Nature of the infall motion: "It is found that models with a spherical envelope cannot explain the observations better than FE models."]
- result: Disk model M*sin^2 i = 0.56 ± ±0.05 Msun [Table 2: "$0.56^{+0.05}_{-0.05}\ M_{\odot}$"]
- result: Disk model rotation index v = 0.53 ± ±0.05 dimensionless [Table 2: "$0.53^{+0.05}_{-0.05}$"]
- result: Inclination-corrected dynamical mass = 0.68 ± not stated Msun [5.2 Nature of the infall motion: "the inclination-corrected central stellar mass is calculated at $M_{*}=0.68\ M_{\odot}$"]
- result: Infall velocity factor alpha = 0.3 ± not stated dimensionless [5.2 Nature of the infall motion: "$(v_{\rm out},\ R_{\rm kep},\ \alpha,\ i)=(0.85,\ 100\ {\rm AU},\ 0.3,\ 65^{\circ})$"]
- result: Mass infall rate = (1.5-3.0)e-6 at R=100-200 AU ± not stated Msun/yr [5.2 Nature of the infall motion: "$\dot{M}=2\pi RV_{r}\Sigma =(1.5-3.0)\times 10^{-6}\ M_{\odot}\,{\rm yr}^{-1}\ (R=100-200\ {\rm AU})$"]
- result: Keplerian disk mass (R_kep=100 AU) = 2.5e-3 ± not stated Msun [5.2 Nature of the infall motion: "$M_{\rm disk}=\int \Sigma 2\pi RdR=2.5\times 10^{-3}\ M_{\odot}$"]
- limitation (authors): Inclination poorly constrained by the disk model; mass estimates are uncertain by optical depth, temperature, abundance [4.3 Rotating Disk Models: "it is difficult to give a constraint on the inclination angle in this model fitting"]
- limitation (authors): PV mean-position method underestimates break radius at limited resolution [4.2 Rotation Profile: "may systematically underestimate $R_{b}$ when the spatial resolution is not high enough"]
- not stated (card): MCMC details for disk model fit (sampler, priors, steps, convergence); CASA version and calibration pipeline/script version; CLEAN iterations/threshold, cell and image size; Line-free channel ranges for continuum; Exact flux-correction handling beyond the 0.85 factor; Mask definitions and uncertainty computation for mean positions; Disk code is from Ohashi et al. 2014, not available here; Continu…

### 2020NatAs...4..861C: Unusually high CO abundance of the first active interstellar comet (2020; solar_system, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Parent volatile composition of 2I/Borisov and implications for its natal environment.
- step 5: Construct molecular excitation models
  - method: Modified cometary LIME: three-dimensional NLTE radiative transfer with molecular and electron collisions and solar pumping; use LTE for CO. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "using a modified version of the LIne Modeling Engine (LIME)(cite: bri10), adapted for cometary comae as described by Cordiner et al.(cite: cor19)."] [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "This three-dimensional, non-local-thermodynamic-equilibrium (NLTE) radiative transfer model incorporates a calculation of the rotational energy level populations resulting from collisions with gas-phase molecules and electrons, as well as pumping by solar radiation."]
  - software: LIME
  - parameter: Scale radiative pumping and photodissociation rates to r_H = 2.01 au. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "Radiative pumping and photodissociation rates(cite: hue15) were appropriately scaled for the comet’s heliocentric distance ($r_H=2.01$ au)."]
  - assumption: HCN-H2O and HCN-CO collisions represented by H2 collisional rates. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "Collisional rates for HCN with H$_2$O and CO were taken from the H$_2$ collisional rates in the Leiden Atomic and Molecular Database (cite: sch05)."]
  - assumption: Nominal electron density scaling x_ne = 1.0; kinetic temperature 50 K. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "Our nominal model assumed a standard electron density scaling factor of $x_{n_e}=1.0$ and kinetic temperature $T_{kin}=$ 50 K to obtain the results given in Table 1"]
  - validation: Scaling H2 collision rates by 0.1-10 changed HCN production rates by only 2%. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "The impact of this assumption was tested by scaling the H$_2$ collision rates by a factor of 0.1-10, but the resulting HCN production rates changed by only 2%."]
- step 6: Account for interferometric filtering
  - method: Process three-dimensional synthetic coma images through simobserve; clean and image synthetic visibilities with the science-image parameters. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "Flux losses due to the filtering of large-scale coma structures by the interferometer were simulated by processing the (three-dimensional) synthetic coma images using the CASA `simobserve` task"] [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "The resulting synthetic visibilities were cleaned and imaged with the same parameters used for the observed cometary images."]
  - software: CASA, simobserve
  - parameter: Same hour angle, observation duration and array configuration as the science observations. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "with the same hour angle, observation duration and array configuration parameters used for our science observations."]
- step 7: Fit molecular production and outflow
  - method: Fit extracted spectra with nonlinear least squares; start with isotropic uniform expansion, refit HCN at the CO velocity, and test a conical HCN jet. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "The observed spectra were modeled with a nonlinear least-squares fitting procedure"] [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "The molecules of interest (X) were initially modeled assuming an isotropic, uniformly expanding coma, with outflow velocity ($v_{out}$) and production rate $Q({\rm X})$ allowed to vary as free parameters."]
  - software: LIME
  - parameter: Refit HCN with v_out fixed at 0.47 km s^-1. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "so this fit was re-done, fixing the outflow velocity at the best-fitting value for CO ($v_{out}=0.47$ km s$^{-1}$"]
  - parameter: Jet axis perpendicular to the line of sight; all outgassing in a cone; v_out = 0.47 km s^-1. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "with $v_{out}=0.47$ km s$^{-1}$, and assuming all outgassing was confined to a conical region emanating from the nucleus, with its axis perpendicular to the line of sight"]
  - assumption: CO and HCN well mixed; constant kinetic temperature 50 K. [Unusually High CO Abundance of the Firs…: "assuming CO and HCN were well mixed, with a constant kinetic temperature of $T_{kin}=50$ K."]
  - assumption: HCN optically thin; its derived production rate scales linearly with assumed outflow velocity. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "The HCN emission is optically thin, so the derived production rate scales linearly with the assumed outflow velocity."]
  - fitting: Nonlinear least squares; initially free v_out and Q(X); assess reduced chi-squared. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "The observed spectra were modeled with a nonlinear least-squares fitting procedure"] [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "with outflow velocity ($v_{out}$) and production rate $Q({\rm X})$ allowed to vary as free parameters."]
  - validation: CO interferometric χr² = 1.03; HCN χr² changes 1.07 to 1.13 when velocity is fixed; autocorrelation CO production agrees within errors. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "resulting in $\chi_r^2=1.03$ for our best fit to the CO interferometric data (Figure 2)."] [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "This led to only a minor degradation of the HCN $\chi_r^2$ value, from 1.07 to 1.13"]
- step 8: Test excitation uncertainties
  - method: Vary kinetic temperature and electron density scaling; assess changes in molecular production and CO/HCN. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "Modeling was carried out for a range of coma kinetic temperatures $T_{kin}=20$–70 K"] [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "However, in the case of $x_{n_e}=0.2$, the HCN production rate is more sensitive to the value of $T_{kin}$"]
  - software: LIME
  - parameter: T_kin = 20–70 K; nominal x_ne = 1.0; low-electron case x_ne = 0.2. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "For $x_{n_e}=1.0$, the CO and HCN production rates are relatively insensitive to the assumed kinetic temperature, changing by less than 8% across the range $T_{kin}=$ 20–70 K."] [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "However, in the case of $x_{n_e}=0.2$, the HCN production rate is more sensitive to the value of $T_{kin}$"]
  - assumption: Temperature range based on prior radio and infrared comet observations at 1.7-2.4 au. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "based on the temperatures previously observed at radio and infrared wavelengths for comets in the distance range 1.7-2.4 au (Supplementary Table 1)."]
  - validation: At nominal electron scaling, production rates change by less than 8%; even the low-temperature, low-electron case remains CO-rich. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "For $x_{n_e}=1.0$, the CO and HCN production rates are relatively insensitive to the assumed kinetic temperature, changing by less than 8% across the range $T_{kin}=$ 20–70 K."] [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "2I/Borisov’s CO/HCN ratio could plausibly be as low as $370\pm80$, but this is still higher than the range of values (1.25–238) observed in previous Solar System comets"]
- step 9: Test hemispheric CO outgassing
  - method: Fit the CO autocorrelation spectrum with sunward and anti-sunward hemispheres and compare against isotropic outgassing. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "Assuming hemispheric asymmetry along the Sun-comet vector"] [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "Statistically, the 2-component, asymmetric model is therefore favoured."]
  - software: LIME
  - parameter: Sunward and anti-sunward expansion velocities and production-rate asymmetry factor. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "an expansion velocity of $v_{out}(1)=0.49$ km s$^{-1}$ in the sunward hemisphere, and $v_{out}(2)=0.26$ km s$^{-1}$ in the anti-sunward hemisphere, with an asymmetry factor (ratio of sunward to anti-sunward production rates) of 3.3."]
  - assumption: Hemispheric asymmetry along the Sun-comet vector; CO J=3-2 optically thin; T_kin = 50 K. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "Assuming hemispheric asymmetry along the Sun-comet vector"] [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "Since the CO $J=3-2$ emission is optically thin"]
  - fitting: Compare reduced chi-squared and probability P for isotropic and asymmetric models. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "The isotropic model has a best-ftting $\chi^2_r=1.21$ (corresponding to $P = 0.02$"] [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "whereas the asymmetric model has $\chi^2_r=1.01$ and $P = 0.45$."]
  - validation: Asymmetric model favoured; production rate unchanged from the isotropic model because CO emission is optically thin. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "Statistically, the 2-component, asymmetric model is therefore favoured."] [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "Since the CO $J=3-2$ emission is optically thin, the production rate for our asymmetric model is the same as that obtained using the standard, isotropic model"]
- result: CO production rate from interferometric data = 4.4×10^26 ± ±0.7×10^26 mol. s^-1 [Table 1: "| CO | $J=3-2$ | 345.796 | $8.6\pm1.5$ | $(4.4\pm0.7)\times10^{26}$ |"]
- result: HCN production rate from interferometric data = 7.0×10^23 ± ±1.1×10^23 mol. s^-1 [Table 1: "| HCN | $J=4-3$ | 354.505 | $6.5\pm1.2$ | $(7.0\pm1.1)\times10^{23}$ |"]
- result: CO production rate from autocorrelation data = 5.0×10^26 ± ±0.5×10^26 s^-1 [Unusually High CO Abundance of the Firs…: "Modeling the autocorrelation data assuming isotropic outgassing from the nucleus results in a CO production rate of $(5.0\pm0.5)\times10^{26}$ s$^{-1}$ (assuming an amplitude calibration error of $\pm10$%)."]
- result: CO spectral FWHM = 1.1 ± ±0.1 km s^-1 [Unusually High CO Abundance of the Firs…: "For CO, the spectral line profile (with FWHM = $1.1\pm0.1$ km s$^{-1}$)"]
- result: HCN spectral FWHM = 0.57 ± ±0.09 km s^-1 [Unusually High CO Abundance of the Firs…: "HCN exhibits a narrower spectral line shape (with FWHM = $0.57\pm0.09$ km s$^{-1}$)"]
- result: CO isotropic outflow velocity from interferometric data = 0.47 ± ±0.04 km s^-1 [Unusually High CO Abundance of the Firs…: "with best-fitting outflow velocity $v_{out}=0.47\pm0.04$ km s$^{-1}$."]
- result: CO isotropic outflow velocity from autocorrelation data = 0.45 ± ±0.03 km s^-1 [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "the CO autocorrelation data also gave $v_{out}=0.45\pm0.03$ km s$^{-1}$"]
- result: HCN initial free-fit outflow velocity = 0.23 ± ±0.06 km s^-1 [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "The best fit for HCN resulted in a surprisingly small value of $v_{out}=0.23\pm0.06$ km s$^{-1}$"]
- result: HCN conical jet opening angle = 96 ± not stated degrees [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "a best-fitting cone opening angle of $96^{\circ}$."]
- result: HCN production increase for the conical jet model = 13 ± not stated % [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "This resulted in a $13$% increase in the derived HCN production rate, which is within the range of our observational uncertainties."]
- result: HCN production at T_kin = 20 K and x_ne = 0.2 = 1.2×10^24 ± not stated s^-1 [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "resulting in a value of $Q({\rm HCN})=1.2\times10^{24}$ s$^{-1}$ at 20 K"]
- result: CO production at T_kin = 20 K = 4.6×10^26 ± not stated s^-1 [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "For comparison, we found $Q({\rm CO})=4.6\times10^{26}$ s$^{-1}$ at $T_{kin}=20$ K."]
- result: CO/HCN in the low-temperature, low-electron-collision regime = 370 ± ±80 dimensionless [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "Consequently, in the regime of very low kinetic temperature and electron collision rates, 2I/Borisov’s CO/HCN ratio could plausibly be as low as $370\pm80$"]
- result: Sunward CO expansion velocity = 0.49 ± not stated km s^-1 [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "an expansion velocity of $v_{out}(1)=0.49$ km s$^{-1}$ in the sunward hemisphere"]
- result: Anti-sunward CO expansion velocity = 0.26 ± not stated km s^-1 [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "$v_{out}(2)=0.26$ km s$^{-1}$ in the anti-sunward hemisphere"]
- result: Sunward/anti-sunward CO production-rate asymmetry = 3.3 ± not stated dimensionless [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "an asymmetry factor (ratio of sunward to anti-sunward production rates) of 3.3."]
- result: Isotropic CO autocorrelation model reduced chi-squared = 1.21 ± not stated dimensionless [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "The isotropic model has a best-ftting $\chi^2_r=1.21$"]
- result: Isotropic CO autocorrelation model P = 0.02 ± not stated dimensionless [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "The isotropic model has a best-ftting $\chi^2_r=1.21$ (corresponding to $P = 0.02$"]
- result: Asymmetric CO autocorrelation model reduced chi-squared = 1.01 ± not stated dimensionless [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "whereas the asymmetric model has $\chi^2_r=1.01$ and $P = 0.45$."]
- result: Asymmetric CO autocorrelation model P = 0.45 ± not stated dimensionless [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "whereas the asymmetric model has $\chi^2_r=1.01$ and $P = 0.45$."]
- limitation (authors): Detailed coma morphology largely hidden by noise. [Unusually High CO Abundance of the Firs…: "Our HCN and CO detections are robust, but the detailed coma morphology in Figure 1 is largely hidden by noise"]
- limitation (authors): HCN signal-to-noise cannot distinguish a jet from isotropic outgassing. [Unusually High CO Abundance of the Firs…: "However, the signal-to-noise is too low to conclusively distinguish this scenario from isotropic outgassing."]
- limitation (authors): Interferometric observations filter emission on angular scales ≳10''. [Unusually High CO Abundance of the Firs…: "because they include flux from angular scales $\gtrsim10''$, which was filtered out by the interferometer due to a lack of very short baselines in the telescope array."]
- not stated (card): The paper's program identifier is printed as 2019.01008.T; an unambiguous archive identifier is needed.; Spectral-window bandwidths, channel selections, and the construction of the combined continuum image.; Image cell size, image dimensions, exact spectral regridding, and primary-beam correction treatment.; Exact line-free channels, polynomial orders, and interpolation interval for the autocorre…

### 2021ApJ...909...84U: Internal Structure of Molecular Gas in a Main-sequence Galaxy With a UV Clump at z = 1.45 (2021; high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Internal distribution and properties of molecular gas (CO(2-1), CO(5-4), R52 ratio, rotation, gas fraction profile) in a massive main-sequence galaxy with a UV clump at z = 1.45, compared with the stellar distribution
- step 5: Test significance of CO(5-4) peak offset and velocity shift; align HST to ALMA
  - method: Simulate CO(5-4) observations with simobserve of a Gaussian mimicking the CO(5-4) distribution and line profile to get 1-sigma uncertainties on peak position and velocity; shift HST astrometry assuming the J125 peak coincides with the CO(2… [4.1.2 Spatial Distribution of R52: "We performed simulations of CO(5-4) observation with CASA task `simobserve` assuming a gaussian distribution mimicking the CO(5-4) distribution and line profile."] [4.1.2 Spatial Distribution of R52: "We assume the peak of $J_{125}$ image coincides with the peak of the CO(2-1) 0th moment map"]
  - software: CASA simobserve
  - parameter: HST offset (dRA, dDec) = (+0.058, -0.198) arcsec [4.1.2 Spatial Distribution of R52: "The offset is $(\Delta{\rm R.A.}, \Delta{\rm Dec.}) = (+0.\ensuremath{''}058, -0.\ensuremath{''}198)$."]
  - assumption: J125 peak coincides with CO(2-1) peak because CO(2-1) has a symmetric profile and high S/N [4.1.2 Spatial Distribution of R52: "because the CO(2-1) shows a good symmetry in the line profile and S/N of the image is high"]
  - validation: Offset 0.15 arcsec = ~3.8 sigma and velocity difference ~150 km/s = ~5 sigma, so judged real [4.1.2 Spatial Distribution of R52: "Thus the offset and the peak velocity difference are considered to be real."]
- step 6: Fit a rotating disk to the CO(2-1) cube
  - method: GalPaK3D disk model (exponential flux profile n=1, hyperbolic tangent rotation curve) convolved with PSF and LSF fit to the CO(2-1) channel cube; uncertainties from 100 simulated cubes with random noise seeds, combined in quadrature with s… [4.1.3 Fitting a Rotating Disk Model: "We fit a disk model whose radial flux profile is exponential (Sérsic index $n=1$) and radial rotational velocity profile is hyperbolic tangent to the CO(2-1) channel map."] [4.1.3 Fitting a Rotating Disk Model: "In order to estimate the uncertainties on the best-fit parameters, we created 100 cleaned cubes by simulating observations of the best-fit disk model with `simobserve` by changing the noise seeds randomly."]
  - software: GalPaK3D, CASA simobserve
  - parameter: PSF = synthesized beam 0.61 x 0.53 arcsec, PA = 79 deg; LSF = 25 km/s channel [4.1.3 Fitting a Rotating Disk Model: "PSF and LSF correspond to the synthesized beam ($0.\ensuremath{''}61 \times 0.\ensuremath{''}53$, ${\rm PA} = 79\ensuremath{^\circ}$) and the channel bin (velocity width is $25 \ \rm km \, s^{-1}$), respectively."]
  - parameter: Free parameters: central position, central frequency, total flux, r1/2, inclination, PA, turnover radius, Vmax, constant sigma0 [4.1.3 Fitting a Rotating Disk Model: "The fitting parameters are central position, central frequency, total flux, half-light radius ($r_{1/2}$), inclination, position angle, turnover radius ($r_{t}$)"]
  - assumption: Velocity dispersion constant over the disk; sigma0 poorly constrained because of beam smearing [4.1.3 Fitting a Rotating Disk Model: "Due to the large beam size, the observed line width seems to be largely affected by beam smearing effect, and it is hard to constrain the intrinsic velocity dispersion well."]
  - fitting: Disk model convolved with 2D PSF and 1D LSF fit to the data cube; sampler, priors and likelihood not stated [4.1.3 Fitting a Rotating Disk Model: "by fitting a disk model convolved with two-dimensional PSF and one-dimensional line spread function (LSF) to a given data cube"]
  - validation: Residual map and line profile show the disk model represents the data; fit with fixed sigma0 ~ 50 km/s reproduces the line profile without changing r1/2 [4.1.3 Fitting a Rotating Disk Model: "The residuals demonstrate that the CO(2-1) distribution of the galaxy is well represented by the rotating disk model."] [4.1.3 Fitting a Rotating Disk Model: "In fact, the observed line profile is well reproduced without changing other output parameters such as $r_{1/2}$, even if we fit a disk model with a fixed $\sigma_{0} \sim 50 \ \rm km \, s^{-1}$"]
- result: Offset between CO(5-4) and CO(2-1) peaks = ~0.15 ± 1-sigma position uncertainty ~0.04 arcsec (~3.8 sigma) arcsec [4.1.2 Spatial Distribution of R52: "observed offset between the peak positions of CO(5-4) and CO(2-1) is $\sim 0.15 \ \rm arcsec$ which corresponds to $\sim 3.8 \sigma$."]
- result: GalPaK3D total CO(2-1) flux = 1.10 ± 0.05 Jy km/s [4.1.3 Fitting a Rotating Disk Model: "The best-fit flux ($1.10 \pm 0.05 \ \rm Jy \, km \, s^{-1}$)"]
- result: CO(2-1) half-light radius r1/2 (GalPaK3D) = 2.30 ± 0.12 kpc [4.1.3 Fitting a Rotating Disk Model: "The best-fit $r_{1/2} = 2.30 \pm 0.12 \ \rm kpc$"]
- result: Disk inclination (GalPaK3D) = 47.6 ± 6.5 deg [Table 3: "$47.6 \pm 6.5$"]
- result: Disk position angle (GalPaK3D) = 42.8 ± 2.0 deg [Table 3: "$42.8 \pm 2.0$"]
- result: Turnover radius r_t (GalPaK3D) = 0.48 ± 0.22 kpc [Table 3: "$0.48 \pm 0.22$"]
- result: Inclination-corrected maximum rotational velocity Vmax = 350 ± 30 km/s [4.1.3 Fitting a Rotating Disk Model: "The best-fit $V_{\rm max}$ and $\sigma_0$ are $350 \pm 30 \ \rm km \, s^{-1}$ and $2.7 \pm 8.7 \ \rm km \, s^{-1}$, respectively."]
- result: Velocity dispersion sigma0 (GalPaK3D, judged unreliable) = 2.7 ± 8.7 km/s [4.1.3 Fitting a Rotating Disk Model: "The best-fit $V_{\rm max}$ and $\sigma_0$ are $350 \pm 30 \ \rm km \, s^{-1}$ and $2.7 \pm 8.7 \ \rm km \, s^{-1}$, respectively."]
- limitation (authors): Best-fit velocity dispersion is unreliable; beam smearing from the large beam [4.1.3 Fitting a Rotating Disk Model: "The value is considered not to be reliable."]
- limitation (authors): Stellar surface density within ~1 kpc is poorly recovered because the HST PSF HWHM is comparable to that scale [4.2 Stellar Distribution: "it is possible that the profile is not sufficiently recovered within this scale."]
- limitation (authors): CO(5-4) flux differs from the earlier Seko16 measurement because of velocity width and data reduction differences [4.1.1 Total Flux and Molecular Gas Mass: "The CO(5-4) flux value is different from that derived by (cite: Seko16) because of the differences of the adopted velocity width and the data reduction."]
- not stated (card): Line-free channels used for uvcontsub in the CO(5-4) data, and whether any continuum subtraction was done for CO(2-1); Image size, cell size, tclean niter/threshold and the clean mask beyond '3 sigma'; Primary-beam correction and flux-scale handling for the two datasets, and the CASA 4.2 calibration choices for CO(5-4) (only in Seko16); Velocity range and masking used to build the CO(2-1) and CO(…

### 2020ApJ...896...43W: Arp 220: New Observational Insights into the Structure and Kinematics of the Nuclear Molecular Disks and Surrounding Gas (2020; nearby_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the morphology, kinematics, outflows and physical conditions (temperature, optical depth, mass) of the Arp 220 nuclear disks and extended gas, as probed by CO, 13CO, HCN and SiO and non-LTE LIME models?
- step 3: Outflow masses and mass loss rates
  - method: Moment 0 of high-velocity red/blue gas; L'CO from Solomon 1992 equation; scale 3-2 to 1-0 using nuclear luminosity ratio; mass with alpha=0.8; age = corrected size / corrected velocity [5 outflow: "an age is calculated by dividing the corrected size by the corrected velocity"]
  - software: CASA
  - assumption: CO-to-H2 factor 0.8 Msun (K km/s pc^2)^-1; inclination from LIME models [5 outflow: "using the conversion factor 0.8 M$_\odot$ (K km/s pc$^2$)$^{-1}$"]
  - fitting: Outflow sizes fit with CASA [5 outflow: "The sizes of the outflow were fit using CASA and are shown in Table [2](#out_table)."]
- step 4: Non-LTE LIME disk modeling of nuclei
  - method: Exponential disk density profile with uniform gas/dust temperature and turbulence, CO/H2 = 2e-4; model images convolved with beam, imcontsub, spectra compared at half-beam increments; parameters adjusted by eye [6 LIME modeling: "The spectra are extracted from the model images and the observations at half-beam increments along the plane of the disk as well as orthogonal to it"]
  - software: LIME, CASA
  - parameter: Continuum integrated intensity in model matched to observations [6 LIME modeling: "the integrated intensity in the continuum map that is produced is required to be the same as that measured in the observations"]
  - assumption: 12CO/13CO = 40; gas-to-dust 100; Ossenkopf & Henning dust opacities [Table 3: "Abundance of $^{12}$CO to $^{13}$CO$^a$"]
  - validation: Column densities compared with literature values [6.1.3: "The agreement with these other values in the literature help validate this model against other physical measurements of Arp 220 east."]
- result: Western outflow red mass = 2.0e7 ± not stated Msun [5 outflow: "a mass of 2.0$\times$10$^7$ M$_\odot$ for the red outflow and 2.0$\times$10$^6$ M$_\odot$ for the blue outflow in the western nucleus"]
- result: Western outflow mass loss rates red/blue = 50 and 8 ± not stated Msun/yr [5 outflow: "the mass loss rates of 50 and 8 M$_\odot$ yr$^{-1}$ for the red and blue outflows respectively were obtained"]
- result: Eastern outflow masses red/blue = 5e6 and 4e6 ± not stated Msun [5 outflow: "This yields a mass of 5$\times$10$^6$ M$_\odot$ and 4$\times$10$^6$ M$_\odot$ for the red and blue outflows respectively."]
- result: East nucleus gas mass (LIME) = 8e8 ± range 4-8e8 Msun [Table 3: "$8 \cdot 10^8$, M$_\odot$"]
- result: West nucleus gas mass (LIME) = 1.6e9 ± range 0.8-2.4e9 Msun [Table 4: "$1.6 \cdot 10^9$ M$_\odot$"]
- result: West nucleus 13CO peak optical depth = 18 ± not stated dimensionless [6.2 Arp 220 Western Nucleus: "a peak optical depth at the center of the western nucleus is found to be 18"]
- result: West nucleus central column density = 4.9e25 ± not stated H2 cm^-2 [6.2 Arp 220 Western Nucleus: "we find a central position column density of 4.9$\times10^{25}$ H$_2$ cm$^{-2}$"]
- not stated (card): Channel/velocity ranges used for continuum fitting and line-free channels in uvcontsub/imcontsub; Image size, cell size, clean threshold, masks, deconvolver; Exact velocity ranges for the outflow moment maps and HCN/other moments; LIME setup: number of grid points, collision rate files, model image pixel size, band velocity resolution; Distance source and inclination/PA for east disk (45 deg axis…

### 2024A&A...685A.142Y: Fitting the light curves of Sagittarius A* with a hot-spot model. Bayesian modeling of QU loops in the millimeter band (2024; agn_and_quasars; archival_reuse)
- question: Constrain collisionless plasma conditions and hot-spot geometry using polarized millimeter light curves.
- step 2: Generate polarized hot-spot light curves
  - method: Ray-trace a circular equatorial Gaussian spot; integrate snapshot intensities over the FOV into all four Stokes flux densities. [Model: "The spot lies in a circular orbit in the equatorial plane"] [Model: "Synthetic light curves were then generated by creating snapshot images at a predefined sequence of times and integrating their intensities over the FOV into total flux densities in all four Stokes parameters."]
  - software: ipole
  - parameter: FOV=40 M ≈200 μas; 64×64 pixels per frame [Model: "We adopted FOV=40$M$$\approx 200\, \mu$as"] [Model: "Due to large numerical cost of these calculations, we adopted a resolution of 64$\times$64 pixels per single frame."]
  - parameter: Gaussian standard deviation fixed at 3 M; boundary n_e,s ≤0.01 n_0,s [Model: "$R_s$ is the size of the Gaussian standard deviation, defining the size of the spot, and it remained fixed at $3M$ to limit the number of parameters."] [Model: "Additionally, we used a spot boundary, ignoring matter when $n_{e,s} \leq 0.01n_{0,s}$"]
  - parameter: Exponential temperature evolution; T_ini=2000 M; camera r=1000 M [Model: "We extended the spot models of (cite: Vos:2022) and allowed the Gaussian spot to change its temperature as an exponential function of time."] [Model: "We introduce the cooling coefficient $C_{\textrm{coef}}$ and the numerical parameter $T_{\rm{ini}}=2000M$"]
  - assumption: Relativistic thermal electrons; synchrotron emission, self-absorption, Faraday rotation and conversion [Model: "we assumed that electrons have a relativistic thermal energy distribution function"] [Model: "The underlying calculations employ the full relativistic radiative transfer model of `ipole`, including synchrotron emission, self-absorption, and the effects of Faraday rotation and conversion."]
  - assumption: Finite light speed; emission cut below ISCO; Kerr metric [Model: "We self-consistently accounted for the finite velocity of light, that is, we did not employ the commonly used fast light approximation."] [Model: "In our model, all emission is cut below the innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO)."]
  - assumption: M=4.155×10^6 M_sun [Model: "We assumed a mass of Sgr A\* $M=4.155 \times 10^6\,M_{\odot}$"]
  - validation: Compare 16², 64² and 196² light curves; check primary and secondary image contributions. [Resolution: "we show the relative difference (in percentile) of the light curves using three different resolutions ($16^2$, $64^2$, and $196^2$)"] [Resolution: "The second test we performed determined the relation of the first to the secondary image, and how in particular the latter is affected by the resolution."]
- step 4: Fit eight spot models
  - method: Fit spot-only Q and U with additive static Q_sha and U_sha; compare cooling, magnetic polarity and Keplerian/non-Keplerian combinations. [Two-step parameter estimation procedure: "In our first fitting step, we fit emission from the spot alone"] [Two-step parameter estimation procedure: "which were simply added to the variable light curves."]
  - software: ipole, dynesty, bipole
  - parameter: 200 initial live points; 200 per extra dynamical batch; 10 batches [Two-step parameter estimation procedure: "we used a dynamic sampler that was introduced by (cite: Higson:2019) with 200 initial live points and 200 points per extra dynamical batch (10 in total)."]
  - parameter: Terminating log-evidence change 0.01; posterior-exploration weight 100%; random walk [Two-step parameter estimation procedure: "We used the default stopping criteria, meaning a terminating change in the log-evidence of 0.01."] [Two-step parameter estimation procedure: "Additionally, we adopted a weight function that shifted the importance of the posterior exploration (rather than the evidence) to $100\%$."]
  - parameter: i=[90,180°]; a_*=[0.0,0.9]; r_s=[7,15] M [Model: "| i | $[90,180^\circ]$ | Viewing angle (inclination) of the observer: $i=0^\circ$ is face-on, $i=90^\circ$ is edge-on |"] [Model: "| $a_*$ | $[0.0,0.9]$ | Dimensionless black hole spin |"]
  - parameter: n_0,s=[10^4,10^8] cm^-3; Θ_0,s=[1,10^3]; B_0,s=[0.1,10^2] G [Model: "| $n_{0,s}$ | $[10^4,10^8]$ | Electron number density at the center of the spot (cm$^{-3}$) |"] [Model: "| $\Theta_{0,s}$ | $[1,10^3]$ | Electron dimensionless temperature at the center of the spot |"]
  - parameter: φ_cam=[0,360°]; Q_sha=[-1,1] Jy; U_sha=[-1,1] Jy [Model: "| $\phi_{\rm cam}$ | $[0,360^\circ]$ | Location of the camera at the first point of observation (shifts light curves left-right) |"] [Model: "| $Q_{\rm sha}$ | $[-1,1]$ | Average background Stokes $Q$ parameter (Jy) |"]
  - parameter: PA=[-90°,90°]; C_coef=[10^-5,10^-3] M^-1; K_coef=[0.5,1.2] [Model: "| PA | $[-90^\circ,90^\circ]$ | Position angle of the black hole spin projected on the observer’s screen, measured east of north |"] [Model: "| $C_{\textrm{coef}}$ | $[10^{-5},10^{-3}]$ | Coefficient for the exponential cooling ($M^{-1}$) |"]
  - assumption: Negligible Faraday effects between the separately fitted components [Two-step parameter estimation procedure: "This split in the fitting procedure works well as long as both Faraday effects between components are negligible, for example, when the two model components are spatially separated in the images."]
  - assumption: σ_Q,j=(ε_s|Q_j|+ε_t)^0.5 and σ_U,j=(ε_s|U_j|+ε_t)^0.5; ε_s=0.02; ε_t=0.01 [Two-step parameter estimation procedure: "\sigma_{Q,j} &=& (\epsilon_{s}|Q_j| + \epsilon_{t})^{0.5}"] [Two-step parameter estimation procedure: "\sigma_{U,j} &=& (\epsilon_{s}|U_j| + \epsilon_{t})^{0.5}"]
  - fitting: Dynamic nested sampling; ray-tracing per log-likelihood evaluation; log-normal plasma priors, flat remaining priors. [Two-step parameter estimation procedure: "`dynesty` is a Bayesian tool capable of performing dynamic nested sampling."] [Two-step parameter estimation procedure: "We embedded `ipole` so that the sampler called a ray-tracing operation for every log-likelihood evaluation."]
  - validation: Controlled synthetic recovery before fitting real data [Two-step parameter estimation procedure: "Finally, before fitting the real data, we performed a controlled `bipole` run using synthetic data to demonstrate the ability of our pipeline to recover the truth value."]
- result: Best-fitting viewing angles across spot models = (155,160) ± not stated deg [Discussion and conclusions: "all spot models show consistently similar best-fitting viewing angles $i \in(155,160)$ deg and a spot orbital radius $r_s\in(9,12.5)M$."]
- result: Spot orbital radii across models = (9,12.5) ± not stated M [Discussion and conclusions: "all spot models show consistently similar best-fitting viewing angles $i \in(155,160)$ deg and a spot orbital radius $r_s\in(9,12.5)M$."]
- result: Black-hole spin estimates = ∼0.8-0.9 ± not stated dimensionless [Discussion and conclusions: "Although we obtain a black hole spin estimate of high values ($a_* \sim 0.8-0.9$) in all cases, we did not consider negative spins in our priors."]
- result: PA for models except c_fb_nk = [-10,10] or [170,190] ± not stated deg [Discussion and conclusions: "All models except for c_fb_nk also consistently show PA $\in[-10,10]$ deg (or $[170,190]$ deg"]
- result: Approximate spot electron density range = (10^6,3×10^7) ± not stated particles/cm^3 [Discussion and conclusions: "All models consistently show that our spot parameters should be in an approximate range of $n_{\rm 0,s}\in(10^6,3\times10^7)$ ${\rm particles/cm^3}$, $\Theta_{\rm 0,s} \in(15,70)$, $B_{\rm 0,s}\in(2,40)$ G."]
- result: Approximate spot dimensionless electron temperature range = (15,70) ± not stated dimensionless [Discussion and conclusions: "All models consistently show that our spot parameters should be in an approximate range of $n_{\rm 0,s}\in(10^6,3\times10^7)$ ${\rm particles/cm^3}$, $\Theta_{\rm 0,s} \in(15,70)$, $B_{\rm 0,s}\in(2,40)$ G."]
- result: Approximate spot magnetic-field strength range = (2,40) ± not stated G [Discussion and conclusions: "All models consistently show that our spot parameters should be in an approximate range of $n_{\rm 0,s}\in(10^6,3\times10^7)$ ${\rm particles/cm^3}$, $\Theta_{\rm 0,s} \in(15,70)$, $B_{\rm 0,s}\in(2,40)$ G."]
- result: Particularly preferred non-Keplerian K_coef range = (0.65-0.85) ± not stated dimensionless [Discussion and conclusions: "Additionally, the non-Keplerian models prefer $K_{\rm coef}\in(0.5,1)$, and in particular, $(0.65-0.85)$."]
- result: nc_fb_nk background linear-polarization offsets Q_sha,U_sha = 0.11, -0.12 ± not stated Jy [Table 2: "| nc_fb_nk | $0.11$ | $-0.12$ |"]
- result: Mean Q and U deviations in resolution test = 2%, 1.5% ± not stated dimensionless [Resolution: "This amounts to $2\%$ for $\mathcal{Q}$ and $1.5\%$ for $\mathcal{U}$."]
- limitation (authors): Static background omits emission inside ISCO and variability. [Two-step parameter estimation procedure: "our model for the background emission (the shadow component) neither includes emission from regions within the ISCO nor has any variability."]
- limitation (authors): Exponential temperature evolution is not a physical synchrotron-cooling model. [Model: "This is a simplistic general prescription that does not aim to simulate the synchrotron cooling, but rather enabled us to explore the impact of cooling without making additional assumptions regarding its physical mechanisms."]
- limitation (authors): Spot shearing and interactions with the background are omitted. [Discussion and conclusions: "spot properties that we did not include in our simple model, such as the shearing of the spot or other spot interaction with the background flow (cite: Tiede2020)."]
- not stated (card): Exact input light-curve file, access location, polarization conventions, and preprocessing inherited from W22.; Precise retained timestamps and handling of calibrator gaps when selecting every twentieth sample.; Widths and truncation rules for the Gaussian priors in log density, log temperature, and log magnetic-field strength.; Exact ipole and dynesty versions, pipeline source, and random seeds.…

### 2020MNRAS.498.1319M: Insights into the planetary dynamics of HD 206893 with ALMA (2020; debris_disks, exoplanets; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What is the radial structure of the HD 206893 debris disc (is there a gap, asymmetry, CO gas, dust around the brown dwarf B), and what does it imply for the dynamics of the companions?
- step 4: Upper limit on dust around the brown dwarf B
  - method: Search for a point source at the expected BD position; 3 sigma flux limits converted to dust mass with radiative transfer [2.1.2 BD dust emission upper limit: "Nevertheless, we can place $3\sigma$ upper limits at 0.88 and 1.3 mm of 27 and 15 $\mu$Jy, respectively."] [2.1.2 BD dust emission upper limit: "Using the same radiative transfer tools as in (cite: Perez2019protolunar), we estimate a dust mass upper limit"]
  - parameter: BD expected at ~0.25'' separation towards the NE [2.1.2 BD dust emission upper limit: "We do not detect any emission arising from a point source towards the NE at the BD separation of $\sim0.25\ensuremath{''}$"]
  - parameter: Dust accretion rate onto BD from the debris disc ~4 kg/s using Bonsor et al. 2018 with corrected K_ej = 5.14e8 [2.1.2 BD dust emission upper limit: "We find that the BD could be accreting $\mu$m-sized dust at a rate of $\sim4$ kg s$^{-1}$."] [Footnote 5: "There is a typo in Table 1 in (cite: Bonsor2018) where $K_\mathrm{ej}$ should be $5.14\times10^{8}$ (private communication with Amy Bonsor)."]
  - assumption: Disc size >~0.1 au (a smaller, optically thick disc could hide more mass) [2.1.2 BD dust emission upper limit: "which is equivalent to a dust to planet ratio of $5\times10^{-8}$ for a disc with a size $\gtrsim0.1$ au"]
  - assumption: Accreted dust: 1 um radius grains, 3 g/cm3, accreted for 700 Myr [2.1.2 BD dust emission upper limit: "Assuming the accreted dust is all made of grains of 1 $\mu$m in radius, have a density of 3 g cm$^{-3}$, and have been accreted for 700 Myr"]
- step 6: Fit parametric gap disc model to visibilities
  - method: Two-power-law surface density with Gaussian gap, radiative transfer with RADMC-3D, MCMC fit in visibility space [3 Parametric axisymmetric disc model: "we fit a parametric disc model with a gap as in (cite: Marino2018hd107; Marino2019), combining radiative transfer simulations (cite: radmc3d) and an MCMC fitting procedure in the visibility space."]
  - software: RADMC-3D
  - parameter: Gap depth fixed to 1 [3 Parametric axisymmetric disc model: "here we fixed the gap depth to 1 (i.e. the surface density is zero at $r_\mathrm{g}$)"]
  - parameter: Vertical extent fixed to 5% of radius, uniform vertical mass distribution [3 Parametric axisymmetric disc model: "We thus choose to fix the vertical extent of the disc to 5% of the radius and use a uniform vertical mass distribution"]
  - parameter: r_min lower limit of 14 au; outer edge fixed at 250 au [3 Parametric axisymmetric disc model: "we impose a lower limit for $r_{\min}$ of 14 au"] [3 Parametric axisymmetric disc model: "we set a fixed outer edge of 250 au"]
  - parameter: Free parameters include disc inclination and PA, phase centre offsets, and position, size (2D Gaussian) and flux of a background SMG ~11.5'' to the SW; spectral index alpha free and uniform [3 Parametric axisymmetric disc model: "we also fit the disc inclination $i$ and position angle PA, and nuisance parameters such as phase center offsets, and the position, size (modelled as a 2D gaussian) and flux of a SMG"] [3 Parametric axisymmetric disc model: "which we leave as a free parameter that is uniform across the disc"]
  - parameter: Reported values are posterior medians with 16th and 84th percentile uncertainties [Table 1: "The quoted values correspond to the median, with uncertainties based on the 16th and 84th percentiles of the marginalised distributions."]
  - assumption: Dust mass counts only grains smaller than 1 cm, set by assumed opacities [3 Parametric axisymmetric disc model: "Note that this dust mass only includes the mass in grains smaller than 1 cm, which is set by the assumed dust opacities (cite: Marino2019)."]
  - assumption: Star modelled with Teff = 6500 K and R = 1.3 Rsun template spectrum [3 Parametric axisymmetric disc model: "The star is modelled with a template spectrum corresponding to an effective temperature of 6500 K and a radius of 1.3 $R_\odot$"]
  - fitting: MCMC in visibility space; sampler, priors and likelihood not stated [3 Parametric axisymmetric disc model: "an MCMC fitting procedure in the visibility space"]
  - validation: Residual maps show no residuals above 3 sigma within 200 au; model without a gap fails to reproduce visibility wiggles beyond 50 klambda [3.1 Results: "We do not find any residuals stronger than $3\sigma$ within 200 au."] [3.1 Results: "The model without a gap fails to reproduce the amplitude of the wiggles beyond 50 k$\lambda$."]
- result: 3 sigma upper limit on point-source flux at BD, 0.88 mm = 27 ± not stated uJy [2.1.2 BD dust emission upper limit: "we can place $3\sigma$ upper limits at 0.88 and 1.3 mm of 27 and 15 $\mu$Jy, respectively"]
- result: 3 sigma upper limit on point-source flux at BD, 1.3 mm = 15 ± not stated uJy [2.1.2 BD dust emission upper limit: "we can place $3\sigma$ upper limits at 0.88 and 1.3 mm of 27 and 15 $\mu$Jy, respectively"]
- result: Dust mass upper limit around B = 2e-4 ± not stated Earth masses [2.1.2 BD dust emission upper limit: "we estimate a dust mass upper limit of $2\times10^{-4}$ M$_\oplus$ ($2\times10^{-2}$ $M_\mathrm{moon}$)"]
- result: Total dust mass M_d = 0.031 ± 0.002 Earth masses [Table 1: "$0.031\pm0.002$ | total dust mass"]
- result: Disc inner radius r_min = 28 ± +5/-8 au [Table 1: "$28_{-8}^{+5}$ | disc inner radius"]
- result: Disc peak radius r_c = 114 ± +7/-5 au [3.1 Results: "we find that the disc dust density peaks at $r_\mathrm{c}=114^{+7}_{-5}$ au"]
- result: Inner surface density slope gamma_1 = 0.8 ± +-0.4 dimensionless [3.1 Results: "the surface density must increase moderately as $r^{0.8\pm0.4}$"]
- result: Outer surface density slope gamma_2 = -3.9 ± +0.5/-0.6 dimensionless [3.1 Results: "Exterior to that the disc surface density declines steeply as $r^{-3.9\pm0.5}$."]
- result: Gap centre radius r_g = 74 ± 3 au [3.1 Results: "we find that the gap center is well constrained to $74\pm3$ au"]
- result: Gap width w_g (FWHM) = 27 ± 5 au [3.1 Results: "The gap width is constrained to $27\pm5$ au."]
- result: Disc inclination from face-on = 40 ± 3 deg [3.1 Results: "The disc orientation is well constrained with $i=40\pm3\ensuremath{^\circ}$ and PA$=61\pm4\ensuremath{^\circ}$."]
- result: Disc position angle = 61 ± 4 deg [3.1 Results: "The disc orientation is well constrained with $i=40\pm3\ensuremath{^\circ}$ and PA$=61\pm4\ensuremath{^\circ}$."]
- result: Disc spectral index alpha (Table 1) = 2.54 ± 0.17 dimensionless [Table 1: "$2.54\pm0.17$ | disc spectral index"]
- result: Disc spectral index alpha (text value, differs slightly from Table 1) = 2.53 ± 0.16 dimensionless [3.1 Results: "The disc spectral index is found to be $2.53\pm0.16$"]
- result: Background SMG integrated flux at 0.88 mm = 1.5 ± not stated mJy [3.1 Results: "has an integrated flux of 1.5 mJy at 0.88 mm and 0.24 mJy at 1.3 mm"]
- limitation (authors): Planet mass assumes the gap is empty at its centre; a shallower, broader gap would change the inferred width and mass [3.1 Results: "A caveat in this interpretation is that we have assumed the gap is empty at its center."]
- limitation (authors): Frankenstein profiles lack a primary beam correction [2.1.1 Dust radial structure: "no primary beam correction is taken into account"]
- limitation (authors): Lower S/N makes gap width and depth comparison with other discs difficult [5.4 Are gaps common among exoKuiper bel…: "Given the lower S/N in HD 206893 observations, it is hard to compare the gap width and depth with the other two."]
- not stated (card): tclean parameters other than weighting and taper: deconvolver, cell size, image size, mask, threshold, niter; How the ACA and 12m data were combined for imaging and for the visibility fit (joint tclean or separate datasets); Which spectral windows were used for the continuum images, and whether the line window was flagged; Flux calibrator and calibrator-derived flux scale for each execution block…

### 2025PSJ.....6..286M: Zonal Winds in Titan's Middle Atmosphere from a Stellar Occultation Observed with Keck Adaptive Optics (2025; solar_system; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Which ALMA-derived or GCM zonal wind profiles reproduce the resolved occultation light distributions around Titan?
- step 5: Determine ALMA altitude sensitivity
  - method: NEMESIS radiative transfer and CH3CN abundance retrieval; derive wind contribution from altitude functional derivatives weighted by the spectral-line gradient [ALMA wind retrieval: "we used the Non-linear optimal Estimator for MultivariatE spectral analySIS (NEMESIS) to generate a detailed radiative transfer model for the observed CH$_3$CN emission"] [ALMA wind retrieval: "This abundance profile formed the basis of our “wind contribution function” calculation, which was obtained from the corresponding NEMESIS functional derivatives ($dQ/dz$) of flux with respect to altitude, as a function of frequency."]
  - software: CASA, tclean, NEMESIS
  - parameter: Re-image calibrated cubes before continuum subtraction; extract disk-averaged spectra with 1.5'' diameter aperture [ALMA wind retrieval: "The calibrated ALMA data cubes were re-imaged prior to continuum subtraction"] [ALMA wind retrieval: "Disk-averaged spectra were then extracted using a circular aperture with a diameter of 1.5$''$"]
  - parameter: 35 annuli; emission angles 0–75 degrees; tangential altitude up to 1050 km [ALMA wind retrieval: "Titan’s disk (plus atmosphere) was divided into 35 annuli corresponding to emission angles between 0-75 degrees, covering the center of Titan up to a tangential altitude of 1050 km"]
  - parameter: Model vertical extent 1200 km; negligible emission above 1000 km [ALMA wind retrieval: "the vertical extent of our model was 1200 km, but emission is negligible above 1000 km"]
  - parameter: Scale observed data, including continuum, by 0.982 [ALMA wind retrieval: "The observed ALMA data (including the continuum) were scaled by a constant factor of 0.982 to produce a match between the modeled and observed continuum levels."]
  - parameter: Average and peak-normalize the weighted contribution functions [ALMA wind retrieval: "which were then averaged and peak-normalized."]
  - assumption: Atmospheric temperature profile from (cite: 2020ApJ...903L..22T) [ALMA wind retrieval: "The atmospheric temperature profile was obtained from (cite: 2020ApJ...903L..22T)."]
  - assumption: A priori CH3CN abundance profile from (cite: nosowitz_improved_2025) [ALMA wind retrieval: "updated to incorporate the more recent CH$_3$CN abundance profile from (cite: nosowitz_improved_2025) for the *a priori* atmospheric state."]
  - assumption: J=36-35 has the same altitude sensitivity range because line opacities and energy levels are similar [ALMA wind retrieval: "Due to the similarity of the line opacities and energy levels, this range is also applicable to the $J=36-35$ band."]
  - fitting: Continuously retrieve CH3CN abundance at each altitude against J=35-34 line profiles [ALMA wind retrieval: "The CH$_3$CN abundance profile was continuously retrieved at each altitude to obtain a fit to the observed $J=35-34$ spectral line profiles."]
  - validation: Continuum matching after the 0.982 scaling [ALMA wind retrieval: "The observed ALMA data (including the continuum) were scaled by a constant factor of 0.982 to produce a match between the modeled and observed continuum levels."]
- step 6: Retrieve intrinsic latitudinal winds
  - method: Fit a simplified 3D radiative transfer Doppler map with modified atmWindMapGaussFit to account for beam-smearing and axial tilt [ALMA wind retrieval: "Due to the effects of beam-smearing, combined with Titan’s axial tilt, extraction of the intrinsic zonal wind field requires deconvolution of the Doppler shift data."] [ALMA wind retrieval: "We therefore followed a similar method to (cite: cordiner_detection_2020) to model the Doppler map and extract a latitudinal wind speed profile using a simplified 3D radiative transfer model."]
  - software: atmWindMapGaussFit, MPFIT
  - parameter: Seven latitude points separated by 30 degrees [ALMA wind retrieval: "Seven latitudinal points (with $\Delta{(l)}=30^{\circ}$ separation) were found to produce a good fit to the observed Doppler map"]
