  - parameter: Segmented linear interpolation, smoothed with Gaussian FWHM equal to point spacing [ALMA wind retrieval: "by a segmented linear function. This function was then smoothed by a Gaussian function of FWHM equal to the spacing between the points."]
  - parameter: Exponential taper with 1 degree scale length; zero wind at poles [ALMA wind retrieval: "An exponential taper was applied at the ends of the $v(l)$ profile (with an exponential smoothing scale length of 1$^{\circ}$), to ensure a velocity of zero at the poles."]
  - assumption: Zonal winds symmetric with respect to Titan’s polar axis [ALMA wind retrieval: "Our ALMA data analysis here therefore also inherently assumes that the zonal winds are symmetric with respect to Titan’s polar axis."]
  - fitting: Optimize each velocity point with MPFIT least squares and associated error estimates [ALMA wind retrieval: "Each velocity point $v_i$ was then optimized using MPFIT, to obtain the least-squares solution and associated error estimates."]
  - validation: Experiment with point counts; seven points give a good Doppler-map fit while remaining constrained by the data [ALMA wind retrieval: "To maximize the information content in the $v(l)$ retrieval, we experimented in fitting the observations with profiles incorporating different numbers of variable latitudinal points $i$."] [ALMA wind retrieval: "Seven latitudinal points (with $\Delta{(l)}=30^{\circ}$ separation) were found to produce a good fit to the observed Doppler map, while still allowing the retrieved profile to be well constrained by the data"]
- result: ALMA continuum scaling factor = 0.982 ± not stated dimensionless [ALMA wind retrieval: "The observed ALMA data (including the continuum) were scaled by a constant factor of 0.982 to produce a match between the modeled and observed continuum levels."]
- result: Average CH3CN emission altitude = 303 ± not stated km [ALMA wind retrieval: "The corresponding average emission altitude is 303 km, with sensitivity to winds primarily in the range $\sim150$-450 km."]
- result: Primary ALMA wind sensitivity altitude range = ~150-450 ± not stated km [ALMA wind retrieval: "The corresponding average emission altitude is 303 km, with sensitivity to winds primarily in the range $\sim150$-450 km."]
- result: Peak ALMA-derived wind speed = just under 200 ± not stated m/s [On the better model match to the ingres…: "The best match for the ingress lightspot was observed with the ALMA data with a peak wind speed just under 200 m/s, higher than nearly all of the zonal wind profiles simulated by the GCM."]
- limitation (authors): ALMA rubidium-maser cable-routing error likely introduced an unknown frequency shift and slight spectral broadening [ALMA wind retrieval: "an error in the cable routing of the ALMA rubidium maser at the time of our observations, which is likely to have introduced an erroneous and unknown frequency shift in our observations"]
- limitation (authors): ALMA winds are not vertically resolved; equatorial-jet contributions cannot be isolated [Comparison between GCM, occultation, an…: "As the ALMA wind profile is not vertically resolved, isolating the contribution of the equatorial jet from the contributions of winds at lower altitudes that contribute more heavily to the CH$_3$CN profile is not possible."]
- limitation (authors): GCM and ALMA forward-model inputs differ in vertical treatment [Comparison between GCM, occultation, an…: "the implementations of ALMA and GCM profiles into our forward model varied slightly due to GCM simulations being altitudinally resolved while ALMA retrieved wind speeds are averaged over an altitude contribution function"]

### 2021MNRAS.503.1459B: Dynamical model of an obscuring clumpy torus in AGNs - I. Velocity and velocity dispersion maps for interpretation of ALMA observations (2021; agn_and_quasars, nearby_galaxies; theory_or_simulation_compared_to_alma)
- question: Develop a dynamical N-body model of a self-gravitating obscuring clumpy torus in an AGN to construct velocity, velocity dispersion, and temperature maps and interpret ALMA and VLTI/MIDI observations.
- step 3: Construct model velocity and velocity dispersion maps via ray tracing and compare with ALMA observations
  - method: Orient the N=128k torus at inclination angle alpha, divide the sky plane into 30x30 cells matching ALMA resolution, trace 100x100 parallel rays per cell onto optically thick spherical clouds, and average line-of-sight velocities per cell. [Velocity and velocity dispersion maps: "Since we want to use our model velocity and velocity dispersion maps to interpret the ALMA observations of NGC 1068, we have first rescaled our model maps to the observational maps of (cite: Imanishi2018) (their figure 2) and divided the common area into $30 \times 30$ cells to match the resolution."] [Velocity and velocity dispersion maps: "Then we send $100\times100$ parallel rays to each cell from an observer to the clumpy structure."]
  - parameter: N = 128k clouds; grid = 30 x 30 cells; 100 x 100 rays per cell; relative cloud radii epsilon_cl = 0.01, 0.025; inclination angles alpha = 30 deg, 45 deg, 60 deg, 90 deg; scale factor R = 3.5 pc (NGC 1068), R = 10 pc (NGC 1672), R = 8 pc (N… [Velocity and velocity dispersion maps: "We use the result of our $N$-body simulations for $N$=128k clouds in correspondence with the previous estimation of the obscuration condition (cite: Nenkova2008b; Bannikova2012)."] [The velocity dispersion map and the mas…: "In our maps, 3 pc correspond to 3 cells owing to a scale factor $R=3.5$ pc chosen to match the observational velocity map in (cite: Imanishi2018) (fig.[11](#fig:map_01))."]
  - assumption: Clouds are optically thick spheres (if a ray hits a cloud, it does not pass through it). [Velocity and velocity dispersion maps: "The clouds are considered to have high optical depth, so that if a ray hits a cloud, it does not go through it (fig. [10](#fig:SketchRaytracing))."]
  - fitting: Mean velocity in rows of cells at 3 pc (3 cells) on left and right sides of the SMBH matched to v_obs = 20 km/s for NGC 1068; parameter grid search for qualitative visual match to ALMA velocity dispersion maps of NGC 1326 and NGC 1672. [The velocity dispersion map and the mas…: "To obtain the analogue of the observational velocity we find the mean value of the velocity for rows of cells at the left and at the right sides of the SMBH position located at a distance of 3 pc on a velocity map for a given inclination angle $\alpha$."] [NGC 1326 and NGC 1672 velocity dispersi…: "We produced a set of velocity dispersion maps for different combinations of parameters with the purpose of finding the best coincidence with the observational maps."]
  - validation: Tested alternative scenarios (power-law increase of cloud radii with distance, and non-equilibrium initial state) via ray tracing to check if they produce velocity dispersion anisotropy. [The velocity dispersion map and the mas…: "In order to check the above scenarios, we repeated the ray tracing experiments assuming a power law increase of the cloud radii with the distance to the SMBH."]
- step 4: Derive cloud temperatures and construct temperature distribution maps for NGC 1068
  - method: Calculate equilibrium blackbody cloud temperatures heated by an anisotropically emitting Shakura-Sunyaev accretion disk using ray tracing from the center, then project onto a 100x100 cell observer grid via line-of-sight ray tracing. [Temperature of clouds in the torus: "We send the rays from the accretion disk over the whole range of steradians with a step that is equal to the angular size of a cloud at 3 pc distance."] [Temperature of clouds in the torus: "For this reason, the next step is to divide the image into $100\times100$ cells and each cell gets the value of the mean temperature within it."]
  - parameter: Accretion rate M_dot = 0.2 M_sun/year (from L_bol = 5.65 x 10^44 erg/s); image grid = 100 x 100 cells; epsilon_cl = 0.01 and 0.025; theta = 45 deg [Temperature of clouds in the torus: "Taking into account ([\[eq10.9\]](#eq10.9)), we obtain the accretion rate in NGC 1068 to be $\dot{M} \approx 0.2 M_\odot$/year."] [Temperature of clouds in the torus: "Fig.[18](#fig:temp_maps_01) and [19](#fig:temp_maps_025) show the temperature distribution maps for a single cloud radius $\varepsilon_{\rm cl} = 0.01$ and $\varepsilon_{\rm cl} = 0.025$ respectively, for the initial half-opening angle of the wind $\theta=45^\circ$."]
  - assumption: Standard geometrically thin, optically thick accretion disk; spherical clouds in thermodynamic equilibrium with disk radiation absorbed over half their surface; anisotropic disk flux F(theta) = (1/3) cos(theta)(1 + 2 cos(theta)); optically… [Temperature of clouds in the torus: "Assume that the clouds are in thermodynamic equilibrium with the accretion disk. Within the framework of a standard geometrically thin and optically thick accretion disk, the energy flux emitted by the disk surface has the form (cite: Shakura1973):"] [Temperature of clouds in the torus: "To take it into account in our model, we use an angular dependence for the flux (cite: Phillips1986; Netzer1987) for the semi-infinite scattering atmosphere:"]
  - validation: Compared derived cloud temperatures and hot-region morphology with VLTI/MIDI mid-infrared observations of NGC 1068. [Temperature of clouds in the torus: "So, for the given accretion rate and torus scales, the temperature satisfies the observational values (cite: Jaffe2004; Raban2009)."]
- result: SMBH mass in NGC 1068 (M_smbh) = 5e6 ± not stated M_odot [Abstract: "By comparing the model with the observational maps of NGC 1068 we find that the SMBH mass is $M_\text{smbh}=5\times 10^6 M_\odot$ for the range of the torus inclination angles $45^\circ - 60^\circ$."]
- result: Torus inclination angle in NGC 1068 (alpha) = 45 - 60 ± not stated deg [Abstract: "By comparing the model with the observational maps of NGC 1068 we find that the SMBH mass is $M_\text{smbh}=5\times 10^6 M_\odot$ for the range of the torus inclination angles $45^\circ - 60^\circ$."]
- result: Cloud radius in NGC 1068 (R_cl) = 0.1 ± not stated pc [The velocity dispersion map and the mas…: "As it can be seen from the Table [1](#tab:1068mass), the observational velocity $v_{obs} = 20$ km s$^{-1}$ for a SMBH mass $M_\text{smbh} = 5 \times 10^6 M_\odot$ is satisfied for the range of the torus inclination angles $\alpha=45^\circ - 60^\circ$ for the relative radius of the clouds $\varepsilon_{\rm cl} = 0.025$…"]
- result: SMBH mass in NGC 1672 (M_smbh) = 1e8 ± not stated M_odot [NGC 1326 and NGC 1672 velocity dispersi…: "The best combinations of the parameters are the following; for NGC 1672, the SMBH mass $M_\text{smbh} = 1 \times 10^8 M_\odot$; the inclination angle of the torus $\alpha = 60^\circ$, with the relative cloud radius $\varepsilon_{\rm cl} =0.01$ or $\alpha = 45^\circ$ with $\varepsilon_{\rm cl} =0.025$"]
- result: SMBH mass in NGC 1326 (M_smbh) = 1.5e7 ± not stated M_odot [NGC 1326 and NGC 1672 velocity dispersi…: "for NGC 1326, $M_\text{smbh} = 1.5 \times 10^7 M_\odot$; $\alpha = 60^\circ$ with $\varepsilon_{\rm cl} =0.01$ or $\alpha = 45^\circ$ with $\varepsilon_{\rm cl} =0.025$."]
- result: Accretion rate in NGC 1068 (M_dot) = 0.2 ± not stated M_odot/year [Temperature of clouds in the torus: "Taking into account ([\[eq10.9\]](#eq10.9)), we obtain the accretion rate in NGC 1068 to be $\dot{M} \approx 0.2 M_\odot$/year."]
- result: Maximum equilibrium cloud temperature at torus throat in NGC 1068 (T_cl^max) = 820 ± not stated K [Temperature of clouds in the torus: "For the accretion rate $\dot{M}=0.2 M_\odot/$ year, the equilibrium temperature has a maximum value $T_{\rm cl}^{\rm max} \simeq 820$ K at the inner edge (throat) of the torus with $r_{\rm min} \simeq 0.4$ pc; and a minimal value $T_{\rm cl}^{\rm min} \simeq 300$ K in the torus body region with $r_{\rm max} \simeq 3$ …"]
- result: Minimum equilibrium cloud temperature in torus body in NGC 1068 (T_cl^min) = 300 ± not stated K [Temperature of clouds in the torus: "For the accretion rate $\dot{M}=0.2 M_\odot/$ year, the equilibrium temperature has a maximum value $T_{\rm cl}^{\rm max} \simeq 820$ K at the inner edge (throat) of the torus with $r_{\rm min} \simeq 0.4$ pc; and a minimal value $T_{\rm cl}^{\rm min} \simeq 300$ K in the torus body region with $r_{\rm max} \simeq 3$ …"]
- limitation (authors): The torus-to-SMBH mass ratio is fixed to M_torus/M_smbh = 0.02 rather than varied as a free parameter. [Initial conditions: "Actually, the ratio $M_{\text{torus}}/M_{\text{smbh}}$ should be a free parameter."]
- limitation (authors): Simulations assume a dissipationless system and neglect cloud collisions and tidal shearing/merging. [Results of N-body simulations: "These oscillations have been derived under the assumption that the system is dissipationless. Were collisions included in the simulation, the result might be different."]
- limitation (authors): Radiation pressure on cloud dynamics near the accretion disk is neglected. [Box-orbits as an explanation for the os…: "Obviously, the dynamics of clouds located near the accretion disk can be also influenced by the radiation pressure (cite: Plewa2013; Venanzi2020). ### 2026A&A...706A.174A: Hiding behind a curtain of dust: Gas and dust properties of an ultra-luminous strongly-lensed z = 3.75 galaxy behind the Milky Way disc (2026; high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Confirm the spectroscopic redshift of the strongly lensed submm galaxy J154506 behind the Milky Way (Lupus-I cloud) and make an initial characterisation of its dust and molecular gas properties
- step 5: Model dust and CO SED jointly with non-LTE radiative transfer
  - method: TUNER non-LTE LVG radiative transfer with lognormal density PDF fitted to CO line SED and dust continuum with MCMC [6.2 Radiative transfer model results: "non-local thermodynamic equilibrium (non-LTE) radiative transfer calculations model the CO line intensities and dust continuum using a large velocity gradient approximation"]
  - software: TUNER, emcee
  - parameter: Magnification fixed to give intrinsic properties [Simultaneous modelling of the observed …: "is fixed in the model to therefore provide the intrinsic source properties"]
  - parameter: Gas-to-dust mass ratio fixed at about 100 after an initial free fit [6.2 Radiative transfer model results: "The initial model fits, obtained with a free molecular gas-to-dust mass ratio (GDMR), yielded a median GDMR of $100$, which we subsequently fixed"]
  - assumption: [CO]/[H2] fixed to a fiducial Milky Way value (log = -4.0) [6.2 Radiative transfer model results: "to a fiducial Milky Way value"]
  - fitting: emcee with 100 walkers and 50 autocorrelation times, uniform priors [Simultaneous modelling of the observed …: "using 100 walkers and 50 autocorrelation times and uniform priors."]
  - validation: Narrow marginalised posteriors; consistency with MERCURIUS [6.2 Radiative transfer model results: "the narrowness of the marginalised posterior distributions of the parameters"]
- result: Dust temperature from TUNER joint fit = 38 ± not stated K [7 Summary and conclusions: "The combined modelling of the dust SED and CO excitation ladder constrains the dust temperature to 38 K"]
- result: Gas-to-dust temperature ratio Tk/Td (median) = 1.1 ± 0.2 dimensionless [6.2 Radiative transfer model results: "the median gas-to-dust temperature ratio is T$_{\rm{k}}$/T$_{\rm{d}} =1.1 \pm 0.2$"]
- result: TUNER dust emissivity index = 2.2 ± not stated dimensionless [6.2 Radiative transfer model results: "yielding a dust emissivity index of $2.2$"]
- result: Total molecular gas mass M_ISM (16th-84th percentile) = 3.1-8.5e11 ± 16th-84th percentile range Msun [6.2 Radiative transfer model results: "M$_{\rm{ISM}} = 3.1-8.5 \times 10^{11} ~\rm{M}_{\odot}$"]
- result: CO(1-0) luminosity L'CO(1-0) (16th-84th percentile) = 4.1-6.5e10 ± 16th-84th percentile range K km/s pc^2 [6.2 Radiative transfer model results: "CO luminosity L$^{\prime}_{\rm{CO(1-0)}}=4.1-6.5 \times 10^{10}~\rm{K\, km \,s}^{-1}\,\rm{pc}^2$"]
- result: CO-to-H2 conversion factor alpha_CO(1-0) (16th-84th percentile) = 5.2-17 ± 16th-84th percentile range Msun pc^-2 (K km/s)^-1 [6.2 Radiative transfer model results: "$\alpha_{\rm{CO(1-0)}} = 5.2-17~\rm{M}_\odot\, \rm{pc}^{-2} (\rm{K\, km \,s}^{-1})^{-1}$"]
- result: Intrinsic L_IR (8-1000 micron) from TUNER (16th-84th percentile) = 5.5-6.5e12 ± 16th-84th percentile range Lsun [6.2 Radiative transfer model results: "L$_{\rm{IR}}(8-1000 \mu \rm{m})=5.5-6.5\times10^{12} \rm{L}_{\odot}$ after correcting for magnification"]
- limitation (authors): Stellar mass and other SED-derived properties highly uncertain because of Lupus-I extinction [4 Spectroscopic redshift confirmation: "makes SED-derived properties, such as the stellar mass, highly uncertain."]
- limitation (authors): Lens redshift not confirmed; MUSE and FORS2 data too poor [3.5 MUSE and FORS2 observations: "The low quality of this dataset precluded spectroscopic confirmation of the lens and prevented further detailed analysis."]
- limitation (authors): Dust mass strongly depends on the assumed kappa and optical depth [6.1 Dust emission and FIR-derived prope…: "Dust mass estimates are highly dependent on the assumed"]

### 2017Icar..286..289L: Detection of CO and HCN in Pluto's atmosphere with ALMA (2017; solar_system; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Detect CO and HCN (and search for HC3N, HC15N) in Pluto's atmosphere with ALMA and constrain the CO abundance, the thermal structure and the HCN distribution.
- step 3: Retrieve CO mole fraction and temperature profile
  - method: Spherical-geometry LTE radiative transfer model (181 layers, 0-1300 km) convolved with 0.35" beam; iterative regularized optimal-estimation inversion with fixed-CO and free-CO runs and several a priori profiles [4 Modelling and analysis: "The model covers the 0-1300 km altitude range by means of 181 layers, with an altitude-varying layer thickness."]
  - software: custom radiative transfer code
  - parameter: Surface pressure 12 μbar nominal (10, 14 tested); CO uniformly mixed; correlation length = scale height; convergence when fit rms constant within 1% [4.1.1 Method: "Iterations were performed until convergence – defined by the constancy of the fit quality (rms between observations and models) within 1 % from an iteration to the next one – was reached."]
  - assumption: LTE; molecular mass 27.94; surface radius 1190 km; Fresnel surface with dielectric constant 3.5 [4 Modelling and analysis: "Emission from the surface was calculated by using a Fresnel-emission model with a dielectric constant of 3.5"]
  - fitting: Optimal-estimation (Conrath/Rodgers) with alpha set by equalling traces of alpha K S K^T and E [4.1.1 Method: "an optimum choice of $\alpha$ (in terms of stability and rapidity of the algorithm) was achieved by equalling the traces of the $\alpha$**KSK$^T$** and **E** matrices."]
  - validation: Reduced chi2 ~0.95; averaging kernels; tests of a priori, surface pressure and ±5% calibration [4.1.1 Method: "the best fit corresponded to a reduced $\chi^2$ of $\sim$0.95 over a $\pm$15 MHz interval centered on the CO(3-2) line frequency"]
- step 4: Constrain HCN vertical distribution
  - method: Manual fits with parametrized HCN profiles (uniform, saturated, step, two-component) and inversion retrievals using the CO-derived nominal temperature profile; test warm-layer solutions [4.2.2 HCN initial fits: "At this stage, we obtained an optimum manual fit of the HCN spectrum (solid light blue line in Fig. 6) with a two-component HCN distribution"]
  - software: custom radiative transfer code
  - parameter: Saturation from Fray & Schmitt vapor pressure; nominal profile has 106 K stratopause [4.2.2 HCN initial fits: "calculated as saturated HCN for the adopted temperature profile (with a 106 K stratopause)"]
  - assumption: Warm-layer tests required HCN at saturation everywhere [4.2.4 Evidence for HCN supersaturation: "requiring that HCN is at saturation throughout the atmosphere."]
  - fitting: Manual fits plus optimal-estimation retrievals with several a priori HCN profiles [4.2.3 HCN retrievals: "Similar HCN profiles are returned for various a priori values of the HCN mole fraction"]
  - validation: Averaging kernels; consistency test against New Horizons/Alice CH4/N2 LOS columns and occultation lightcurve [5.1.1 New Horizons/Alice: "We conclude that alternate solutions with an HCN profile at saturation, while not excluded from the sole point-of-view of the ALMA data, are not viable"]
- step 5: Upper limits on HC15N and HC3N
  - method: Scale the best-fit HCN model by constant isotope ratio or assume HC3N has the HCN distribution; compare with non-detections at 2 sigma [4.3 HC15N and HC3N upper limits: "Using our best fit HCN model (light blue line from Fig. 6) and scaling it by a vertically constant isotopic ratio factor"]
  - software: custom radiative transfer code
  - assumption: HC3N has same vertical distribution as HCN [4.3 HC15N and HC3N upper limits: "Assuming here for simplicity that HC$_{3}$N has the same distribution as our best fit HCN model"]
- result: CO mole fraction at 12 μbar = 515 ± ±40 ppm [4.1.2 Results: "We conclude that the CO mole fraction in Pluto’s atmosphere is 515 $\pm$ 40 ppm for p$_{surf}$ = 12 $\mu$bar"]
- result: CO surface-referred column density at 12 μbar = 0.100 ± ±0.008 cm-am [4.1.2 Results: "(2.69$\pm$0.21)$\times$10$^{18}$ mol cm$^{-2}$ (0.100$\pm$0.008 cm-am) for p$_{surf}$ = 12 $\mu$bar"]
- result: Temperature at 300 km = 70 ± ±2 K [4.1.2 Results: "with a best determined temperature of 70$\pm$2 K at 300 km"]
- result: HCN column density = 1.6e14 ± ±0.4e14 cm^-2 [Summary: "The HCN column is (1.6$\pm$0.4)$\times$10$^{14}$ cm$^{-2}$"]
- result: HCN mole fraction near 800 km = 4e-5 ± not stated dimensionless [Summary: "a best determined value of 4$\times$10$^{-5}$ near 800 km"]
- result: HC3N column upper limit = <2e13 ± not stated cm^-2 [4.3 HC15N and HC3N upper limits: "corresponding to a maximum HC$_{3}$N column density of 2$\times$10$^{13}$ cm$^{-2}$ (Fig. 10)"]
- result: HC14N/HC15N lower limit (2 sigma) = 125 ± not stated dimensionless [4.3 HC15N and HC3N upper limits: "implies a lower limit of the HC$^{14}$N / HC$^{15}$N ratio equal to 125 at 2$\sigma$ confidence level"]
- result: HCN surface-referred net production rate = ~2e7 ± not stated cm^-2 s^-1 [Summary: "This suggests a surface-referred net column production rate of $\sim$2$\times$10$^{7}$ cm$^{-2}$s$^{-1}$."]
- limitation (authors): Lower mesosphere and stratopause temperatures are more uncertain, and the HCN vertical distribution is not unique given moderate optical thickness [5.1.2 Stellar occultations: "the lower mesosphere and stratopause temperatures must be considered as more uncertain."]
- limitation (authors): Spatial resolution insufficient to resolve surface [Observations and data reduction: "This is sufficient to separate the emission of Pluto from Charon, but not enough to provide spatial resolution on the surface of these bodies."]
- not stated (card): The radiative transfer and inversion code is custom and not released; reproduction requires reimplementation.; Which channels were treated as line-free and the AIPS imaging parameters for the continuum self-calibration (weighting, cell size) are not given.; Calibration scheduling-block names or pipeline script versions and flagging are not specified.; Exact a priori temperature profiles (mixed pr…

### 2025A&A...699A..70H: Investigating the chemical link between H<SUB>2</SUB>CO and CH<SUB>3</SUB>OH within the central molecular zone of NGC 253 (2025; nearby_galaxies, astrochemistry; large_program_or_survey)
- question: Investigate the physical and chemical properties traced by CH3OH and H2CO in NGC 253 and whether the two species are chemically linked

  - method: RADEX via SpectralRadex coupled with Nautilus sampler; fit n_H2, T_kin, column densities of both spin isomers and beam filling factor per GMC [4 Non-LTE radiative transfer modeling a…: "for Bayesian posterior and evidence estimation."] [4 Non-LTE radiative transfer modeling a…: "The collisional excitation rates used for are from (cite: Dagdigian_2023) while the rates are from (cite: H2CO_mole_data)."]
  - software: RADEX, SpectralRadex, Nautilus
  - parameter: Priors (Table 2): n_H2 1e2-1e8 cm^-3 log-uniform; T_kin 10-800 K uniform; column densities 1e12-1e18 cm^-2 log-uniform; eta_ff 0-1 uniform [Table 2: "$10^{2}-10^{8}$"] [Table 2: "$10-800$"]
  - parameter: T_kin outside the collisional-rate grid (3-250 K CH3OH, 10-300 K H2CO) is clamped to the nearest grid temperature [4 Non-LTE radiative transfer modeling a…: "uses the minimum or maximum kinetic temperature at which collisional excitation rates are provided"]
  - assumption: Gaussian intensity uncertainties [4 Non-LTE radiative transfer modeling a…: "We also assume that the uncertainty on our measured intensities is Gaussian"]
  - assumption: Beam filling factor eta_ff = theta_S^2 / (theta_MB^2 + theta_S^2) [4 Non-LTE radiative transfer modeling a…: "the beam filling factor ($\eta_{ff}=\frac{\theta^{2}_{S}}{\theta^{2}_{MB}+\theta^{2}_{S}}$)"]
  - fitting: Nautilus sampler; uniform or log-uniform priors; likelihood exp(-chi^2/2) [4 Non-LTE radiative transfer modeling a…: "We assume priors of uniform or log-uniform distribution within the determined ranges"] [4 Non-LTE radiative transfer modeling a…: "our likelihood is given by $P(\theta | d) \sim \exp(-\frac{1}{2}\chi^2),$"]
  - validation: Compared old (Rabli and Flower 2010) and new (Dagdigian 2023) CH3OH rates: the E/A ratio range changes from 0.85-2.84 to 0.25-1.26; Nautilus gave results indistinguishable from UltraNest [4 Non-LTE radiative transfer modeling a…: "With the (cite: CH3OH_mol_data_2010) rates, the average range for the best EAR was 0.85–2.84 whilst with the (cite: Dagdigian_2023) rates, the range is 0.25–1.26."] [Footnote 6: "while providing indistinguishable results."]
- result: CH3OH T_kin range across GMCs = 10.59-87.5 ± not stated K [Table (CH3OH Bayesian results): "$10.59-87.5$"]
- result: CH3OH T_kin, GMC 1a = 76.06 ± +11.44/-9.69 K [Table (CH3OH Bayesian results): "$76.06^{+11.44}_{-9.69}$"]
- result: CH3OH T_kin, GMC 2b = 11.14 ± +0.57/-0.55 K [Table (CH3OH Bayesian results): "$11.14^{+0.57}_{-0.55}$"]
- result: CH3OH log n_H2 range across GMCs = 4.11-7.11 ± not stated log(cm^-3) [Table (CH3OH Bayesian results): "$4.11-7.11$"]
- result: CH3OH beam filling factor range = 0.07-0.92 ± not stated dimensionless [Table (CH3OH Bayesian results): "$0.07-0.92$"]
- result: CH3OH E-/A- column density ratio (best) range across GMCs = 0.25-1.26 ± not stated dimensionless [Table (CH3OH Bayesian results): "$0.25-1.26$"]
- result: H2CO T_kin, GMC 4 = 266.73 ± +316.23/-122.64 K [Table (H2CO Bayesian results): "$266.73^{+316.23}_{-122.64}$"]
- result: H2CO T_kin, GMC 3 = 129.71 ± +49.30/-32.99 K [Table (H2CO Bayesian results): "$129.71^{+49.30}_{-32.99}$"]
- result: H2CO T_kin, all GMCs lower bound = >=67.23 ± not stated K [Table (H2CO Bayesian results): "$\geq67.23$"]
- result: H2CO beam filling factor range = 0.04-0.67 ± not stated dimensionless [Table (H2CO Bayesian results): "$0.04-0.67$"]
- limitation (authors): H2CO abundances and parameters are poorly constrained [abstract: "The H$_2$CO abundances are constant, though poorly constrained"]
- limitation (authors): Derived EAR depends on the choice of CH3OH collisional rates [4 Non-LTE radiative transfer modeling a…: "The choice of the rates used for thus seem more critical if one aims to derive the EAR."]

### 2021A&A...647A..10G: Bottlenecks to interstellar sulfur chemistry. Sulfur-bearing hydrides in UV-illuminated gas and grains (2021; astrochemistry, milky_way_ism; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Whether gas-phase reactions of S atoms and SH+ molecules with vibrationally excited H2 can explain the presence of abundant H2S in FUV-illuminated gas, or if grain surface chemistry has to be invoked.
- step 3: Derive single-slab non-LTE excitation and column densities of SH+, SH, and H2S
  - method: Solve statistical equilibrium and nonlocal radiative transfer with a Monte Carlo code including chemical formation pumping and destruction rates, dust continuum radiation, and inelastic collisions with H2, H, and e-. [4 Coupled nonlocal excitation and chemi…: "In these cases, a proper treatment of the molecule excitation requires including chemical formation and destruction rates in the statistical equilibrium"] [4. Coupled nonlocal excitation and chem…: "Here we implemented this formalism in a Monte Carlo code that explicitly models the nonlocal behavior of the excitation and radiative transfer problem (see Appendix of Goicoechea et al. 2006)."]
  - parameter: Modified blackbody dust continuum at T_d ∼ 50 K with τ_λ = 0.03 (150/λ[µm])^1.6 [4. Coupled nonlocal excitation and chem…: "for completeness we also included radiative excitation by a modified blackbody at a dust temperature of ∼ 50 K and a dust opacity τ λ = 0.03 (150/λ[µm]) [1][.][6]"]
  - parameter: T_form ≈ 2000 K for SH+; T_form = T_k for SH [4.1. SH + excitation and column density: "Since the formation of SH [+] is driven by reaction (1) when H 2 molecules are in v ≥ 2, here we adopted T form ≃ E (v = 2, J = 0) / k − 9860 K ≈ 2000 K ."] [4.2. SH excitation and column density: "Models in Fig. 7 include these chemical rates for T form = T k (a lower limit to the unknown formation temperature)."]
  - assumption: f(H2) = 2n(H2)/n_H = 2/3; n_e = 1.4x10^-4 n_H; H2 OTP ratio thermalized to T_k [4. Coupled nonlocal excitation and chem…: "The molecular gas fraction, f (H 2 ) = 2 n (H 2 )/ n H, is set to 2/3, where n H = n (H) + 2 n (H 2 ) is the total density of H nuclei ."] [4. Coupled nonlocal excitation and chem…: "the electron density n e is set to n e ≃ n (C [+] ) = 1.4×10 [−][4] n H (e.g., Cuadrado et al. 2019). For the inelastic collisions with o -H 2 and p -H 2, we assumed that the H 2 ortho-to-para (OTP) ratio is thermalized to the gas temperature."]
  - assumption: SH-H and SH-H2 collisional rates scaled from SH-He rates by the square root of reduced mass ratios [4.2. SH excitation and column density: "Lacking specific inelastic collision rate coefficients, we scaled the available SH– He rates of Kłos et al. (2009) by the square root of the reduced mass ratios and estimated the SH– H and SH– H 2 collisional rates."]
  - fitting: Grid search / comparison of single-slab models against observed line intensities and ratios [4. Coupled nonlocal excitation and chem…: "we search for the combination of single T k, n H, and N that better reproduces the observed line intensities (so-called “single-slab” approach)."]
  - validation: Compared models with and without formation pumping (F=D=0 vs F,D vs F,Dx10) and verified consistency with the 683 GHz SH+ upper limit. [4.1. SH + excitation and column density: "Figure 6 shows results of several models: without formation pumping (dotted curves for model “ F = D = 0” ), adding formation pumping with SH [+] destruction by H and e [−] (continuous curves for model “ F, D ” )"] [4.1. SH + excitation and column density: "This model is consistent with the upper limit intensity of the 683 GHz line (Nagy et al. 2013) ."]
- step 4: Model depth-dependent PDR chemistry and multi-slab radiative transfer for pure gas-phase and gas-grain networks
  - method: Run stationary isobaric PDR models of the Orion Bar (and constant-density models for general G0 and n_H) in the Meudon PDR code (v1.5.4) with updated gas-phase rates, wavelength-dependent photoreaction cross sections, and gas-grain / surfa… [6. PDR models of S-bearing hydrides: "In this analysis we used version 1.5.4. of the Meudon PDR code (Le Petit et al. 2006; Bron et al. 2014). Following our previous studies, we model the Orion Bar as a stationary PDR at constant thermal-pressure"] [6.3. Gas-grain PDR model results: "Here we show PDR model results in which we add a simple network of gas-grain reactions for a small number of S-bearing (S, SH, and H 2 S) and O-bearing (O, OH, H 2 O, O 2, and CO) species."]
  - software: Meudon PDR code
  - parameter: G0 = 2x10^4 Habing; A_V = 10 mag; P_th/k = 2x10^8 cm^-3 K; ζ_CR = 10^-16 H2 s^-1; R_V = 5.5; M_gas/M_dust = 100 [Table 2: "FUV illumination, G 0 2×10 [4] Habing ( a ) Total depth A V 10 mag Thermal pressure P th / k 2×10 [8] cm [−][3] K"] [Table 2: "Cosmic Ray ζ CR 10 [−][16] H 2 s [−][1] ( b ) R V = A V / E B−V 5.5 Orion [c] M gas / M dust 100 Local ISM"]
  - parameter: Elemental abundances: O/H = 3.2x10^-4, C/H = 1.4x10^-4, S/H = 1.4x10^-5 [Table 2: "Abundance O / H 3.2×10 [−][4] Abundance C / H 1.4×10 [−][4] Orion [d] Abundance S / H 1.4×10 [−][5] Solar [e]"]
  - parameter: Binding energies: low E_b/k (S = 1100 K, SH = 1500 K) vs high E_b/k (S = 2600 K, SH = 2700 K), E_b/k(H2S) = 2700 K; Y(H2S) = 1.2x10^-3 photon^-1 [Table 4: "S 1100 [a] /2600 [b] 10 [−][4] SH 1500 [a] /2700 [b] 10 [−][4] H 2 S 2700 [b] [,] [c] 1.2×10 [−][3] [g] (as H 2 S)"]

The lower intensity limits refer to a face-on PDR."]
  - assumption: Grain size distribution n_gr ∝ a^-3.5; chemical desorption s-H + s-SH -> H2S efficiency of 50%; instant surface hydrogenation when τ_gr,H is shorter than sublimation/photodesorption timescales [6.3. Gas-grain PDR model results: "Grain size distributions ( n gr ∝ a [−][3][.][5], where a is the grain radius) and gas-grain reactions are treated within the Meudon code formalism"] [6.3. Gas-grain PDR model results: "In addition, we add the direct chemical desorption reaction s-H + s-SH → H 2 S with an efficiency of 50 % per reactive event"]
  - fitting: Direct comparison of multi-slab Monte Carlo predicted line intensities and column densities with observations [6.4. Line intensity comparison and H 2 …: "We now specifically compare the SH [+], SH, and H 2 S line intensities implied by the different PDR models with the intensities observed toward the DF position of the Bar."]
  - validation: Tested sensitivity to P_th, inclination angle (face-on vs edge-on), extinction curve, binding energies (low vs high E_b), and chemical desorption efficiency ϵ. [6.4. Line intensity comparison and H 2 …: "Right panel of Fig. 15 shows SH and H 2 S line emission predictions for the high E b gas-grain model (magenta squares), low E b gas-grain model (gray triangles), and a pure gas-phase model (cyan circles)."]
- result: SH+ column density N(SH+) = 1.1x10^13 ± not stated cm^-2 [4.1. SH + excitation and column density: "The best fit to the observed lines in model F, D is for N (SH [+] ) ≃ 1.1×10 [13] cm [−][3], n H ≃ 3×10 [5] cm [−][3], and T k ≃ 200 K ."]
- result: SH column density upper limit N(SH) = ≤ (0.6-1.6)x10^14 ± not stated cm^-2 [4.2. SH excitation and column density: "If SH and SH [+] arise from roughly the same gas at similar physical conditions ( n H ≃ 10 [6] cm [−][3] and T k ≃ 200 K ) the best model column density is for N (SH) ≤ (0.6-1.6)×10 [14] cm [−][2] ."]
- result: H2S total column density N(H2S) = 2.5x10^14 ± not stated cm^-2 [4.3. H 2 S excitation and column density: "The vertical dotted lines in Fig. 8 show the best model, N (H 2 S) = N ( o -H 2 S)+ N ( p -H 2 S) = 2.5×10 [14] cm [−][2], with an OTP ratio of 2.9 ± 0.3"]
- result: H2S ortho-to-para (OTP) ratio = 2.9 ± 0.3 dimensionless [abstract: "explain the observed H 2 S column density ( ∼ 2.5 × 10 [14] cm [−][2], with an ortho-to-para ratio of 2.9 ± 0.3; consistent with the high-temperature statistical value)."]

- limitation (authors): Bad weather limited SOFIA/GREAT observations to ~70 min on a single flight, so the achieved rms (50 mK) is insufficient for an unambiguous detection of SH, providing only upper limits. SOFIA/GREAT search for SH emission: "The original plan was to observe during two flights in November 2019 but due to bad weather conditions, only ∼70 min of observations were carried out in a single flight."]
