### 2015ApJ...814..143A: The Peculiar Distribution of CH3CN in IRC +10216 Seen by ALMA (2015; evolved_stars, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Where is CH3CN distributed in the envelope of IRC +10216 at sub-arcsec resolution, and where is it formed?
- step 3: Excitation and radiative transfer modelling of radial brightness
  - method: Multi-shell LVG model with Gaussian-in-log-radius abundance profile, fit to observed J=14-13 radial brightness [2 Excitation and radiative transfer mod…: "we have carried out excitation and radiative transfer calculations based on a multi-shell LVG formalism."]
  - parameter: Levels up to J=30 in ground and nu6=1 states; A:E ratio 1:1 [2 Excitation and radiative transfer mod…: "We considered rotational levels within the ground and $\nu_6=1$ vibrational states up to $J=30$."]
  - assumption: Distance 130 pc; mass loss 2e-5 Msun/yr; collisional rates scale as sqrt(T) above 140 K [2 Excitation and radiative transfer mod…: "extrapolated at higher temperatures assuming that the rate coefficients scale with the square root of temperature"]
  - fitting: Best-reproducing profile chosen (method not stated) [2 Excitation and radiative transfer mod…: "The radial abundance profile which best reproduces the $J=14-13$ transition observed with ALMA"]
  - validation: Compared azimuthally averaged radial brightness for each K component [2 Excitation and radiative transfer mod…: "The radial brightness distributions calculated for the various $K$ components of the $J=14-13$ transition are confronted with the observed ones"]
- result: Peak abundance profile radius r0 = 1.2e16 ± not stated cm [2 Excitation and radiative transfer mod…: "$r_0=1.2\times10^{16}$ cm, $f(r_0)=5\times10^{-8}$"]
- result: Peak fractional abundance f(r0) relative to H2 = 5e-8 ± not stated dimensionless [2 Excitation and radiative transfer mod…: "reaches a maximum value of $5\times10^{-8}$ at 6$''$"]
- result: Delta log r = 0.45 (r<r0), 0.3 (r>r0) ± not stated dimensionless [2 Excitation and radiative transfer mod…: "$\Delta \log r=0.45$ for $r<r_0$ and 0.3 for $r>r_0$"]
- result: Maximum CH3CN volume density = ~0.02 at 2" ± not stated molecules cm-3 [abstract: "reaching a maximum abundance of $\sim0.02$ molecules cm$^{-3}$ at 2$''$ from the star"]
- limitation (authors): Smooth spherical shell model does not reproduce fine structure; infrared pumping only partly included [2 Excitation and radiative transfer mod…: "Our model of a smooth hollow spherical shell does not aim at reproducing these fine details"]
- not stated (card): Channel ranges used for continuum subtraction; Clean mask/iteration count, weighting, cell and image size; How 30m and ALMA data were merged in GILDAS; CASA version; Description of the LVG code and fitting procedure for the abundance profile (no goodness-of-fit); Phase center coordinates

### 2017A&A...604A...2S: PdBI U/LIRG Survey (PULS): Dense molecular gas in Arp 220 and NGC 6240 (2017; nearby_galaxies, astrochemistry; multi_facility_alma_minor)
- question: What are the molecular gas physical conditions and CO isotopologue abundance ratios in Arp 220 and NGC 6240, and how do dense-gas line ratios differ?
- step 3: Radiative transfer modelling of 12CO, 13CO, C18O
  - method: RADEX models with MultiNest nested sampling likelihood, filling factor, three priors [4 Radiative Transfer Analysis: "This code implements the nested sampling algorithm MultiNest (cite: Feroz2009) using its Python wrapper PyMultiNest (cite: Buchner2014) to constrain parameters."]
  - software: RADEX, MultiNest, PyMultiNest
  - parameter: Free: Tkin, N/dV, nH2, filling factor ≤1; optical depth prior 0-100 [4 Radiative Transfer Analysis: "An optical depth range of 0 to 100."]
  - assumption: [12CO]/[H2] = 3e-4; 13CO/C18O = 1; diameter ~700 pc [Table 4 notes: "Molecular Gas (H$_{2}$) mass assuming \[$^{12}$CO\]/\[H$_{2}$\] = 3 $\times$ 10$^{-4}$"]
  - fitting: Nested sampling (MultiNest) with column length, dynamical mass and optical depth priors [4 Radiative Transfer Analysis: "We also implement three priors:"]
  - validation: Arp 220W fit excluded 3-2 line because it gave poor SLED fits [4 Radiative Transfer Analysis: "the addition of the $J$=3-2 line resulted in poor SLED fits"]
- result: Best-fit Tkin Arp 220C = 38 ± -1σ 37, +1σ 690 K [Table 4: "| | Best Fit | 38 | 3.35 | 4.9 | -0.036 | 19.20 | 8.69 | 0.3 | 90 | 91 |"]
- result: Best-fit log10 nH2 Arp 220C = 3.35 ± -1σ 2.55, +1σ 3.40 log cm^-3 [Table 4: "| | Best Fit | 38 | 3.35 | 4.9 | -0.036 | 19.20 | 8.69 | 0.3 | 90 | 91 |"]
- result: Best-fit [12CO]/[13CO] Arp 220C = 90 ± -1σ 58, +1σ 309 dimensionless [Table 4: "| | Best Fit | 38 | 3.35 | 4.9 | -0.036 | 19.20 | 8.69 | 0.3 | 90 | 91 |"]
- limitation (authors): Resolution too poor to separate the two nuclei [Table 3 notes: "Our resolution is too poor to distinguish the nuclei spatially"]
- not stated (card): CASA imaging parameters for ALMA SV cubes (cell, image size, continuum subtraction, line-free channels); MCMC prior bounds on Tkin, nH2, N/dV; number of live points; Which delivered ALMA calibration products/scripts version; Data release location of PdBI products not given as a specific link

### 2025A&A...700A.141H: Statistical nuclear spin ratios of deuterated ammonia in the pre-stellar core L1544 (2025; astrochemistry, star_formation, milky_way_ism; multi_facility_alma_minor)
- question: Determine the ortho/para (o/p) nuclear spin ratios of NH2D and NHD2 in the pre-stellar core L1544 and test whether they match statistical weights (proton hop, PH) or full scrambling (FS) predictions
- step 3: Fit constant fractional abundances with radiative transfer
  - method: Simulate spectra with LOC on the spheroid assuming constant fractional abundances and fit to observed spectra [Core model: "Line simulations were performed using the Line radiative transfer with the OpenCL (LOC) program (cite: 2020A&A...644A.151J)."] [Core model: "The fractional abundances were determined by comparing the simulated spectra with the observed ones"]
  - software: LOC
  - parameter: Positions (0,0) and (-23'',35'') of the APEX LAsMA footprint [Core model: "obtained in the centre of the map (0,0) and the second strongest position $(-23\ensuremath{''},35\ensuremath{''})$"]
  - assumption: LTE between hyperfine components; line overlap not included [Core model: "The hyperfine structure of the lines was modelled by assuming local thermodynamic equilibrium between the components"]
  - fitting: Minimum chi-square estimation (Harju et al. 2024, Sect. 3.2) [Core model: "the method that applies minimum chi-square estimation is described in Sect. 3.2 of (cite: 2024A&A...682A...8H)"]
  - validation: Only statistical uncertainties from spectral noise are included [Core model: "Only the statistical uncertainties based on the noise of the observed spectra are taken into account in this method."]
- step 6: Fit ortho/para abundances with an ad hoc density-dependent abundance profile using APEX, IRAM and ALMA
  - method: Fractional abundances constant in n_H2 = 5e4-5e5 cm^-3 and decreasing at lower and higher density, abundances of each spin species varied independently [Simulations with an ad hoc abundance pr…: "We ran another series of simulations assuming that the fractional abundances of ortho- and para-$\rm NH_2D$ and $\rm NHD_2$ are constant in the density range $n_{\rm H_2}=5\times10^4-5\times10^5\,\rm cm^{-3}$"] [Simulations with an ad hoc abundance pr…: "The fractional abundances of o$\rm NH_2D$, p$\rm NH_2D$, o$\rm NHD_2$, and p$\rm NHD_2$ (in the flat region) were independently varied"]
  - software: LOC
  - parameter: Abundances proportional to n^-2 in the inner parts of the core (steeper than n^-1 needed for ALMA); n in the outer part [Simulations with an ad hoc abundance pr…: "We obtained rather a good agreement with all the observed spectra by assuming that the fractional $\rm NH_2D$ and $\rm NHD_2$ abundances are proportional to $n^{-2}$ in the inner parts of the core."] [Simulations with an ad hoc abundance pr…: "We first assumed that fractional abundances are proportional to $n^{-1}$ and $n$ in these two regions."]
  - assumption: Spheroidal core model from Core model section used for the radiative transfer [Simulations with an ad hoc abundance pr…: "the simulated spectra of the spheroidal core model were compared with observations"]
  - fitting: chi-square statistic as in Harju et al. 2024 [Simulations with an ad hoc abundance pr…: "The best-fit abundances and their uncertainties were estimated by evaluating the $\chi^2$ statistic as explained in (cite: 2024A&A...682A...8H)."]
  - validation: Simulated spectra compared with observed spectra at seven LAsMA positions and along two ALMA strips [Simulations with an ad hoc abundance pr…: "The simulated spectra from the best-fit model towards the seven positions observed with APEX/LAsMA are shown in Figs."]
- result: o/p-NH2D (broken power law / ad hoc profile) = 2.85 ± 0.05 (1 sigma) dimensionless [abstract: "o/p-$\rm NH_2D=2.85\pm0.05$, o/p-$\rm NHD_2=2.10\pm0.06$ ($1\,\sigma$)"]
- result: o/p-NHD2 (broken power law / ad hoc profile) = 2.10 ± 0.06 (1 sigma) dimensionless [abstract: "o/p-$\rm NH_2D=2.85\pm0.05$, o/p-$\rm NHD_2=2.10\pm0.06$ ($1\,\sigma$)"]
- result: o/p-NH2D at (0,0), constant abundance model = 3.13 ± 0.07 dimensionless [Table 2: "$3.13 (0.07)$"]
- result: o/p-NHD2 at (0,0), constant abundance model = 2.15 ± 0.06 dimensionless [Table 2: "$2.15 (0.06)$"]
- result: Maximum fractional abundance X(NH2D), ad hoc profile = 12.5e-9 ± 0.1e-9 dimensionless [Table 3: "$12.5\pm0.1$"]
- result: Maximum fractional abundance X(NHD2), ad hoc profile = 3.30e-9 ± 0.03e-9 dimensionless [Table 3: "$3.30\pm0.03$"]
- result: NHD2/NH2D abundance ratio (printed in the table as NH2D/NH2D), ad hoc profile = 0.264 ± 0.003 dimensionless [Table 3: "$0.264\pm0.003$"]
- limitation (authors): Rest frequencies of the NHD2 lines are probably less accurate than those of the NH2D lines [Observed spectra and maps: "This suggests that the rest frequencies of the $\rm NHD_2$ lines are not as accurate as those of the $\rm NH_2D$ lines."]
- limitation (authors): Static core model neglects core contraction during chemical evolution [Simulated spectra: "We think the difference is attributable to the static core model, which neglects the contraction of the core at the same time as the chemistry is evolving."]
- limitation (authors): Fit uncertainties include only statistical noise [Core model: "Only the statistical uncertainties based on the noise of the observed spectra are taken into account in this method."]
- not stated (card): Calibration details of the 2016.1.00240.S ALMA data (CASA version, calibrators, self-calibration, line-free channels) are only in Caselli et al. 2022; ALMA spectral setup, band and baseline range are not given in this paper; pyRate source code, network files and exact output grids are not stated as available; Beam efficiencies for APEX and IRAM are only 'provided by the telescope teams', values n…

### 2022FrASS...9.1118D: A Genetic Algorithm to Model Solar Radio Active Regions From 3D Magnetic Field Extrapolations (2022; sun; archival_reuse)
- question: Determine the plasma temperature and electron density height profiles in the solar atmosphere of active region NOAA 12470 to reproduce observed radio brightness temperature maps at 17, 100, and 230 GHz.
- step 4: Fit 1D atmospheric temperature and density profiles per pixel via genetic algorithm and radiative transfer
  - method: Run Pikaia genetic algorithm independently for each vertical column (pixel) to modify the seed SSC quiet Sun model via 5 free parameters, compute thermal bremsstrahlung and gyroresonance absorption coefficients and 1D radiative transfer to… [Atmospheric modelling via genetic algor…: "An initial 1D atmospheric model is used as a seed for the GA: at each step, $T_\mathrm{b}$ is obtained via the calculation of radiative transfer incorporating opacity from both the thermal bremsstrahlung and gyroresonance emission mechanisms."] [Atmospheric modelling via genetic algor…: "The GA iteratively updates the atmospheric plasma trapped by magnetic field lines until a best model is found via $\chi^2$ minimization of the calculated and observed $T_\mathrm{b}$ values."]
  - software: Pikaia
  - parameter: 5 free parameters: chromospheric temperature gradient, chromospheric electron density gradient, coronal temperature factor N_T, coronal electron density factor N_n_e, and TR position shift Delta h [Genetic Algorithm Features: "Five free parameters are supplied to the GA representing the atmospheric model."]
  - parameter: GA settings: full-generation replacement, 10 generations, 100 children per generation, crossover probability = 0.85, variable mutation mode, initial mutation rate = 0.005, mutation rate range = 0.0005 to 0.25 [Genetic Algorithm Features: "The method used here was full-generation replacement using ten generations with one hundred children each. The crossover probability was 0.85 and the mutation mode was variable. The initial mutation rate, that is, the initial probability that any gene locus will mutate in any generation, was 0.005. Finally, the mutati…"]
  - parameter: Gyroresonance harmonics s <= 5 with 10% uncertainty in |B| (3rd harmonic at 2000±200 G, 4th at 1500±150 G, 5th at 1200±120 G) [Emission mechanisms: "Since the contribution of higher harmonics to the radio emission is very small (cite: Shibasaki1994), the gyroresonance emission was only calculated for harmonics smaller than 5 ($s\leq 5$)."] [Gyroresonance contribution: "To investigate the gyroresonance contribution, $\kappa_g$ was calculated for harmonics $s\leq 5$, allowing for an uncertainty of $10\%$ in the $|\vec{B}|$ values."]
  - assumption: Effective ion charge Z = 1.178, only electron-proton collisions considered, densities grouped as (n_e * n_i)^(1/2), plasma fully ionized above 1000 km in SSC model [Emission mechanisms: "Only the contributions of collisions with protons was considered, since its density is much larger than that of other ion species ($n_i$)."] [Emission mechanisms: "Following (cite: Zirin1988), it was assumed $Z=1.178$, if a fully hydrogen atmosphere was considered, i.e. $Z=1$, the resulting $T_\mathrm{b}$ should be only $\sim 3\%$ smaller for all frequencies simulated here."]
  - fitting: Pikaia genetic algorithm with chi-squared minimization; full-generation replacement over 10 generations with 100 children each; stabilizes by ~7th generation. [Genetic Algorithm Features: "A generation of child atmospheric models is created by the algorithm based on the parent models with the lowest $\chi^2$ values, using the genetic algorithm described in (cite: Charbonneau1995)."] [Genetic Algorithm Features: "In our runs the $\chi^2$ measure typically stabilizes to a minimum value by about the seventh generation."]
  - validation: Monitored stabilization of chi-squared across generations and evaluated optical depth and contribution functions across atmospheric layers. [Genetic Algorithm Features: "In our runs the $\chi^2$ measure typically stabilizes to a minimum value by about the seventh generation."] [Free-free contribution: "In Figure [5](#fig:tau_cf) we show, for each atmospheric class, the optical depth ($\tau$, top row) and the contribution function (CF, bottom row), that represents the emission variation with the atmosphere height."]
- result: Transition region (TR) height for umbrae = 1080 ± 20 km [Abstract: "for umbrae the TR is located at $1080\pm20$ km above the solar surface;"]
- result: Transition region (TR) height for penumbrae = 1800 ± 50 km [Abstract: "for penumbrae, the TR is located at $1800\pm50$ km;"]
- result: Transition region (TR) height for plages (bright regions outside sunspots) = 2000 ± 100 km [Abstract: "and for bright regions outside sunspots, the TR is located at $2000\pm100$ km."]
- limitation (authors): The genetic algorithm is purely a mathematical solution and does not verify whether the atmospheric plasma is in local thermodynamic equilibrium (LTE). [Genetic Algorithm Features: "Nevertheless, the GA used here is a mathematical solution and it does not verify if the atmospheric plasma is in local thermodynamic equilibrium (LTE) or not."]
- limitation (authors): Low spatial resolution of ALMA single-dish observations (25" at 230 GHz, 58" at 100 GHz) smooths the dark umbra (25" diameter) with surrounding bright areas, masking the umbra at 100 GHz and reducing its contrast at 230 GHz. [Comparison of the synthetic and observe…: "The resulting low contrast of the dark umbra with respect to the quiet Sun in the model is due to the low resolution of the ALMA single-dish observations. Since the umbral size is almost equal to the ALMA 230 GHz beam, the umbral $T_\mathrm{b}$ is smoothed with the bright surrounding areas."]
- limitation (authors): The modeled atmospheric profiles that fit 230 and 100 GHz well cannot reach the high brightness temperatures observed at 17 GHz in non-polarized regions (reaching at most 1.2 T_qS vs. 2.8 T_qS observed). [Comparison of the synthetic and observe…: "However, the modeled atmospheric profiles that achieved good agreement with the $T_\mathrm{b}$ observations at 230 and 100 GHz were not able to reach the high $T_\mathrm{b}$ values observed at 17 GHz. While these regions showed observational values as high as $2.8\times T_{qS}$, the model was only able to reach a maxi…"]
- not stated (card): Prior bounds (minimum and maximum values) chosen for each of the five GA free parameters (chromospheric temperature gradient, chromospheric electron density gradient, coronal temperature factor, coronal electron density factor, and TR position shift); Exact functional mathematical equations for how the five GA free parameters modify the SSC temperature and density height profiles; Details of the …

### 2024A&A...691A..70N: Disruption of a massive molecular cloud by a supernova in the Galactic Centre: Initial results from the ACES project (2024; milky_way_ism; large_program_or_survey)
- question: What is the origin of the massive ring-like molecular structure M0.8-0.2 in the Galactic Centre (young-star feedback, supernova feedback, cloud-cloud collision, shear, or line-of-sight coincidence)?
- step 3: Estimate gas density with non-LTE radiative transfer
  - method: Grid of Radex models (SpectralRadex) in T_kin, n_H2, line width, X_HNCO; frequency of models reproducing observed brightness temperature 1-5 K; depth L = N_H2/n_H2 bounded [3.3 Mass, energy, and momentum: "depict the frequency with which the models produce brightness temperatures within the observed range of 1 to 5 K for the M0.8$-$0.2 ring"] [3.3 Mass, energy, and momentum: "Bounds on the depth are imposed, assuming that the structure should not be much deeper than its projected size ($\sim 10$ pc) and should not be smaller than the angular resolution of our observations ($\sim 0.1$ pc)."]
  - software: Radex, SpectralRadex
  - parameter: n_H2 grid 10^2-10^8 cm^-3, step 0.05 dex; X_HNCO 10^-10-10^-6, step 0.05 dex [3.3 Mass, energy, and momentum: "The number density of molecular hydrogen ($n_\mathrm{H_2}$) spanned $10^2$ to $10^8$ cm$^{-3}$ with a step size of 0.05 dex."]
  - assumption: Fixed N_H2 = 8.5e22 cm^-2 (Herschel average over the ring) [3.3 Mass, energy, and momentum: "We assumed a fixed molecular hydrogen column density ($N_\mathrm{H_2}$) of $8.5 \times 10^{22}$ cm$^{-2}$"]
  - fitting: Grid search with likelihood contours (90 per cent contour quoted); peak likelihood n_H2 ~ 10^4.2 cm^-3 [3.3 Mass, energy, and momentum: "The analysis yields a peak likelihood of $n_{\mathrm{H2}} \approx 10^{4.2}$ cm$^{-3}$,"]
  - validation: Cross-check against shell-width estimate (n ~ 10^4.1 cm^-3) and Martin et al. 2008 abundance [3.3 Mass, energy, and momentum: "A simple check of this value can be done by comparing our HNCO-derived density with what we get if we divide the mean H$_2$ column density by twice the shell width"]
- result: Peak-likelihood H2 number density = 10^4.2 (range 10^3.5 to 10^5.5) ± not stated cm^-3 [3.3 Mass, energy, and momentum: "The analysis yields a peak likelihood of $n_{\mathrm{H2}} \approx 10^{4.2}$ cm$^{-3}$,"]
- limitation (authors): Expansion velocity is a lower limit because of projection effects [3.2 Kinematics and expansion: "We acknowledge that this estimate is a lower limit due to projection effects"]
- limitation (authors): Three-dimensional geometry unknown; effects of clustered SNe not well understood; comprehensive kinematic analysis left to future work [3.3 Mass, energy, and momentum: "In the absence of prior knowledge regarding the three-dimensional structure of the molecular cloud"]
- not stated (card): Exact ACES pipeline modifications (size mitigation, divergent-clean fixes), weighting/robust, cell size, image size, deconvolver and masks for the cubes; Continuum subtraction method and line-free channels for the line cubes; Feathering parameters (uv cutoffs, scaling factor) for ACA/TP/12 m and for the GBT combination; How the four mosaics were stitched and the weighting scheme used; Exact area/…

### 2015ApJ...806...17R: Morphology and Kinematics of Warm Molecular Gas in the Nuclear Region of Arp 220 as Revealed by ALMA (2015; nearby_galaxies, milky_way_ism; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the morphology and kinematics of the warm (CO J=6-5) molecular gas in the two nuclei of Arp 220, and what energy source heats it?
- step 3: Model eastern nuclear disk line profiles
  - method: 2D edge-on axisymmetric rotating disk radiative transfer with Gaussian line/dust source functions and optical depths, beam-convolved; variants with foreground absorber or outflow [4 Modeling: "For simplicity, we assume that the disk is perfectly edge-on."]
  - software: RADEX
  - parameter: tau_0,l=4, tau_0,d=1, sigma=85 km/s, V_c=135 km/s with inner bump peaking ~240 km/s [4.1 Model Results: "center-to-edge line center optical depth $\tau_{0,l}=4$, dust optical depth $\tau_{0,d}=1$, velocity dispersion $\sigma = 85$ km s$^{-1}$"]
  - assumption: Disk uniform vertically; inclination ~50 deg ignored [4 Modeling: "We also assume that the disk is uniform in the vertical direction"]
  - fitting: Manual comparison of model and observed profiles; no formal fit [4.1 Model Results: "A reasonable match to the observed profiles for the eastern nucleus is provided by a model"]
  - validation: Compared with IWMV curve from moment-1; absorption model preferred over outflow [4.4: "Hence the observed IWMV curve is completely consistent with an underlying axisymmetric disk model"]
- result: Absorber mass = few x (1e7-1e8) ± not stated Msun [4.4: "M_{abs}\sim {\rm \ few}\times \left(10^7 - 10^8\right) M_\odot."]
- limitation (authors): Warm-gas mass per nucleus uses global Herschel ratio [footnote 5: "our mass estimate for the warm molecular gas for the individual nuclei should be used with caution"]
- not stated (card): Clean thresholds, iteration counts and clean box definitions; Cell size and image size; Line-free channels used for continuum subtraction; Self-calibration solution intervals; Robust/weighting details beyond natural vs uniform; Optimization method for choosing kinematic model parameters (apparently by eye); Rotation curve tabulation for model; Moment-map clipping thresholds; Data cube product not…

### 2019MNRAS.485.2457H: `The Brick' is not a brick: a comprehensive study of the structure and dynamics of the central molecular zone cloud G0.253+0.016 (2019; milky_way_ism, star_formation; archival_reuse)
- question: What are the internal structure and dynamics of the CMZ cloud G0.253+0.016, and is it a single coherent centrally-condensed cloud or a hierarchically structured complex of line-of-sight components?
- step 6: Test optical thickness of HNCO
  - method: Radiative transfer with LVG (RADEX) and 3-D spherical model with LTE/NLTE in TORUS over density-abundance grid, compared with observed brightness temperatures [5.1.2: "We perform radiative transfer calculations using both the large velocity gradient (LVG) approximation and a 3-D model evaluated on a 1-D grid."]
  - software: TORUS, RADEX
  - parameter: T_kin 60 K, line width 4.4 km/s, sphere radius 2.35 pc, abundance 1e-9, density 1e4 cm^-3 [5.1.2: "We adopt a kinetic temperature of 60 K, (cite: ginsburg_2016) and a fixed turbulent line width of 4.4 km s$^{-1}$"]
  - assumption: spherical symmetry; uniform abundance, density and temperature [5.1.2: "our calculations assume spherical symmetry, as well as a uniform abundance, density, and temperature."]
  - validation: compared with observed T_B distribution (~1.75 K mean) [5.1.2: "three standard deviations from the mean scousepy-derived brightness temperature $\sim1.75$ K"]
- limitation (authors): Coarse spectral resolution (3.4 km/s) may overestimate dispersions and Mach number; Mach number is an upper bound [3.3: "This should be taken as an upper bound on the level of turbulent motion"]
- limitation (authors): Cloud line-of-sight extent unknown; PPV not equivalent to physical space [3.3: "we do not know the extent of the cloud along the line-of-sight."]
- not stated (card): Calibration details, CASA version and imaging parameters of the Rathborne et al. cube (robust, cleaning, combination method with single-dish); Exact velocity window and handling of the combined ALMA+MALT90 HNCO cube used for fitting; Manual SAA spectral fits are user-interactive; initial guesses and SAA sizes per bin (log bin edges for delta v_m) not given; acorns parameters min_height and stop v…

### 2020PASJ...72...33K: Deeply cooled core of the Phoenix galaxy cluster imaged by ALMA with the Sunyaev-Zel'dovich effect (2020; galaxy_clusters_and_sz; observational_alma_primary)
- question: How much thermal gas is cooling to low temperatures in the Phoenix cluster core, probed with an ALMA SZE image combined with Chandra X-ray data independent of the X-ray spectrum
- step 4: Simulate ALMA observations to calibrate missing flux
  - method: Generate mock visibilities with simobserve from an X-ray pseudo-pressure model at four spectral windows, add noise, deconvolve as for real data, and fit I_out = c1 I_in + c0 [4.2 Imaging simulations: "Visibility data were then produced using the CASA task *simobserve* including both instrumental and atmospheric thermal noise in each spectral window."] [4.2 Imaging simulations: "The mock visibility was deconvolved in the same way as the real data"]
  - software: CASA simobserve, CASA tclean
  - parameter: Four spectral windows at 85, 87, 97, 99 GHz with 1.875 GHz bandwidth each [4.2 Imaging simulations: "We created model images separately at four spectral windows centered at 85, 87, 97, and 99 GHz with an effective bandwidth of 1.875 GHz each."]
  - parameter: Pointings, array configuration, hour angle, integration time and PWV matched to each execution block [4.2 Imaging simulations: "The pointing directions, the array configuration, the hour angle, the total effective integration time, and the average precipitable water vapor were set to match those of each executing block of real observations."]
  - parameter: 10 noise realizations for each ypeak (4e-4, 8e-4, 12e-4) [4.2 Imaging simulations: "For each value of $y_{\rm peak}$, we repeated the above procedure 10 times adopting different noise realizations."]
  - assumption: Pseudo pressure map normalised to ypeak = 8e-4, described as typical of massive cool core clusters; relativistic correction applied [4.2 Imaging simulations: "which is a typical value for massive cool core clusters"] [4.2 Imaging simulations: "A relativistic correction to the SZE intensity by (cite: Itoh04) was applied"]
  - fitting: Linear fit to the 10 realizations per ypeak with c1 common among ypeak values [4.2 Imaging simulations: "where $c_1$ is assumed to be common among different values of $y_{\rm peak}$"]
  - validation: Simulated images reproduced by the linear relation with c1 close to unity; result consistent with the real image representing differential true intensity [4.2 Imaging simulations: "confirms that the observed ALMA SZE image gives a reasonable representation of differential values of the true intensity (cite: Kitayama16)"]
- result: Missing-flux slope c1 = 0.94 ± 0.02 dimensionless [4.2 Imaging simulations: "This yielded $c_1=0.94 \pm 0.02$ and $c_0= (1.19 \pm 0.16, ~2.07 \pm 0.20, ~3.02 \pm 0.28)$ $\mu$Jy/arcsec$^2$"]
- result: Offset c0 for ypeak = 4e-4, 8e-4, 12e-4 = 1.19, 2.07, 3.02 ± 0.16, 0.20, 0.28 uJy/arcsec^2 [4.2 Imaging simulations: "This yielded $c_1=0.94 \pm 0.02$ and $c_0= (1.19 \pm 0.16, ~2.07 \pm 0.20, ~3.02 \pm 0.28)$ $\mu$Jy/arcsec$^2$"]
- result: 1 sigma systematic uncertainty in true intensity from missing-flux offset = 0.24 ± not stated uJy/arcsec^2 [4.2 Imaging simulations: "We regard $\sqrt{2}$ times this value (i.e., $\Delta I_\nu^{\rm in}=0.24 \mu$Jy/arcsec$^2$)"]
- limitation (authors): Equation-of-state limit is weak [4.1 Comparison to X-ray data: "While the limit is still weak, the observed perturbations in the Phoenix cluster core is consistent with being isobaric"]
- limitation (authors): Absolute values of the X-ray pseudo pressure are arbitrary [4.1 Comparison to X-ray data: "Note that absolute values of the pseudo pressure are arbitrary"]
- limitation (authors): Temperature at r > 250 kpc cannot be constrained by the ALMA data [4.4 Deprojected electron temperature an…: "As the temperature at $r > 250$ kpc ($\theta > 37''$) cannot be constrained by the ALMA data"]
- not stated (card): Phase centre (target field centre) coordinates are blank in the text; tclean image size, cell size, niter / number of minor cycles, gridder and phasecenter for the 7-pointing mosaic; Calibration CASA/pipeline version and whether QA2 products were used unmodified or re-calibrated; any self-calibration; Starting parameters, weighting and error treatment used in uvmodelfit; source positions are blan…

### 2019ApJ...875L...5E: First M87 Event Horizon Telescope Results. V. Physical Origin of the Asymmetric Ring (2019; agn_and_quasars, nearby_galaxies; theory_or_simulation_compared_to_alma)
- question: Is the EHT2017 1.3 mm asymmetric ring in M87 consistent with a Kerr black hole with a GRMHD accretion flow, and what do the data imply for black hole spin, magnetic flux and jet power?
- step 2: Produce synthetic image library
  - method: GRRT with Rhigh electron temperature prescription, mass scaled so flux is ~0.5 Jy [Section 3: "were produced using the general relativistic ray-tracing (GRRT)"]
  - software: ipole, RAPTOR, BHOSS
  - parameter: Rhigh = 1, 10, 20, 40, 80, 160; i = 12, 17, 22, 158, 163, 168 deg; 160x160 microarcsec FOV, 1 microarcsec pixels [3.2. Image Library Generation: "The model images are generated with a 160 ´ 160 _m_ as field of view and 1 _m_ as pixels"]
  - assumption: M = 6.2e9 Msun, D = 16.9 Mpc, M/D = 3.62 microarcsec [3.2. Image Library Generation: "The ratio _GM_ ( _c_ 2 _D_ ) = 3.62 _m_ as"]
  - validation: Tests with M=3.5e9 Msun, different resolution and flux show insensitivity [3.3. Image Library Summary: "The comparisons with the data are also insensitive to image resolution."]
- step 5: Apply radiative, X-ray and jet power constraints
  - method: Compute efficiency and L_X with Monte Carlo radiative transfer; measure Pjet in simulations; reject models failing thresholds [Section 6: "with the Monte Carlo code grmonty"]
  - software: grmonty
  - parameter: Pjet,min = 1e42 erg/s; L_X obs = 4.4e40 erg/s; (beta gamma)cut = 1 [6.3. Jet Power: "adopt a very conservative lower limit on jet power º _P_ jet,min = 10 42 erg s - 1 ."]
  - validation: Sensitivity to the jet definition tested using Pout and varied cutoff [6.3. Jet Power: "This conclusion is not sensitive to the definition of P jet"]
- result: Spindown time, a*=0.94 MAD Rhigh=160 = 7.3e12 ± not stated yr [6.3. Jet Power: "t = 7.3 ´ 10 12 yr, which is long compared to a Hubble time"]
- limitation (authors): Thermal single-parameter electron model, untilted disks, nonradiative GRMHD, fast-light approximation [7. Discussion: "We have interpreted the EHT2017 data using a limited library of models with attendant limitations."]
- not stated (card): Public EHT2017 visibility data and closure-quantity data products (not given here); THEMIS and GENA fitting settings, priors and chain configuration (deferred to Paper VI and in-prep papers); GRMHD and GRRT code versions and run parameters; Exact snapshot selection and ISCO/time sampling per model

### 2015ApJ...812...43B: HerMES: ALMA Imaging of Herschel-selected Dusty Star-forming Galaxies (2015; high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: How does gravitational lensing and multiplicity affect the properties of the brightest Herschel-selected DSFGs, and what do the lens models imply for the bright end of the intrinsic DSFG number counts?
- step 3: Visibility-plane lens modeling
  - method: uvmcmcfit: elliptical Gaussian sources, SIE lenses, ray tracing, compare model visibilities to data [3.1 Model Fitting Methodology: "Sources are assumed to be elliptical Gaussians that are parameterized by the following six free parameters"]
  - software: uvmcmcfit, emcee
  - parameter: 512 walkers; burn-in 500-1000 iterations; final 10 iterations [3.1 Model Fitting Methodology: "We employ a “burn-in” phase with 512 walkers and 500-1000 iterations"]
  - parameter: uniform priors; theta_E 0.1-6 arcsec; q_lens>0.3; q_s>0.2; r_s 0.01-1.5 arcsec [3.1 Model Fitting Methodology: "The axial ratios of the lenses and sources are restricted to be $q_{\rm lens} > 0.3$ and $q_{\rm s} > 0.2$."]
  - assumption: Additional lenses at same redshift as primary; maximal-lensing Einstein radii fixed for weakly lensed systems [3.1 Model Fitting Methodology: "We assume secondary, tertiary, etc., lenses are located at the same redshift as the primary lens."]
  - fitting: emcee affine-invariant ensemble MCMC with Gaussian likelihood in visibility plane [3.1 Model Fitting Methodology: "We use emcee (cite: Foreman-Mackey:2013yq) to sample the posterior probability density function (PDF) of our model parameters."]
  - validation: Bolton ray tracing tested against Gravlens; residual maps inspected [3.1 Model Fitting Methodology: "tests of the Bolton ray-tracing routine indicate it produces results consistent with Gravlens"]
- result: Brightest intrinsic source S870 (HADFS11.0) = 17.5 ± 0.4 mJy [4.1 De-lensing: "with an intrinsic flux density of $S_{870} = 17.5 \pm 0.4\,$mJy"]
- limitation (authors): Resolution may cause over-predicted sizes and under-predicted magnifications; optically faint lenses may be missed [3.1 Model Fitting Methodology: "in some cases the lens models may moderately over-predict the intrinsic sizes of the lensed galaxies and hence under-predict the magnification factors"]
- not stated (card): Line-free channels/continuum definition and clean thresholds, cell and image size; Which targets received selfcal and solint; Exact MCMC convergence criteria; final phase of only 10 iterations; Lens position/Einstein radius fixings are given per object but the SMA data and fits for the combined sample are not tabulated here; Details of logistic regression and running-average smoothing parameters;…

### 2016PASJ...68...88K: The Sunyaev-Zel'dovich effect at 5″: RX J1347.5-1145 imaged by ALMA (2016; galaxy_clusters_and_sz; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Can ALMA (7-m plus 12-m arrays, Band 3) image the thermal SZE of RX J1347.5-1145 at 5 arcsec resolution, and how should missing flux be corrected and the map compared with X-ray and earlier SZE data?
- step 5: Imaging simulations for missing flux
  - method: Simulate ALMA observations of pressure-map model with simobserve; derive Fourier-space correction (i) and real-space linear relation (ii) [4.2: "we performed imaging simulations using the latter as a realistic input model to quantify the degree of missing flux"]
  - software: CASA simobserve
  - parameter: y_peak=8e-4; four spectral windows 1.875 GHz each [4.2: "We created model images separately at four spectral windows centered at 85, 87, 97, and 99 GHz with an effective bandwidth of 1.875 GHz each."]
  - assumption: Relativistic SZE correction of Nozawa05 with projected temperature [4.2: "A relativistic correction to the SZE intensity by (cite: Nozawa05) was applied"]
  - fitting: Linear fit I_out = c1 I_in + c0 to unbinned pixels [4.2: "with $c_1=0.88$ and $c_0=3.6 ~\mu$Jy/arcsec$^2$."]
  - validation: Repeated with y_peak doubled/halved and different noise; relations converge [4.2: "we repeated the simulations by doubling or halving the signal of the entire input map"]
- result: Fit slope c1 = 0.88 ± not stated dimensionless [4.2: "with $c_1=0.88$ and $c_0=3.6 ~\mu$Jy/arcsec$^2$."]
- limitation (authors): Missing flux on large scales due to no Total Power Array [Conclusions: "The accuracy of the map is limited primarily by missing flux arising from the lack of short-spacing data in the current configuration of ALMA."]
- not stated (card): Phase center coordinates (blank in text); Exact CASA task parameters (niter, mask, spw selection) for tclean/clean; Simulation details: simobserve PWV and noise settings, exact large-scale fall-off factor shape; Contour-binning and XSPEC fit details for pseudo pressure map; Whether Band 3 7-m QA2 weights were rescaled; Number of pixels/image size

### 2015ApJ...800L..14C: Ethyl Cyanide On Titan: Spectroscopic Detection and Mapping Using Alma (2015; solar_system, astrochemistry; archival_reuse)
- question: Is gas-phase ethyl cyanide (C2H5CN) present in Titan's atmosphere, and what are its vertical and spatial distributions compared with HC3N, CH3CN and CH3CCH?
- step 4: retrieve C2H5CN vertical profile
  - method: Line-by-line radiative transfer least-squares fits of step and gradient abundance profiles to 223.9-224.2 GHz lines [Results: "least-squares fits to the observed spectra were performed using C$_2$H$_5$CN abundance profiles with constant mixing ratio above a specified cutoff altitude $z_c$ and zero abundance below."]
  - software: NEMESIS
  - parameter: step z_c 100-400 km; gradient model reference altitude 292 km, slope as fraction of scale height [Results: "The free parameters in this model were the abundance at a reference altitude $z_r = 292$ km"]
  - assumption: Profiles do not vary with latitude/longitude; Lorentzian half-width 0.075 cm-1 atm-1 at 296 K, exponent 0.50; model atmosphere to 1000 km [Results: "The Lorentzian broadening half-width at 296 K was assumed to be 0.075 cm$^{-1}$ atm$^{-1}$, with a temperature-dependence exponent of 0.50."]
  - fitting: least-squares, reduced chi^2 [Results: "Initially, least-squares fits to the observed spectra were performed using C$_2$H$_5$CN abundance profiles with constant mixing ratio above a specified cutoff altitude $z_c$ and zero abundance below."]
  - validation: Reduced chi^2 values; fit quality of 300 and 400 km models [Results: "The quality of these fits confirms that our C$_2$H$_5$CN line assignments are secure."]
- result: C2H5CN abundance at 292 km (gradient model) = 1.3 ± 0.4 ppb [Results: "The best-fitting abundance at $z_r$ was found to be $1.3 \pm 0.4$ ppb"]
- result: fractional scale height f_H = 4.6 ± 1.1 dimensionless [Results: "with a retrieved fractional scale height of $f_H = 4.6 \pm 1.1$"]
- result: Vertical column density, gradient model = 3.6e14 ± not stated cm^-2 [Table 2: "Gradient | 1.30 | 292 | 0.97 | $3.6\times10^{14}$"]
- result: Column density range for good-fit models = (1-5)e14 ± not stated cm^-2 [Results: "The models that fit the data well have values in the range $(1-5)\times10^{14}$ cm$^{-2}$."]
- not stated (card): uvcontsub line-free channels and fit order; clean mask and iterations; Titan-frame velocity handling details; Specific aerosol and isotopologue abundance values; Noise estimation for fit uncertainties and the 3 sigma line detection; Exact lines summed for the C2H5CN map; Fitting algorithm details within NEMESIS

### 2013ApJ...772..149C: Constraining a Model of Turbulent Coronal Heating for AU Microscopii with X-Ray, Radio, and Millimeter Observations (2013; stellar_physics, debris_disks; theory_or_simulation_compared_to_alma)
- question: Can a turbulence-driven coronal loop heating model for AU Mic reproduce the X-ray luminosity, the ALMA 1.3 mm central emission peak, and radio upper limits?
- step 2: Synthesize X-ray and radio/mm emission
  - method: L_X from the ROSAT PSPC loss function; free-free radiative transfer for radio/mm; convolve over N(L) [3 Synthesis: "then we convolved them together using $N(L)$ to produce a result that is weighted properly"]
  - parameter: Grid 250 v_perp × 250 ε, 0.2–200 km/s, ε 0–2.5 [Footnote 2: "The $250 \times 250$ grid encompasses the ranges $0.2 \leq v_{\perp} \leq 200$ km s$^{-1}$ and $0 \leq \varepsilon \leq 2.5$."]
  - assumption: Vertical loops, no limb effects, S_ν = π R*² I_ν / D² [3 Synthesis: "Thus, we used $S_{\nu} = \pi R_{\ast}^{2} I_{\nu} / D^{2}$."]
  - fitting: Best model is where L_X and 1.3 mm flux contours intersect; no formal fit [4 Results: "the two empirical constraints intersect one another (roughly perpendicularly) at a single parameter value"]
  - validation: Compared with Chandra temperatures, densities, UV pressure and radio upper limits [4 Results: "Our best-fitting solution sits comfortably in the region that is consistent with all of the observed radio upper limits."]
- result: Best-fit v_perp = 1.5545 ± not stated km/s [4 Results: "This best-fitting model has a basal velocity amplitude $v_{\perp} = 1.5545$ km s$^{-1}$"]
- result: Best-fit loop-length exponent ε = 1.245 ± not stated dimensionless [4 Results: "a loop distribution power-law exponent $\varepsilon = 1.245$"]
- result: Loop T_max range = 3.7 to 7.2 ± not stated MK [4 Results: "peak temperatures between 3.7 MK (for $L_{\rm min}$) and 7.2 MK (for $L_{\rm max}$)"]
- result: Model mass loss rate at v_perp=1.5545 km/s = 9e-12 ± not stated Msun/yr [5 Discussion: "the (cite: CS11) mass loss rate increases to $9 \times 10^{-12} \, M_{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$"]
- limitation (authors): Only time-steady heating modeled; no flares, nonthermal emission, or 3D geometry [5 Discussion: "We limited ourselves to just the time-steady component of coronal heating and not the more intermittent flaring"]
- not stated (card): Exact ALMA data reduction behind the 320 μJy flux (in MacGregor et al. 2013); Code implementing the model; Numerical values of the Martens (2010) proportionality constants; Details of the stellar wind model used for mass loss

### 2020ApJ...894...76W: The MESAS Project: ALMA Observations of the F-type Stars γ Lep, γ Vir A, and γ Vir B (2020; stellar_physics, debris_disks; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the submm/mm fluxes and chromospheric structure of debris-poor F-type stars, and is there short-term variability or bias for debris disk studies?
- step 4: Fit semi-empirical chromosphere models
  - method: KINICH-PAKAL iteratively modifies temperature/density profiles, ionization and opacity of a solar base model to fit ALMA and Herschel data [3.3 Stellar atmosphere models: "This code iteratively modifies the radial temperature and hydrogen density profiles, the ionization balance, and the opacity of a base model using the Levenberg-Marquardt algorithm"]
  - software: KINICH-PAKAL
  - assumption: Non-LTE ionization-excitation and radiative transfer; hydrostatic equilibrium Avrett & Loeser solar starting model [3.3 Stellar atmosphere models: "we do not assume that ionization-excitation and radiative transfer are in local thermal equilibrium"]
  - fitting: Levenberg-Marquardt [3.3 Stellar atmosphere models: "using the Levenberg-Marquardt algorithm to adjust the synthetic spectrum"]
  - validation: Synthetic spectrum compared with observed [3.3 Stellar atmosphere models: "leading the synthethic spectrum to be in agreement with the observed one shown in Fig. [3](#tb_plot)"]
- result: gamma Lep temperature minimum = 4800 K at 750 km (Teff/TRmin = 0.76) ± not stated K [4.1 Semi-empirical chromospheric model: "we see a temperature minimum of T$_{R, min}$ = 4800 K (T$_{eff}$/T$_{R, min}$ = 0.76) at a height of 750 km above the photosphere"]
- limitation (authors): gamma Vir models lack constraints at other wavelengths; temperature minimum cannot be analyzed [4.1 Semi-empirical chromospheric model: "Therefore, it is not possible to perform an analysis of the temperature minimum in these two stars."]
- limitation (authors): Variability at longer timescales cannot be ruled out for gamma Lep [4.2 Potential millimeter wavelength var…: "we cannot rule out the possibility of variability at time periods longer than the length of the EBs."]
- not stated (card): CLEAN mask, niter, cell and image size; Whether pipeline products were used or manually re-imaged; spectral line flagging; uvmodelfit starting parameters; KINICH-PAKAL settings, convergence criteria and weighting of data points; Source of stellar parameters (footnotes a-e); How per-scan time series were binned and uncertainties assigned; Gamma Vir EB combination procedure

### 2020ApJS..251...14H: CMZoom. II. Catalog of Compact Submillimeter Dust Continuum Sources in the Milky Way's Central Molecular Zone (2020; milky_way_ism, star_formation; multi_facility_alma_minor)
- question: Which compact sources (<10'', ~0.4 pc) exist in the 1.3 mm SMA CMZoom continuum maps of the Central Molecular Zone, what are their properties and completeness, and what do they imply for CMZ star formation potential?
- step 3: Completeness and false-positive from simulated observations
  - method: Inject 10 point sources, simobserve, tclean, make RMS map, dendrogram and prune, count recovered sources [3.2 Source Recovery and Simulated Obser…: "A given point-source is considered to be recovered if its coordinates are located anywhere within a pruned leaf."]
  - software: CASA simobserve, CASA tclean, AATM
  - parameter: masses from flux assuming Td = 20 K [3.2 Source Recovery and Simulated Obser…: "we assume a constant dust temperature of 20K"]
  - assumption: Point-source-like emission model [3.2 Source Recovery and Simulated Obser…: "can be modeled by point-source-like emission for the purposes of this simplistic completeness estimate."]
  - validation: Completeness resilient to atmospheric variation [3.2 Source Recovery and Simulated Obser…: "The completeness results for our catalogs constructed from the simulated observations is resilient to variations typical in atmospheric conditions"]
- result: 95% completeness mass, robust = 80 ± not stated Msun [3.2 Source Recovery and Simulated Obser…: "point-sources above 80 solar masses, under the typical observational conditions for the *CMZoom* survey, are detected with more than 95% completeness"]
- result: 95% completeness mass, high-completeness = ~50 ± not stated Msun [4.5 High-Completeness Catalog: "with a 95% completeness of $\sim 50$ M$_\odot$ (assuming T$_d$=20K)"]
- limitation (authors): Completeness assumes point sources and does not capture extended emission; masses uncertain by factor ~2 [3.2 Source Recovery and Simulated Obser…: "These mass completeness estimates account only for the observational biases in observing point-source-like emission"]
- not stated (card): Exact dendrogram parameter values in the appendix-referenced setup (min_value, min_npix, min_delta numeric values beyond 3 MJy/sr scaling); Access/processing of CMZoom SMA maps from Dataverse versus Paper I imaging details; Simulated observation antenna configuration, integration time and weather settings; Herschel temperature map not yet published (in prep.); Exact point-source flux values and m…

## All papers in this technique
